# DECISIONS archive

Rulings here are **not in force**. They moved verbatim from DECISIONS.md under their original IDs (docs/DOCUMENTATION.md §7). Never cite one as a reason for current behaviour.

## Note H — industry suffix vocabulary and channel packing (owner, 2026-10-09)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note R-fills-2, 2026-10-11)
- Owner (2026-10-09): **roughness default 0.5 confirmed.** For every packed channel without a source map, the user can set a **global fill value** — a slider in the GUI and a CLI option (e.g. metallic 255 writes 255 instead of the neutral 0); the neutral defaults above apply only when no value is given. Scope: milestone M2.5. (superseded by R-fills-2)

## Note M — channel packing decisions (M2.5 plan, 2026-10-09)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AZ, 2026-10-15)
- **M1 (#18) Env mask:** a packed `cpm`/`vanilla` `_m` R is the metallic source, else the specular source. Under `cpm` with neither it is `packing.fill.metallic`, else `packing.fill.specular`, else 0. The `cpm` `_m` height default is 0 (owner 2026-10-10): alpha 0–4 keeps CS complex material on with parallax off; 0.5 ran parallax on a flat height (Spike S8 DXBC). Widened by AH1 and AH2 (2026-10-14): any complex `_m` without source alpha, or with `alpha.parallax` off, writes 0.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note BV1, 2026-10-17)
- **M9 Pre-packed conflict** is keyed on an image member whose canonical suffix equals the recipe's suffix (`rmaos`, `rfaos`, `p` under `cs`; `m` under `cpm`/`vanilla`). DDS members are never written, so they never conflict. With `packing.prefer = raw` the replaced file is skipped with `PACK-CONFLICT` and counts as an intentional skip (Note I). Exception (never write neither): when the recipe's sources differ in size, the sizes are checked first, the pre-packed file stays a job (no skip, no `PACK-CONFLICT`) and the recipe fails with `PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH`. When `packing.prefer = raw` but the raw sources differ in size, the recipe fails (`PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH`) and the pre-packed file is kept; it converts under the normal never-auto-decide rule (its sub-type is a suggestion, so it needs `--accept-suggestions` or a rule). (amended by BV1, 2026-10-17: a re-encoded DDS member is written, so it conflicts like an image; a skipped DDS still never conflicts)
  - (pointer, Note CO7: a generated member that a real map replaces is skipped `GEN-REPLACED` before M9, so it never reaches M9 and `packing.prefer` never sees it.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **M5 (#22) Fill values:** field `packing.fill.<role>` (one per §4.4 raw-map role: `roughness`, `metallic`, `ao`, `specular`, `glossiness`, `height`, `opacity`, `fuzz`), an 8-bit code 0–255. CLI `--fill <role>=<0..255>` is an unscoped `--set packing.fill.<role>=<v>` added after every `--set`, so it beats an unscoped `--set` of the same field and loses to sub-type- and file-scoped ones. A roughness fill is used for a glossiness channel as `255 − v` and back. A filled channel has provenance `fill:<code written>` and raises **no** `PACK-DEFAULTED` (the user chose it). The GUI slider lands with M3's settings panel.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **M9 Pre-packed conflict** is keyed on an image member whose canonical suffix equals the recipe's suffix (`rmaos`, `rfaos`, `p` under `cs`; `m` under `cpm`/`vanilla`). A re-encoded DDS member is written, so it conflicts like an image; a skipped DDS member never conflicts (Note BV1). With `packing.prefer = raw` the replaced file is skipped with `PACK-CONFLICT` and counts as an intentional skip (Note I). Exception (never write neither): when the recipe's sources differ in size, the sizes are checked first, the pre-packed file stays a job (no skip, no `PACK-CONFLICT`) and the recipe fails with `PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH`. When `packing.prefer = raw` but the raw sources differ in size, the recipe fails (`PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH`) and the pre-packed file is kept; it converts under the normal never-auto-decide rule (its sub-type is a suggestion, so it needs `--accept-suggestions` or a rule).
  - (pointer, Note CO7: a generated member that a real map replaces is skipped `GEN-REPLACED` before M9, so it never reaches M9 and `packing.prefer` never sees it.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **M6 Two-pass resolve:** `packing.*` of a packed output is read from its profile resolved with a placeholder analysis (the packed image does not exist yet); the real packed image is then assembled, analysed and resolved again for everything else. A rule that matches on `alpha` therefore cannot change `packing.*`. The same holds for `channels.grayscale_source`: the value of that first profile is stored in `PackJob::gray` and used by the plan-time assembly, the provenance, the report and the pipeline's assembly, so the written image is the analysed one even when the final profile's value differs.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **M12 Provenance:** the recipe's sub-type is `recipe:<kind>`; each packed channel is recorded under `pack.r`, `pack.g`, `pack.b`, `pack.a` as `source:<file>:R` (`source:<file>:luma` with `channels.grayscale_source = luma`, `source:1-<file>:R` when inverted), `default:<value>` or `fill:<code>`.

## Note N — M3a converter GUI decisions (owner and M3a plan, 2026-10-10)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CA1, 2026-10-18)
- **N3 Proceed anyway** (owner): exporting with undecided textures asks "Proceed anyway / Cancel"; proceeding skips the undecided textures and never decides for the user. (Dialog half superseded by CA1 and CA3: Review's Export button is the Proceed anyway, and it is no longer a dialog. The rule that undecided textures are skipped and never decided still holds.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **N1 Preview size limit 4096 by default** (owner): replaces the 8192 of #19 for the GUI preview only. Values 1024–16384; 16384 only as an explicit setting. The preview encodes only mips at or below the limit; each is byte-identical to the exported mip.

## Note P — M3b set editor and PGPatcher JSON decisions (owner and M3b plan, 2026-10-10)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC10, 2026-10-29)
- **P8 A bad `sdc-project.toml` makes sets and JSON read-only** (plan); `sdc.toml` editing continues. (superseded by FC10)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC11, 2026-10-29)
- **P21 Read-only `sdc-project.toml` at export** (plan, execution ruling): the JSON is still written but its SHA-256 is not recorded (a note says so), so the next export asks before overwriting. A byte-identical file on disk is never prompted. (superseded by FC11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **P25 LOSS-ALPHA-DATA is narrower** (owner, 2026-10-11: "the alpha warning is too broad"): a varying alpha dropped from a `pbr_normal` (vanilla specular, which PBR normals do not use) is the Info `ALPHA-PBR-NORMAL-DROPPED`, not a block; and an `alpha.policy = "remove"` the user set explicitly (any layer but a built-in default, the same test as the alpha-meaning check, spec §5.2) raises neither code, since the user already decided. Every other discarded varying alpha still blocks.

## Owner checks pending

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AW1, 2026-10-15)
3. **`_normalgl` / `_normaldx` precedence.** They state the green convention as a fact that beats presets, configs and rules but not `--set`. (superseded by AW1)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note AW1, 2026-10-15)
4. **Plain `_normal` / `_nrm`.** It stays unresolved, even with `--accept-suggestions high` or `--allow`, until a preset, config, rule or `--set` states `opengl` or `directx`. (superseded by AW1)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note M1, 2026-10-14)
7. **Open packing questions for M2.5 (spec §14 #18–#21):** the env-mask source of a packed `cpm`/`vanilla` `_m` and the constant height of a `cpm` `_m`; raw maps without a recipe (`_opacity`, specular-only and fuzz-only sets, RGB `_sheen`); the scale of a raw specular map; whether a set missing its packed map should exit 1. (superseded by M1, owner 2026-10-10: the cpm `_m` height is 0)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
8. **Interim `IN-FLOAT-LDR` block.** Until the M6 transfer split, a 32-bit float source written to anything but BC6H needs `--allow IN-FLOAT-LDR`. Cost if wrong: float albedo TIFFs need the flag.

## Note R — per-texture packing fills and the _rfaos choice (owner, 2026-10-11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC3, 2026-10-29)
- **R-fills-2 GUI: per-output fills, the recipe choice and the skin follow (owner, 2026-10-11).** Owner: "packing fill values needs to be per texture, not a global value ... take them out of advanced and put them in rmaos / rfaos / m ... cards"; "vanilla m doesn't need a fill. It is a one channel texture"; "keep fuzz fill for rfaos"; "i assume specular is for non pbr normals" (it is the `_rmaos`/`_rfaos` A channel, the PBR specular level). **Fills** are drawn on the packed output's card, only for a channel that has no source: `_rmaos` roughness, metallic, AO, specular; `_rfaos` roughness, fuzz, AO, specular (no metallic); cpm `_m` glossiness, metallic, height (shown under the slot cards, as the cpm `_m` fills no slot); vanilla `_m`, the parallax `_p` and the opacity channel have none, and `_rmaos` never shows fuzz. A channel with a source shows read-only "from <file>". The value is a 0..255 box (it shows the neutral default while unset) and a Reset that removes the key. Each is stored at `packing.fill.<role>` on that output's own `[files]` entry, so it does not leak to another set; an edit commits when the drag or typed value settles, as one undo step. Old project-wide `defaults.packing.fill.*` keys still load and still apply under the per-output ones; the Advanced drawer's fill panel is gone and the drawer lists them on one read-only line "Project-wide fills (legacy): ..." with a Clear button. **Where the recipe is written (reading of the Task 1 ruling):** a `packing.recipe` left on the key of the output that is not planned reads as an unmatched `[files]` entry (CFG-FILE-UNMATCHED, with a Remove button on the entry that holds the choice) and the resolved profile of the output reads `auto`. The card therefore writes `rmaos`/`rfaos` on the key of the recipe it picks (`<base>_rfaos` for RFAOS), removes `packing.recipe` from the other key so the two never disagree, and moves the fills both recipes have (roughness, AO, specular) to the new key (metallic and fuzz are dropped). The selector shows the recipe the engine chose, with "(auto)" while neither key holds an explicit value. **RFAOS switches the set to Advanced skin by engine suggestion only:** the card writes just the recipe (and the fill moves). With the recipe resolved to `rfaos` the engine (R-skin-1) suggests `skin_albedo` and `skin_sk` for the members whose sub-type is inferred, and suggests the vanilla ones again when the recipe goes back to `rmaos`; a member the user set has a provenance other than `Suggestion` and stays put. Nothing is written for members and there is no tracked list (an earlier draft had `[project] auto_skin`; it was dropped as redundant, since it turned suggestions into explicit `[files]` entries). It works the same while `sdc-project.toml` is read-only. A fill a recipe switch removes (metallic going to RFAOS, fuzz going to RMAOS) is reported on the card ("metallic fill removed (RFAOS has no metallic channel)") until the next edit. **Slot 3:** `SlotKind::SkinSk` (suffix `_sk`, label "Skin _sk") shares slot 3 with the emissive `_g`; a set holding both is a slot clash and blocks export ("_g and _sk both use slot 3"), like `_cnr`/`_f` at slot 7. `skin_wet` stays unslotted. **Dropdown:** the group "Advanced skin (Community Shaders)" (`skin_albedo`, `skin_rfaos`, `skin_sk`, `skin_wet`) follows the PBR group, and `ui_sky` moved from "Colour maps" to a last group "Other". Sub-type labels stay `id` with spaces ("skin rfaos"), the `pbr_*` style. (superseded by FC3)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note AG90, 2026-10-14)
  - **C1:** an Advanced skin set (`skin_albedo` diffuse) is a PBR set for the PGPatcher JSON and every PBR export check (owner: "Rfaos = skin … very different shader", still Community Shaders TruePBR). The GUI's `is_pbr_diffuse` (`pbr_albedo` or `skin_albedo`) sets `SetView.pbr` (P5 amended), `SetView.skin` gives the "skin → JSON" badge, the PBR follow and Accept-all ordering treat `skin_albedo` as PBR, a skin set's `_rfaos` keeps its name under the set prefix (it had been written as `<prefix>_rmaos.dds`) and is named in slot6, and an own `_sk` is always named in slot3 (PGPatcher's slot 3 default is not known to find `_sk`).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CL40 and Note DD5, 2026-10-20)
- **R6 The bake decision lives on the `_n`** (plan): a baking set upgrades its `pbr_normal`'s built-in `mips.normal = renormalize` to `renormalize_bake` with the new provenance `set`; an explicit `renormalize` from any other layer opts the set out. The `_n` is renormalised either way. `normal`, `landscape_normal` and `msn` never bake (no bake into vanilla `_n` alpha or `_m` green, spec §6.3). A companion `BakeJob` attaches only to roughness members (`rmaos`, `skin_rfaos`); a `_cnr` only self-bakes (execution ruling, Tasks 7 and 8).
  - (superseded for one opt-in by Note CL40, 2026-10-20: an explicit `renormalize_bake` on a `normal` bakes its set's complex `_m` gloss)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **R-skin-1 Advanced skin sub-types (owner, 2026-10-11).** Owner: "Add a separate section to hold Advanced skin maps, rfaos, sk(grayscale vs vanilla), wet(normal w/binary mask or grayscale height)"; "advanced skin uses unorm as it's albedo, that's why I said it should be it's own thing"; "_wet is either a normal with a binary mask or a grayscale height map, advanced skin supports both map types with no distinction"; "_sk maps for advanced skin use only the red channel"; "ensure all advanced skin matierials are prefixed like pbr is, so it's not confusing". Four sub-types, all `cs` only (unavailable on `cpm`/`vanilla`, no fallback): `skin_albedo` (Color; BC7 **UNORM**, never sRGB, so it is not `is_pbr_color`; alpha like `diffuse`, the mesh decides), `skin_rfaos` (Mixed-Packed; the former `rfaos`, unchanged formats), `skin_sk` (Grayscale; red only, BC4 UNORM) and `skin_wet` (see below). **Rename:** `rfaos` became `skin_rfaos`. `rfaos` stays a deserialise alias (`sdc.toml`, presets, `--set`, rules), the new id is written, the schema lists `rfaos` as deprecated, and the sub-type table `[subtypes.rfaos]` of `sdc-base` is now `[subtypes.skin_rfaos]`. The packed file suffix stays `_rfaos` (PGPatcher/CS naming) and the `packing.recipe` value stays `rfaos` (it names the output recipe, not a sub-type). Output bytes and names are unchanged; the golden cases only changed where they print the sub-type id (`skin_rfaos`). Existing projects: a set with an `_rfaos` member or a fuzz (`_sheen`) source now infers `skin_albedo` (UNORM, High) for its diffuse instead of `pbr_albedo` (sRGB), so that diffuse's tag and bytes change, `if-changed` re-encodes it, and `--accept-suggestions high` now converts it. **`skin_wet`** has two forms of one sub-type. Grey content (`R = G = B` within 4/255, the `VAL-M-GRAYSCALE` test) is a height map: BC4 UNORM, plain box mips, alpha removed. Anything else is a tangent-space normal with a binary mask in alpha: BC7 UNORM, RGB renormalised like `normal`, alpha policy `data`. The form is picked by analysis as a fact below every explicit layer, so a preset, config, rule or `--set` that sets `format` (BC4 vs BC7) wins (BC4 on colour content reads red only; `CHAN-NOT-GRAY` applies). `SKIN-WET-FORM` (Info) states which form was chosen and why. `wet` collides with no vocabulary term; it is a suffix only in a cs Advanced skin set (R-review I3). **Detection:** a set is Advanced skin when it contains an `_rfaos` member, or a raw packing input whose resolved `packing.recipe` builds `_rfaos` (`auto` with a fuzz source, or `rfaos`; read per set the way Pass 1 does, `set_is_advanced_skin`). There the diffuse suggests `skin_albedo`, `_sk` suggests `skin_sk` and `_wet` suggests `skin_wet`, all High. Outside one `_sk` keeps `backlight_tint` (Low) and `_wet` stays part of the base name (R-review I3; the earlier low `skin_wet` suggestion is gone). **Slots (open question):** `skin_albedo` is the Diffuse slot. `skin_sk` belongs to slot 3 (glow/skin, P6 index 3) but the set editor has no slot kind for it yet, so Task 2 must add one. `skin_wet` stays unslotted in the GUI set model and the PGPatcher JSON. Owner, 2026-10-11: "it's currently patched at runtime, I think it is forced into slot 5 or 7 using a non standard method"; sdc only converts it. **Codes:** Task 1 had reused `PACK-CONFLICT` for the two `packing.recipe` notices. They are now `PACK-RECIPE-CONFLICT` (the two keys disagree) and `PACK-RECIPE-UNBUILDABLE` (`rfaos` asked for on a metallic-only set), both Warning; `PACK-CONFLICT` keeps its pre-packed meaning. `SKIN-WET-FORM` is the third new code (catalogue 59 to 62).

It is a one channel texture"; "keep fuzz fill for rfaos"; "i assume specular is for non pbr normals" (it is the `_rmaos`/`_rfaos` A channel, the PBR specular level). **Fills** are drawn on the packed output's card, only for a channel that has no source: `_rmaos` roughness, metallic, AO, specular; `_rfaos` roughness, fuzz, AO, specular (no metallic); cpm `_m` glossiness, metallic, height (shown under the slot cards, as the cpm `_m` fills no slot); vanilla `_m`, the parallax `_p` and the opacity channel have none, and `_rmaos` never shows fuzz. ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **R-skin-1 Advanced skin sub-types (owner, 2026-10-11).** Owner: "Add a separate section to hold Advanced skin maps, rfaos, sk(grayscale vs vanilla), wet(normal w/binary mask or grayscale height)"; "advanced skin uses unorm as it's albedo, that's why I said it should be it's own thing"; "_wet is either a normal with a binary mask or a grayscale height map, advanced skin supports both map types with no distinction"; "_sk maps for advanced skin use only the red channel"; "ensure all advanced skin matierials are prefixed like pbr is, so it's not confusing". The packed file suffix stays `_rfaos` (PGPatcher/CS naming) and the `packing.recipe` value stays `rfaos` (it names the output recipe, not a sub-type). Output bytes and names are unchanged; the golden cases only changed where they print the sub-type id (`skin_rfaos`). Existing projects: a set with an `_rfaos` member or a fuzz (`_sheen`) source now infers `skin_albedo` (UNORM, High) for its diffuse instead of `pbr_albedo` (sRGB), so that diffuse's tag and bytes change, `if-changed` re-encodes it, and `--accept-suggestions high` now converts it. **`skin_wet`** has two forms of one sub-type. Grey content (`R = G = B` within 4/255, the `VAL-M-GRAYSCALE` test) is a height map: BC4 UNORM, plain box mips, alpha removed. Anything else is a tangent-space normal with a binary mask in alpha: BC7 UNORM, RGB renormalised like `normal`, alpha policy `data`. The form is picked by analysis as a fact below every explicit layer, so a preset, config, rule or `--set` that sets `format` (BC4 vs BC7) wins (BC4 on colour content reads red only; `CHAN-NOT-GRAY` applies). `SKIN-WET-FORM` (Info) states which form was chosen and why. `wet` collides with no vocabulary term; it is a suffix only in a cs Advanced skin set (R-review I3). **Detection:** a set is Advanced skin when it contains an `_rfaos` member, or a raw packing input whose resolved `packing.recipe` builds `_rfaos` (`auto` with a fuzz source, or `rfaos`; read per set the way Pass 1 does, `set_is_advanced_skin`). There the diffuse suggests `skin_albedo`, `_sk` suggests `skin_sk` and `_wet` suggests `skin_wet`, all High. Outside one `_sk` keeps `backlight_tint` (Low) and `_wet` stays part of the base name (R-review I3; the earlier low `skin_wet` suggestion is gone). **Slots (open question):** `skin_albedo` is the Diffuse slot. `skin_sk` belongs to slot 3 (glow/skin, P6 index 3) but the set editor has no slot kind for it yet, so Task 2 must add one. `skin_wet` stays unslotted in the GUI set model and the PGPatcher JSON. Owner, 2026-10-11: "it's currently patched at runtime, I think it is forced into slot 5 or 7 using a non standard method"; sdc only converts it. **Codes:** Task 1 had reused `PACK-CONFLICT` for the two `packing.recipe` notices. They are now `PACK-RECIPE-CONFLICT` (the two keys disagree) and `PACK-RECIPE-UNBUILDABLE` (`rfaos` asked for on a metallic-only set), both Warning; `PACK-CONFLICT` keeps its pre-packed meaning. `SKIN-WET-FORM` is the third new code (catalogue 59 to 62).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **R1 Normal mip 0 is renormalised by default** (owner: "per-preset option, default renormalise"): field `mips.normal_base` = `renormalize` (default) or `keep`, settable per preset, rule, `[files]` entry or `--set`, for authors who keep short normals on purpose. `CHAN-NORMAL-SHORT` (info) reports a normal source whose mean length at mip 0 is below 0.9 (not for Z-rebuilt sources). Controller correction (2026-10-11): the normal chain and the bake average mip 0 **as it is written**, unit length under `renormalize` and as authored under `keep`, so variance reflects real normal divergence rather than exporter shortness; a uniformly short flat `_n` adds no roughness by default. Outputs change for existing normal maps with short sources (mip 0 and the levels averaged from it).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **R8 `SET-*` triggers** (plan): `SET-MISSING-COMPANION` (info) for a `cs` PBR set with a `pbr_normal` but no roughness member or the reverse, an `_msn` without `_s`, a `_flow` without `_n`; `SET-COMPANION-DDS` (warning) when the missing companion is only a DDS (in the set or on disk); `SET-COMPANION-OUTSIDE` (info) when it is an image on disk next to the file but not in the batch; both directions. The code goes on the job it concerns. A PBR set holding only an albedo raises none. A job that already carries `CFG-UNAVAILABLE` skips the `_msn`/`_flow` `SET-MISSING-COMPANION` note (pre-flight ruling: an unavailable profile raises nothing else).

## Note S — M6 Track B: inputs and formats (plan rulings, 2026-10-11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AV-S1, 2026-10-15)
- **S7 DDS header patch (owner idea 2026-10-11):** header-only, payload byte-identical (new header + the old bytes after the old header). Patchable: flags, caps, linear size/pitch, mip count (the whole-level count that fills the payload), reserved fields, `DDPF_NORMAL`, DX10 fields, `ATI1`/`ATI2` → DX10, legacy → DX10 where the tag needs it, and the tag only on an explicit `--tag`, `--subtype` or GUI choice. Refused with a reason (`DDS-HEADER-UNPATCHABLE`): unreadable, cubemap/array, a format sdc does not write, sRGB on a format without one, a payload holding no whole chain, missing mips when a full chain is wanted, BC1 with transparent texels, the wrong block format under `--format`. `DDS-HEADER-PATCHED` (info) lists the fixes. CLI `sdc patch-header` needs `--out-dir` or `--in-place`; the GUI writes a copy to the export folder only. Not recorded in the manifest.
  - *Amended (final review I1, 2026-10-11):* the GUI refuses the patch, with the reason under that item's panel, when another item in the job list exports to the same output key (e.g. `rock.dds` next to `rock.png`); an existing file at the destination alone is not a refusal, so the same DDS can be re-patched.
  - **`--out-dir` onto the input is a usage error** (controller ruling, Task 10 review, overriding the plan's Step 3 code): an `--out-dir` that resolves to an input itself (the S6 guard), or two inputs with one file name, exits 2 before anything is written. `PATCHED` is printed only after the write succeeds; a write failure exits 3 and stops the batch (superseded by AV-S1: exit 1 per failed file, the batch goes on).
  - **The GUI Patch writes under `export_root()`** (controller ruling, Task 11 review, overriding the brief's code): the copy goes to the export root (the chosen export folder, else the default `sdc-out`), so the button never silently does nothing; every error reaches the status line and the confirmation always ends.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)

The writer is unchanged.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **S-mips-2 Where non-box filters are allowed** (spec §3.2, §5.5 read literally). `tent`, `mitchell` and `kaiser` require category Color, sRGB content (domain `LinearLight`) and an alpha policy that does not weight (`remove` or `data`). `tent` is also allowed for HDR, `format.is_hdr()` (`bc6h_uf16` or `rgba16f`), but only for category Color or Hdr with an alpha policy that does not weight; HDR with `mitchell`/`kaiser` is refused ("HDR mips use box or tent"). Anything else with a non-box filter set at sub-type or file scope is **`CFG-INVALID`** (Error; an unscoped value falls back to box instead, S-mips-13), with the reason: normals, `_msn`, `_flow`, data, grayscale, packed, roughness members, linear-content colour, and colour with `cutout`/`blend`/`cutout_blend`. The GUI shows these as locked options. The engine also falls back to box in `mips.rs` (`effective_filter`), but only as a guard; a blocked job never reaches it. **Owner question 2 closed** (2026-10-11): alpha-tested and blended colour (foliage) stays on alpha-weighted box; no alpha-weighted tent (revisit later).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **Deferred:** the Kaiser width/alpha/stretch syntax; alpha-weighted tent for foliage; `resize` upscaling (`pot-up`, `pot-nearest`) and a size cap; matching the resized mip 0's coverage to the source (S-mips-8); resizing pack sources of different sizes to a common size (`PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH` still errors); the 16K box peak and the single-threaded `powf` (S-mips-11).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **S-mips-6 `resize` = `none` (default) or `pot-down`**, a top-level profile field (`--set resize=pot-down`). `pot-down` maps each axis independently to the largest power of two ≤ its size (1000×600 → 512×512; a power-of-two axis is kept). The aspect may change; UVs are normalised, so meshes map the same. The resize runs in Preprocess step 6 (after sRGB decode), in the Work's domain, with the **effective mip filter** and edge: box is area-exact (the generalised `downsample_area`), tent/Mitchell/Kaiser are polyphase on RGB. Alpha is always area box; alpha-weighted colour is weighted by area × alpha; a roughness member is resized on r²; normals, `_msn` and `_flow` are box-resized as vectors and mip 0 is then renormalised unless `mips.normal_base = keep`. Upscaling and a size cap are out of scope. **Owner question 3 closed** (2026-10-11): no preset defaults to `pot-down`; `DIM-NPOT` blocks until the user opts in.
  - **Resize runs at the end of `preprocess_with`** (pre-flight F1): after the Hdr clamp, in the function the pipeline and `BakeJob::load_lengths` call (`preprocess` is only a wrapper), so the written DDS and the bake both see the resized image.
  - **A box resize never clamps** (pre-flight M8): only a non-box resize clamps LinearLight RGB to [0, 1] (polyphase overshoot), mirroring `finalize`; a float LDR value > 1 survives a box resize until Quantise.
  - **`axis_taps` returns identity taps when n == m**, so an axis that is already a power of two is copied unchanged under `pot-down` with any filter (no blur at scale 1).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **S-mips-7 Resize in planning:** `DIM-NPOT`, `DIM-SMALL` and `DIM-FLOW-SMALL` test the **post-resize** size. A resized job gets the new **`DIM-RESIZED`** (Info) code: "1000x600 resized to 512x512 (resize = pot-down)", data `{"from":[w,h],"to":[w',h']}`; a power-of-two input with `pot-down` gets none. The bake plans its shift on the post-resize sizes and `BakeJob::load_lengths` builds the pyramid from the resized, renormalised `_n`; the bake's `NormalVariance` is built on `target_dims`, so the shift check holds on an NPOT `pot-down` pair. `BakeJob::identity_sha256` adds the `_n`'s `resize` only when it is not `none`, so existing identities are unchanged. `PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH` still compares source sizes before assembly; the packed image is resized after assembly.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **S-mips-10 GUI:** the basic Mipmaps group's "Filter" row is a live combo (Box / Tent / Mitchell / Kaiser); disallowed options are greyed out with the S-mips-2 reason. It stays in the basic panel (owner, 2026-10-11). An "Edge" row (Wrap / Clamp) sits under it, locked "only tent, Mitchell and Kaiser read the edge" while the filter is box; a default edge or resize the profile does not serialise shows as Wrap / None. "Resize" (None / Down to a power of two) is the last Advanced texture field. The `DIM-NPOT` decision box gains "Resize to a power of two" next to "Acknowledge for this texture"; it writes `resize = "pot-down"` on that file's `[files]` entry.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **Deferred:** the Kaiser width/alpha/stretch syntax; `resize` upscaling (`pot-up`, `pot-nearest`) and a size cap; matching the resized mip 0's coverage to the source (S-mips-8); resizing pack sources of different sizes to a common size (`PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH` still errors); the 16K box peak and the single-threaded `powf` (S-mips-11).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **S-mips-11 Memory and speed:** the new kernels are separable and row-parallel on the shared rayon pool. Admission (`api::job_bytes`) adds `8·w·h` bytes for a non-box filter and `32·w·h` for a resize, with `(w, h)` from one shared `input_dims` helper (also used by `estimated_bytes`: a DDS by its header, else `load::image_size`), so a re-encoded DDS is admitted on its real size. The 13 GiB box peak at 16K and the single-threaded per-channel `powf` in Preprocess stay deferred to the scheduler revisit.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **S-mips-7 Resize in planning:** `DIM-SMALL` and `DIM-FLOW-SMALL` test the **post-resize** size (`resize::target_dims`). A resized job gets the **`DIM-RESIZED`** (Info) code, data `{"from":[w,h],"to":[w',h']}` (its message is FM29); a job whose size does not change gets none. The bake plans its shift on the post-resize sizes and `BakeJob::load_lengths` builds the pyramid from the resized, renormalised `_n`; the bake's `NormalVariance` is built on `target_dims`, so the shift check holds on an NPOT pair. `BakeJob::identity_sha256` adds the `_n`'s `resize` only when it is not `none`, so existing identities are unchanged. `PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH` still compares source sizes before assembly; the packed image is resized after assembly.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **Deferred:** the Kaiser width/alpha/stretch syntax; a size cap; matching the resized mip 0's coverage to the source (S-mips-8); resizing pack sources of different sizes to a common size (`PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH` still errors); the 16K box peak and the single-threaded `powf` (S-mips-11).

## Note T — M6 Track D: presets (plan rulings, 2026-10-12)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC10, 2026-10-29)
- **T8 Verbs (owner / plan, 2026-10-12).** (superseded by FC10)
  - `diff` compares profiles from presets alone (rules are not shown: spec gap).
  - `export` writes the preset's own TOML under a new id (`<id>-export` or `--id`); `base` stays a link, and there is no `--flat` (owner).
  - `export` writes the parsed table re-serialised, not the original text: comments are dropped and keys may be reordered (final review Minor 9). The output is equivalent and validates.
  - `export` copies `suffix_aliases` as they are and does not strip them. A copy of a preset with suffix aliases therefore cannot load beside the original (the aliases conflict, `CFG-PRESET-SKIPPED`); rename or remove the aliases before putting the copy in the user presets folder. The `export` help says so (final review Minor 8).
  - `export --out` refuses to overwrite the source file of any loaded preset (final review Minor 10).
  - `diff` help and its human output footer say "rules are not compared" (final review Minor 13).
  - `validate` reports the first problem per file, and validates a folder file in place of itself.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FJ, 2026-11-01)
- **T13 rgbcx rounding patch (owner, 2026-10-12).**
  - The vendored rgbcx's BC4/BC5/BC3-alpha decode model now rounds to nearest like bcdec. It used to floor the interpolants, so a wider search found endpoint pairs that were exact only under the floor.
  - Gain at the default radius: BC4 +3.07 dB, BC5 +2.61 dB (mean PSNR under bcdec, bench corpus). PSNR now rises monotonically with the radius.
  - A new manifest `rgbcx_patch` field makes old BC3/BC4/BC5 outputs re-encode. Like `bc7e_patch` it is a content tag, `bc4-round-to-nearest@sha256:<hash of the patched vendor/bc7enc_rdo/rgbcx.h>` (final review Minor 11), so an in-place change to the patched header also re-encodes them.
  - Two bench gates moved from byte-exact to never-worse (per-block bcdec SSE for BC3 alpha, BC4, BC5); BC1 and BC3 colour stay byte-exact.
  - Known quirk: sdc's own BC3 alpha validation decodes through `bcdec_rs::bc3`, which uses `(6a0+a1+1)/7` and matches neither model.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **T9 Migration table (plan, 2026-10-12).** Presets none, config none, manifest records v1 → v2 (`schema` set to 2; the manifest's key is `schema`). Version 0 is refused. `PresetError::NewerConfigSchema(file, v)` is the spec's `ConfigError::NewerSchema`. Config files accept an optional `schema_version`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **T2 BC1 rule mechanics (plan, 2026-10-12).**
  - `RuleMatch` had no sub-type matcher, so it gained `match.subtypes` (matched while layering, never while deciding a sub-type).
  - `match.alpha` matches the source's alpha content, so there are two rules: `none`, and `constant255` for sub-types whose policy resolves to `remove`.
  - No resolved-policy matcher was added (it would be circular).
  - An explicit non-`remove` policy on an opaque colour map under compact is `FMT-BC1-ALPHA`.
  - `bc1_transparent_black` stays false.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **T8 Verbs (owner / plan, 2026-10-12).**
  - `diff` compares profiles from presets alone (rules are not shown: spec gap).
  - `export` writes the preset's own TOML under a new id (`<id>-export` or `--id`); `base` stays a link, and there is no `--flat` (owner).
  - `export` copies the preset's text as loaded, sets `id` in place and removes only `[suffix_aliases]`, so comments and order stay and the copy loads beside the original; a preset the schema migration changed is re-serialised from its migrated table, also without aliases (Note FC10).
  - `export --out` refuses to overwrite the source file of any loaded preset (final review Minor 10).
  - `diff` help and its human output footer say "rules are not compared" (final review Minor 13).
  - `validate` reports the first problem per file, and validates a folder file in place of itself.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **T4 `resize = half` (owner, 2026-10-12).** Both sides halve together when the larger is above 512 (`HALF_FLOOR`); the aspect ratio is kept and the larger side floors (1024×512 becomes 512×256); there is no POT step; each side is at least 1 px; `DIM-RESIZED` names the mode; admission counts it like `pot-down`.

## Note U — M6 Track E: NIF-aware alpha and fewer confirmations (plan rulings, 2026-10-12)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC1, 2026-10-29)
- **U3 The GUI accepts High suggestions (owner, 2026-10-12).** (amended by FC1)
  - `AppModel::accept` is `High`, and `request()` sends it. Medium and Low suggestions still ask.
  - Explicit choices win: `[files]`, rules and `--set subtype` decide before any suggestion.
  - The CLI is unchanged (`--accept-suggestions high`).
  - The M3-era GUI test fixtures set `accept = None` to keep their undecided members.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC1, 2026-10-29)
- **U5 Mesh alpha evidence (plan, 2026-10-12).** (amended by FC1)
  - `BatchRequest::mesh_alpha` is keyed by `shader::colour_mesh_key`, the output stem, so a renamed `x_albedo.png` is keyed `x`. The CLI sends none.
  - The GUI reads it with raw SQL (`meshes::mesh_alpha`). The query is composed with `format!` from a static `FROM` fragment shared with `mesh_votes` (`SLOT0_SOURCE_SHAPES`), and the key is a bound parameter, so no user data reaches the SQL text (review ruling, Task 4). The fragment selects:
    - lighting shapes using `<key>.dds` in slot 0;
    - non-derived copies of enabled origins;
    - TruePBR excluded;
    - one entry per NIF path, block and alpha property.
  - Unlike the votes, every lighting shader type counts.
  - NiAlphaProperty bit 0 is blend and bit 9 is test. A shape with no property is no alpha.
  - A database read error gives no evidence, as in `mesh_votes` (the Meshes view reports database errors), so the alpha question is asked as before. Surfacing the error in the planner is parked as minor.

## Note W — M6 Track F2: RNM normal layers (plan rulings, 2026-10-12)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DY16, 2026-10-28)
- **W11 GUI.**

  - Base-level codes show in the RNM section.
  - The target switch writes `rnm.target` on the layer file's key.
  - (Overturned for listing by DY16: layer files are helper rows under the tiles, still never tiles.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AW1, 2026-10-15)
- **W13 `_norm`.** `_norm` (rnm.py's base name) is a vocabulary term `Normal(None)`, like `_normal`. Its green convention is unstated, so it asks `IN-GREEN-UNSTATED` once. rnm.py reads `_norm` as OpenGL, but sdc does not guess. (superseded by AW1)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **W9 AO.**
  - The AO source is, in order:
    1. the set's raw AO file (vocabulary role `PackingInput(Ao)`), channel R;
    2. otherwise the set's `_rmaos`/`_rfaos` **file**, channel B.
  - It is read only for a `bent` layer in `ao_blend` mode with `rnm.bent.ao = true`.
  - `RNM-AO-MISSING` (info) goes on the base when that mode wants AO and there is none.

## Note X — Complex grass sub-type (plan rulings, 2026-10-13)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC10, 2026-10-29)
- **X3 Vanilla.** The sub-type stays encodable under vanilla, with a `GRASS-VANILLA` warning. It is never auto-tagged there (R1, owner-confirmed). The pickers never offer it on any target (`policy::offered`; owner 2026-10-13): the set shader `complex_grass` or the GRAS auto-detect chooses it, and an explicit other set shader suppresses the auto-detect. (superseded by FC10)

An unreadable input root itself is still an error. `inspect` skips the same entries silently. The GUI shows no notice for skipped entries yet (follow-up). Per-thread glob matcher cache: it is never evicted (shortcut: bounded by the distinct patterns of one run; evict if a long-lived process ever feeds it unbounded patterns). (superseded by FC3)

## Note Z — M6 Track F3a: multi-select and bulk settings (plan rulings, 2026-10-13)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC3, 2026-10-29)
- **Z11 Encoding follows type (owner, 2026-10-13, Task 9).** (superseded by FC3)
  - When a plan changes a texture's sub-type (a set shader, a slot command, a drag, the PBR follow-on, a manual or bulk sub-type edit), its explicit `[files]` format and tag are checked against `policy::allowed_encodings` for the new sub-type. A pair it no longer allows is cleared (`clear_field("format")`, which clears the tag too), so the new type's default applies. An explicit pair that is still allowed is kept. Other fields are not touched.
  - A texture in a locked slot (Z10), or whose Format row is field-locked, is not touched; the note reports it as kept.
  - The sub-types before a plan chain are kept by `apply_plan`. They are compared with the final pass when the chain settles in `finish_plan`, so a type that is only intermediate (pass 1 of the two-pass flow) is never cleaned up after. The cleanup is one batched edit and one replan. Its own one-shot guard keeps that replan from cleaning again, and it does not use the `auto_replans` budget of the output-path replan.
  - Undo: the cleanup is folded into the user step that started the chain. That step is tracked explicitly: an edit that records a step and re-plans marks the chain. A change no step caused (scan votes, a document changed outside history) is its own step, "Encoding follows type". Plans of an undo, redo, open or reload never clean up.
  - A short note follows the status line ("g.png: complex grass → pbr albedo; format reset to the default (BC7 · sRGB)."). Several textures read as one count line with each listed on hover. No dialog. The next edit, an undo, redo, open or reload dismisses it.
  - Merge of main (Task 9 Part A): the bulk Format row offers the (format, tag) pairs every selected texture allows (`bulk_encodings`, from `fields::encoding_options`); when they share none, every pair any of them allows. A pair a texture's sub-type does not allow skips it ("not valid for this map", applies to K of N). Copy settings copies the format with its tag the same way (R13): the pair is written through `set_format`, the tag is not copied on its own, and a texture that does not allow the pair is skipped.

## Note AA — M6 Track F3b: input aliases, output names and the PGPatcher rename (plan rulings, 2026-10-14)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC11, 2026-10-29)
- **AA2 Output names.** A set's outputs take a new base name; each output keeps its folder and Skyrim suffix (`renamed_output`). Plan-only: nothing on disk is renamed; outputs written under old names stay (the manifest and `OUT-UNKNOWN-EXISTING` rules apply). (superseded by FC11)
  - Non-PBR sets (and every set off CS): `output_name` in the set's `[[sets]]` table (a reserved key, never in the JSON). `engine_inputs` now sends explicit `output_paths` on every target for those sets.
  - A name ending in a map suffix (`wall_n`) is refused (`check_output_name`): the suffix belongs to each output, and the name would read back as base plus suffix, so a re-plan would not be idempotent. Windows-illegal and reserved device names (`CON`, `COM1`…, any extension) and a trailing dot or space are refused (`check_file_name`).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AN1, 2026-10-15)
- **AA8 A renamed set's entry carries `match_diffuse`** (owner, 2026-10-14; since Note AN1 it carries no `matchN`). Evidence from the owner's load order (PGPatcher 1.2.0, read-only): all 713 rename entries in 169 JSONs pair `rename` with `texture`/`match_diffuse`/`match_normal`, and a frost atronach entry of that form wrote `textures\pbr\actors\frostatronach\frostatronach*.dds` (no leading `\`, lower case). The owner's test entry `{"match1": "dlc01\\clothes\\vampire\\robesf.dds", "rename": "textures\\pbr\\dlc01\\clothes\\vampire\\robesf2"}` matched the robe (so a `matchN` without `textures\` does match, as V2 says; PGPatcher source TP:209-212) and the files were present, yet no mesh was patched. The log names the cause, 48 times over 16 meshes: `Texture "\textures\pbr\dlc01\clothes\vampire\robesf2.dds" does not exist from PBR json … (Skipping)`. Without `match_diffuse`, PGPatcher substitutes `\` + rename for the whole path (pgpsim `prefix()`) and its file check fails. So when a set has a rename, the writer adds `match_diffuse` (owner: `texture` is the old name for it; PGPatcher copies `texture` into `match_diffuse`) = the matched texture (the lowest active match, else the derived diffuse, slot suffix cut) and writes `rename` as the renamed path; both are relative to `textures\pbr\`, like real mods. The match slots stay as the entry's matches (owner: matchN is the default; the PGPatcher wiki, Mod Authors `rename`, says rename renames "the matching part (from match_diffuse or match_normal)", which is why a renamed entry needs `match_diffuse` too). PGPatcher then keeps `textures\pbr` and appends the rename. Two costs. PGPatcher cuts each hit's path by `match_diffuse`'s length, so only meshes hit through the first matched texture are sure to get the renamed files (a hit through `path_contains` or a match slot naming another texture gets a wrong path; the set editor warns). And `match_diffuse` also matches by diffuse, so a set with `match1` off still matches its diffuse once renamed. Confirmed in real PGPatcher 1.2.0 (owner retest, 2026-10-14): `{"texture": "dlc01\\clothes\\vampire\\robesf", "rename": "dlc01\\clothes\\vampire\\robesf2"}` patched all 16 robe meshes to `textures\pbr\dlc01\clothes\vampire\robesf2*.dds` with no leading `\` and no warnings (`texture` is copied into `match_diffuse`, so sdc's form behaves the same).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AA2 Output names.** A set's outputs take a new base name; each output keeps its folder and Skyrim suffix (`renamed_output`). Plan-only: nothing on disk is renamed; outputs written under old names stay until they are deleted from Review's stale outputs (Note FC11); the manifest and `OUT-UNKNOWN-EXISTING` rules apply.
  - Non-PBR sets (and every set off CS): `output_name` in the set's `[[sets]]` table (a reserved key, never in the JSON). `engine_inputs` now sends explicit `output_paths` on every target for those sets.
  - A name ending in a map suffix (`wall_n`) is refused (`check_output_name`): the suffix belongs to each output, and the name would read back as base plus suffix, so a re-plan would not be idempotent. Windows-illegal and reserved device names (`CON`, `COM1`…, any extension) and a trailing dot or space are refused (`check_file_name`).

## Note AD — Community Shaders PBR texture sets and material objects (plan rulings, 2026-10-13)

A recorded file no block binds is reported, never deleted. An EDID whose case changed renames sdc's own unchanged file. (superseded by FC11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note BM, 2026-10-16)
- **AD7 Statics.** The PGPatcher entry of a set with a texture-set block is written when `Store::nifs_using_texture` finds non-LOD winning meshes for the set's key (`statics = "auto"`), or always/never by override. No scan writes no entry. LOD meshes are excluded before the 20,000-row cap, so a LOD-heavy texture still finds its non-LOD use.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AD5 Guard and orphans.** `[project] cs_pbr_sha256` records each file's hash. A file edited outside sdc, or present without a record, is kept and reported, never asked about. A recorded file no block binds is a stale row in Review, with Delete while it still has its recorded hash (Note FC11). An EDID whose case changed renames sdc's own unchanged file.

## Note AG — GUI suggestions, Tessalith (2026-10-14)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AG133, 2026-10-15)
- **AG2 Colour inputs:** every colour input gets two things.
  - A swatch that opens egui's colour-square popup (`egui::color_picker::color_picker_color32`, Alpha::Opaque).
  - A hex text field: `#RRGGBB`, `#` optional, case-insensitive. An invalid string turns the field red and does not commit; the value commits on Enter or focus loss.
  - Stored values keep their current type and range. TruePBR fields stay integers 0-255 with the existing clamping and reset, so the JSON is unchanged. **Amended by AG133:** TruePBR colours are now floats per channel, 0 to 10.
  - This overrides item 16's "no hex entry (Note P)" on the owner's word of 2026-10-14.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AG133, 2026-10-15)
- **AG9 Colour field conventions (Task 1):** one widget, `ui::color::color_field` (egui swatch popup + `#RRGGBB` box). TruePBR colours (`emissive_color`, `subsurface_color`, `coat_color`, `fuzz.color`) keep integer sRGB bytes 0-255 and the number boxes; swatch and hex commit through the same `set_json_value` edit (same merge, undo step, reset). The 3D View background is one sRGB grey (`Look::grey`, perceptual 0..1): swatch and hex edit it, and a non-grey pick or hex is stored as its Rec.709 luma. Sun, point light and radius are brightness values, not colours, so they get no picker. A hex box reverts to the stored value on focus loss when invalid. **Amended by AG133:** the TruePBR colours are float channels now, with float boxes in place of the 0-255 ones.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC5 and Note CA1, 2026-10-29)
- **AG11 Dialog keys (Task 2, item 4).** The shared `dialogs::modal_keys` returns `cancel` (Esc, or a click outside where allowed) and `enter` (Enter while no widget has focus and not on the frame the dialog opened). Esc is the dialog's Cancel everywhere. (superseded by FC5)
  - Click outside does nothing in: the rename dialogs (`set-rename`, and `rename_dialog` as before), Copy settings, Fill from names (its ticks, AG99) and the undecided-textures list (Proceed anyway; it has open combo popups and per-row choices a stray click would drop). It cancels in every other dialog. (The undecided-textures list is no longer a dialog; superseded by CA1.)
  - Enter: Save in Close/Open unsaved; Close in Export blocked; OK in Error; Keep running in Export running; Keep it in Overwrite JSON; Cancel in the Reset all and Delete set confirms. External change: Enter is Cancel, which keeps the edits in memory and leaves the file on disk alone (not Reload, which would drop unsaved edits; never Overwrite). Proceed anyway, Export warnings (N3) and Copy settings: Enter does nothing. Rename keeps its own Enter = Apply. Fill from names: Enter is Fill (AG99). (The Export blocked, Proceed anyway and Export warnings dialogs are gone; the Enter rules for them end with the dialogs, CA1 and CA19.) Enter semantics unchanged; the Enter default is now drawn filled, and the buttons are renamed (Note CE, CE1 to CE4). Rename's Enter now applies only while no widget has keyboard focus (CE8).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC5, 2026-10-29)
- **AG13 Delete set and Rename (Task 2, items 4 and 23).** Delete set asks "Delete set '<id>' (<N> members)? Its textures stay; only the set is removed." with Delete / Cancel; the deletion is still one undo step. The set menu groups Rename…, Rename outputs…  F2 and Rename inputs… under a "Rename" submenu, each with a one-line hover text, built from one data array (`rename_entries` in `ui/sets_column.rs`). The multi-selection menus (`rename_entries` in `model/bulk.rs`) are unchanged. (superseded by FC5)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB11, 2026-10-29)
- **AG22 One action list (Task 4, items 5 and 19).** `ui::actions::ACTIONS` is a static table: `id`, `label`, `area` (Global, Textures grid, Preview, Sets, Dialogs), `keys`, `palette` (offered in the palette), `enabled`, `run: Option<fn(&mut AppModel, &egui::Context)>`. `keys` is a slice, not one optional shortcut: Redo keeps Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z, the sheet has F1 and ?, the preview opens with Enter and Space. `run: None` marks a key handled where it belongs and only listed (a dialog's Enter and Esc, `modal_keys`). It lives in `ui` because `run` needs the egui context (the Textures | Meshes view is in egui memory) and Open folder needs the file picker. The F1 sheet, the Ctrl+K palette and the key handling read the table; the menus keep their own lists but take a shortcut's text from it (`keys_text`: the Undo/Redo hover, the preview's Close hover, "Rename outputs…  F2" in the set and multi-selection menus). docs/gui.md's Keyboard table is `markdown_table()`'s output and a test keeps them equal. A key may appear twice only in areas that are never live together (`Area::exclusive_with`: Esc clears the selection in the grid, closes the preview, cancels a dialog; Enter opens the preview from the grid and presses a dialog's default). Grid and Sets are live together.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC8 and Note CV7, 2026-10-29)
- **AG23 When keys act (Task 4).** One dispatcher, `actions::keys`, runs first in the frame (top of `ui::draw_with_scan`; `app.rs` calls it in the Meshes view), before any widget has seen the input, and matches the keys with the most modifiers first (egui ignores an extra Shift). First, because egui's text field deletes to the end of the line on Ctrl+K, and a key that runs an action must not also reach a widget. Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y moved into it from `app.rs` (`header::undo_redo_keys` is gone). Nothing fires while `model.dialog`, a sets-column box, any popup or menu, or any egui modal (the palette, the sheet, Rename, the slot commands) is open; Ctrl+S and Undo/Redo now wait for popups and modals and for a focused field too, where Ctrl+S used to fire anywhere. While a widget has the keyboard focus (Task 2's guard, `egui_wants_keyboard_input`) only Ctrl+K fires. egui drops the focus on Esc before any widget runs, so a field focused at the previous frame's start still owns this frame's Esc: Esc only leaves the field (before, Esc in the search box also cleared the selection). Grid and Sets keys act in the Textures view with no texture expanded, Preview keys while one is. (superseded by FC8)
  (amended by Note CV, item CV7)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC8 and Note CV7, 2026-10-29)
- **AG32 Keys honour "enabled" (Task 5 carry-over).** `actions::keys` runs an action only when its `enabled` says so, as the palette does: Ctrl+S on a clean project, Ctrl+Z with no history and Enter with no single texture selected do nothing and are not consumed. Up and Down (and Shift+Up/Down) are enabled only when the Textures grid drew last frame: `nav.cols` is 0 until the grid draws and `draw_with_scan` zeroes it every frame after the keys, so a hidden or collapsed Textures column, or one that has not drawn yet, leaves Up and Down off instead of stepping by a default width. Ctrl+S swallowed by a focused text field (AG23) sets the status line to "Ctrl+S: leave the text field first" when there is something to save. (superseded by FC8)
  (amended by Note CV, item CV7)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AG39, 2026-10-14)
- **AG35 Progress line (Task 5).** `done/total · elapsed · files/s · ETA` from the existing events only (no new engine events). `Pace` takes every time as an argument (a fake clock in tests). Done = rows no longer queued or running. Elapsed runs from `start_export` and stops at the end of the batch. Files/s is the number of finishes in the last 10 s divided by min(elapsed, 10 s). The ETA (remaining / files/s) is left out until 3 files have finished or 2 s have passed, and while nothing finished in the window. A finish is a FileDone, a FileFailed, or a skip that sets a row state. The drawer asks for a repaint every 500 ms while the export runs.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note CA16 and Note AG39, 2026-10-18)
- **AG38 Warnings tab (item 14).** One row per warning, sorted by code then file: code, severity, the message (wrapped), the file as a link. Virtualised with `show_rows`, so rows have a fixed height of three lines; a longer message is clipped there and shown whole on hover (`warning_line`: `CODE (severity): message`). The list is cached by the revision. The grouped-by-code view is gone from the drawer (`ExportModel::by_code` stays for the reports). (Superseded by CA16: the Warnings tab is gone; per-file warnings show on the Files row's hover and in Problems.)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note AU3, 2026-10-15)
- **AG76 Missing slots in magenta.** The resolver does not make up textures: render3d also sends RFAOS guesses through it, so a magenta answer there would tint every skin. Instead each `LoadReport::missing_textures` path gets a 1×1 magenta DDS with `replace_texture`, in the load's variant only and before the swap replay, so a real swap still wins. A cube slot refuses a 2D texture and keeps render3d's fallback. The panel lists the paths in magenta; a path whose stem matches a Textures item's game path (case-insensitive) has Open in Textures (selects it and switches to the Textures view).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC11, 2026-10-29)
- **AG90 Advanced skin sets get no PGPatcher JSON (owner, 2026-10-14: "no need for pgpatcher for advanced skin sets, future export json card").** This replaces final review C1. `SetView::pbr` is now true for a `pbr_albedo` diffuse only, so every PGPatcher reader leaves a `skin_albedo` set out: `is_json_set`, `json_entries` (writer, JSON editor, set preview), `export_blocks`, `export_asks`, prefix ownership, the PGPatcher `rename`, and the output-name rule. `shows_json_panel` is false for a skin set even if it holds old JSON settings, so there is no TruePBR card or prefix line; the badge reads "Advanced skin (no JSON)". The engine side is unchanged: `is_pbr_diffuse` still drives the PBR member follow-ons, and encoding is untouched. An old skin entry leaves the JSON at the next export that writes it, under the usual rules: Overwrite asks when the file was edited outside, and with no PBR set left nothing is written and the old file stays (the existing keep rule). The skin card has a disabled "Export JSON…" placeholder with the hover "Community Shaders Advanced Skin override export - spec pending". The set preview draws a skin set without a JSON entry, as a vanilla set (it would not be PGPatcher-patched in game either). Output location (owner confirmed, fix round 2): with no prefix ownership a skin set's files are written to their normal mirrored paths, not under `textures\pbr\<prefix>\`, and renaming its outputs goes through `output_name`, not the PGPatcher `rename` key. Files an earlier export wrote under `textures\pbr\` stay until the user deletes them; Tessalith deletes nothing. (superseded by FC11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC12, 2026-10-29)
- **AG92 Cancel an open.** `cancel_open` cancels the plan's token and bumps the generation, so its `Planned` (a cancelled plan still returns `Ok`) is dropped like any stale result (the generation rule `replan` and audit Task 12 use). `open_folder` clears the previous folder's state before planning, so "previous folder intact" is done by opening that folder again (controller ruling: deferring the clears would touch every path that reads `root`, `project` or `sets_doc`). Its analyses are normally still in the session cache (a cancelled plan never runs `retain_plan`), so it re-plans quickly. Selection, undo history and its export drawer start fresh. With no previous folder the window goes empty (`root`, project and sets doc cleared) and the centre reads "Opening cancelled". Going back is not itself cancellable. (superseded by FC12)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC12, 2026-10-29)
- **AG93 Cancel only while opening; Recent; hover.** The bar and Cancel show while a folder's first plan is in flight (`can_cancel_open`), not for a re-plan after an edit (that keeps the plain status text): a cancelled re-plan would leave the grid showing a plan that no longer matches the project. A second open during an open keeps the first open's previous folder as the one Cancel returns to. Cancel puts Recent back as it was before the open, so a cancelled folder new to Recent is dropped and one already listed keeps its place. Cancel's hover: "Stops opening and re-opens the previous folder (selection and undo history are reset)", or "Stops opening; no folder stays open" when there is none. (superseded by FC12)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CC1, 2026-10-18)
- **AG98 Drop files on a slot (item 17).** egui-winit 0.36 / winit 0.30.13 report a file drop with no position, and no mouse moves reach the window during an OLE drag, so the drop point is read with `GetCursorPos` (windows-sys feature `Win32_UI_WindowsAndMessaging` added to the existing dependency, no new crate) and placed with the viewport's inner rect; without viewport info (tests) the last pointer position stands in. The Textures view handles its drops in `ui::draw_with_scan`: a slot card under the point takes them, and anything else falls through to the old "open the folder" after the frame. The file must be an item of the open folder whose slot (`slot_of`) is that card's; another slot or none is refused with the reason. It then goes through `drop_choice` with Move, so a filled slot asks Replace / Cancel (the same prompt, now drawn from `ui::draw_with_scan` so a hidden Sets column still shows it) and a locked slot refuses. Share and Duplicate are never offered from a file drop, so nothing is copied. Refusals and "only the first file was used" show as a line under the slot cards. In 3D the cards are not drawn, so a drop opens the folder. (Slot cards are rows since CC1; the drop and its rules are unchanged.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CC12, 2026-10-18)
- **AG101 Subsurface mode selector (Task 12b, owner 2026-10-14).** The TruePBR card's Subsurface checkbox and the hidden "Foliage subsurface" checkbox become one selector, Subsurface: Off / Standard / Foliage. Standard writes `subsurface: true`, Foliage writes `subsurface_foliage: true`, Off writes neither; it never writes both and never a `false` (OnlyTrue stays). Why two kinds exist: the schema says `subsurface` "sets or clears the Rim_Lighting flag that enables subsurface scattering. true also puts <prefix>_s.dds in slot 8" and `subsurface_foliage` "true puts <prefix>_s.dds in slot 8 for foliage subsurface scattering" (no flag). Community Shaders `Common/PBR.hlsli:174-187` runs both under the PBR Subsurface flag and picks the term at compile time by `TREE_ANIM` (SLSF2 bit 29): other meshes get forward and back scatter, Tree_Anim meshes get `saturate(-NdotL) * (1 - Thickness)`. The owner's load order sets `subsurface_foliage: false` in 7,383 default-block entries and `true` in none. The "Subsurface settings" group (colour, opacity) shows for Standard and for Foliage (both read `SubsurfaceColor` and `Thickness`); hair and multilayer still rule both out. Both keys stored (a hand edit or the JSON editor) shows "Standard + Foliage (conflict)" with a pitfall and is never rewritten; choosing a mode replaces both keys. Off on a set with an `_s` stores `subsurface: false` so auto stays off (AJ8); on a set without one it removes both keys. A Foliage set with no `_s` blocks export like Standard does. (Superseded in part by CC12: the selector is the first row of the Subsurface group, which is titled "Subsurface".)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CA1, 2026-10-18)
- **AG112 Toasts.** A toast is a short notice at the bottom right that never takes input. It stays 4 s (8 s for a warning or error) and fades over its last 0.5 s. At most 3 are on screen; later ones queue and start their time when they appear. The pointer over a toast freezes its time. The clock is a parameter of `Messages::tick`, so the tests drive it. Raised by: Saved (a save that wrote a file), Export finished (N written, M skipped, K failed; a warning when K > 0), Scan finished (the scan's summary; a paused or cancelled scan repeats its note as a warning), Copied 3D view / Copy failed, Picker result ignored, and the Undid / Redid note. What needs a decision (Proceed anyway, Replace / Cancel, an external change, a blocking error) stays a dialog; no event changed its error class. (Anything that needs a decision no longer includes Proceed anyway: Review is a tab, CA1. Toasts draw above the open bottom panel.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AG133, 2026-10-15)
- **AG123 Glint defaults.** The glint starting values were checked against the observed p1-p99 band (`pgp-numeric-stats.csv`); one outside it moved to the observed median, rounded: `log_microfacet_density` 40 to 10 (observed 6-24, median 12.9, 10 is the second most common value, 12 uses; owner ruling), `microfacet_roughness` 0.015 to 1 (band 0.063-1.43, median 1, 31 of 58 uses), `density_randomization` 2 to 5 (band 2.128-300, median 5, 32 of 58 uses). `screen_space_scale` 1.5 stays (band 1-4). Fuzz has no default; `weight` is written only when set (superseded by AG133: fuzz now has defaults). The fixed `default` block (`roughness_scale` 1, `specular_level` 0.04, `displacement_scale` 1) already sits on the observed medians. An old project that switched glint on and never touched a value exports the new defaults.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CD1, 2026-10-18)
- **AG126 The header wraps (owner, 2026-10-14: the header compresses and the JSON field overlaps).** Cause: the header was one `horizontal` row with a `right_to_left` group (Export, Save, Messages, Export folder, JSON name, Editor…) laid into whatever width the left group left. egui does not stop a group at its neighbour, so in a narrow window the two groups drew over each other and the JSON field was squeezed to a few pixels. Ruling: of the two options in the brief, `horizontal_wrapped` is the simpler correct one, so nothing is hidden behind a "⋯" menu. The row is one left-to-right wrapped line in this order: Open folder…, Recent, Undo, Redo, the view switch, Mode, the column checkboxes, JSON: [name] Editor…, Export folder, Messages, Save, Export. Groups egui cannot wrap by themselves (Recent's disabled scope, Mode and its combo, the JSON label + field + Editor…) start a new line when they would not fit (`header::fit`). The JSON field is always 120 points; the export folder button is at most 280 points, the path cut with "…" (the hover and the accessible name keep it whole). Export is now at the end of the row, not pinned right. The Meshes header (the view switch) and the scan row were already wrapped and are covered by the same test: at 1440, 1024, 800 and 640 points no two header widgets intersect, none runs off the window, and every control is present. (Superseded in part by CD1 and CD2: the header is one row, and the Export to, Save and Export group is what wraps, below about 1340 pt.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI16, 2026-11-01)

Ruling: of the two options in the brief, `horizontal_wrapped` is the simpler correct one, so nothing is hidden behind a "⋯" menu. The row is one left-to-right wrapped line in this order: Open folder…, Recent, Undo, Redo, the view switch, Mode, the column checkboxes, JSON: [name] Editor…, Export folder, Messages, Save, Export. The JSON field is always 120 points; the export folder button is at most 280 points, the path cut with "…" (the hover and the accessible name keep it whole). The Meshes header (the view switch) and the scan row were already wrapped and are covered by the same test: at 1440, 1024, 800 and 640 points no two header widgets intersect, none runs off the window, and every control is present. The header is one row; the Export to, Save and Export group is what wraps, below about 1340 pt (Notes CD1, CD2).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AG40 File pickers (Task 6, item 7).** Every picker goes through `model::picker`: `Picker::request` runs the dialog on a spawned thread and the result lands in an inbox tagged with a `PickerPurpose` (OpenFolder, ExportFolder, ChooseMo2, ScanFolder, NvttPath, SaveReport, ShaderFolder). No executor: `pollster` and `futures` are not direct dependencies (pollster is in the lock only through other crates), so the thread calls the plain blocking `rfd::FileDialog`, which is equivalent. There is no HDRI picker in the code, so there is no Hdri purpose. The inbox is not the `WorkerMsg` channel: the app model, the meshes model and each 3D viewport own separate channels and are built standalone in tests, so each drains its own purposes from the shared inbox in its `poll` (`AppModel` takes OpenFolder, ExportFolder, NvttPath, SaveReport; `MeshesModel` ChooseMo2 and ScanFolder; `Viewport3d` ShaderFolder).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AG42 Stale and cancelled results.** A cancel does nothing and clears the busy state. ExportFolder and SaveReport are tagged with the folder open when the dialog started and are dropped if another folder is open when the answer arrives (the export folder triggers a replan; the report is of that folder's export). OpenFolder was tagged too until AG129; it now always opens. ChooseMo2, ScanFolder, NvttPath and ShaderFolder are app-wide and never stale: they set a scan source or a tool, whatever folder is open.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AG46 Textures filters (Task 7, item 8).** The four toggles stay. Beside them are combo boxes: Sub-type, Output format, Encoder and Set membership (Any, In a set, Not in a set). Sub-type, format and encoder list Any and only the values present in the folder (the encoder list keeps the order bc7e, rgbcx, ispc (BC6H), NVTT, uncompressed; ispc is the BC6H backend, so it is there when a BC6H texture is). A saved value the folder lacks is still listed, so a restored filter is never invisible. A texture with no job (failed, skipped DDS) matches no combo value. Everything combines with AND, search included. "In a set" is the existing rule: a set of more than one member.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AG111 Process-list seam (Task 14 carry-over).** The "PGPatcher is running" check is `MeshesModel::procs`, a fn pointer that defaults to the real process list. `meshes::no_procs` is the fake that sees nothing; the meshes tests build their scan models with it, so the host's PGPatcher cannot pause them, and `a_running_pgpatcher_pauses_the_scan` uses a fake that lists `pgpatcher.exe`. Not seamed: the NVTT probe reads the host's `PATH` and install, but only when a test calls `refresh_nvtt`, and those tests set `nvtt_path`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)

No executor: `pollster` and `futures` are not direct dependencies (pollster is in the lock only through other crates), so the thread calls the plain blocking `rfd::FileDialog`, which is equivalent. There is no HDRI picker in the code, so there is no Hdri purpose.
