ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AG111 Process-list seam (Task 14 carry-over).** The "PGPatcher is running" check is `MeshesModel::procs`, a fn pointer that defaults to the real process list. `meshes::no_procs` is the fake that sees nothing; the meshes tests build their scan models with it, so the host's PGPatcher cannot pause them, and `a_running_pgpatcher_pauses_the_scan` uses a fake that lists `pgpatcher.exe`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG90 Advanced skin sets get no PGPatcher JSON (owner, 2026-10-14: "no need for pgpatcher for advanced skin sets, future export json card").** This replaces final review C1. `SetView::pbr` is now true for a `pbr_albedo` diffuse only, so every PGPatcher reader leaves a `skin_albedo` set out: `is_json_set`, `json_entries` (writer, JSON editor, set preview), `export_blocks`, `export_asks`, prefix ownership, the PGPatcher `rename`, and the output-name rule. `shows_json_panel` is false for a skin set even if it holds old JSON settings, so there is no TruePBR card or prefix line; the badge reads "Advanced skin (no JSON)". The engine side is unchanged: `is_pbr_diffuse` still drives the PBR member follow-ons, and encoding is untouched. An old skin entry leaves the JSON at the next export that writes it, under the usual rules: Overwrite asks when the file was edited outside, and with no PBR set left nothing is written and the old file stays (the existing keep rule). The skin card has a disabled "Export JSON…" placeholder with the hover "Community Shaders Advanced Skin override export - spec pending". The set preview draws a skin set without a JSON entry, as a vanilla set (it would not be PGPatcher-patched in game either). Output location (owner confirmed, fix round 2): with no prefix ownership a skin set's files are written to their normal mirrored paths, not under `textures\pbr\<prefix>\`, and renaming its outputs goes through `output_name`, not the PGPatcher `rename` key. Files an earlier export wrote under `textures\pbr\` are listed in Review's stale outputs, with Delete while they still match what Tessalith wrote (Note FC11).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG112 Toasts.** A toast is a short notice at the bottom right that never takes input. It stays 4 s (8 s for a warning or error) and fades over its last 0.5 s. At most 3 are on screen; later ones queue and start their time when they appear. The pointer over a toast freezes its time. The clock is a parameter of `Messages::tick`, so the tests drive it. Raised by: Saved (a save that wrote a file), Export finished (N written, M skipped, K failed; a warning when K > 0), Scan finished (the scan's summary; a paused or cancelled scan repeats its note as a warning), Copied 3D view / Copy failed, Picker result ignored, and the Undid / Redid note. What needs a decision (Replace / Cancel, an external change, a blocking error) stays a dialog; undecided textures are answered in Review, a tab (Note CA1). No event changed its error class. Toasts draw above the open bottom panel.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - **Details** (collapsed by default): JSON_WINS, the preview size, the mesh line, the other maps' states and times, the compiling and plain shape lines, the emissive note, Timings, the Community Shaders features toggle, the P0 off list, the off line, coverage, frame stats, adapter and the renderer's notes. It scrolls past 220 points. Its open state is per panel, in egui memory, and saved as `[layout] details = ["set-3d-details", "meshes-3d-details"]`.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- Task 9 pin and compare: AG64-AG71.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG8 Item 10 pin:** one pin, held in memory. Dropped on Close, on a texture change and on folder open.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG14 Hover text (Task 2, item 24):** added to the Mode combo and its entries, the four Textures filter toggles, the preview channel buttons, Split, Fit, 1:1 and Live encode. Live encode's wording follows docs/gui.md; the others are new one-liners.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG17 Window and layout memory (Task 3, item 3, AG3).** Everything goes in `gui.toml` under `[layout]` (`settings/layout.rs`), at the existing `%APPDATA%\sdc\gui.toml`; eframe's `persistence` stays off. Saved: window inner size and outer position (points, with the scale they were taken at) and the maximised flag; the widths of the Textures, Sets, set 3D, Meshes tree and Advanced panels; the Textures | Meshes view; the Textures filter toggles (`needs-decision`, `warnings`, `failed`, `not-in-set`); the preview channel (`Channel::shader_id`) and layout (`side-by-side`, `split`); the Meshes view's and the set preview's 3D Look. Not saved: zoom, selection, the open folder, the preview's mip/heatmap/split position. Maximised keeps the last normal rectangle; minimised and fullscreen change nothing. A missing or old file gives 1400x900 and today's widths; the 2D | 3D switch (AB4) is unchanged and defaults to 2D. Each field is read on its own (`lenient`): a wrong type, a width under 200, an unknown filter or panel name, channel 99 or an unknown layout is dropped alone and never fails the file; look values are clamped to their ranges. The panel widths are read each frame from egui's memory (`chosen` for the two columns, `PanelState` for the rest) and put back before the first frame (`SdcApp::restore_ctx`); a change under 0.5 pt is noise.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG21 Layout writes and the title bar (Task 3 review carry-over, refines AG18, AG19).** The first frame's capture is the starting point: what it reads that `gui.toml` did not have (a window, a panel width) is taken into the settings without starting the 2 s wait, so a start where nothing changed writes nothing. Each frame compares a small `layout::Probe` (viewport rectangles and flags, the panel widths, the view, the filters, the channel, the layout, both looks) with the last one and captures only when it changed, so an idle frame allocates nothing. `fit_window` also keeps the top 32 px of the window on a monitor (with at least 64 px of width): a title bar off every monitor moves down or up onto the monitor that shows the most of the window. The 2D preview's settings panel (`preview-settings`) joins the saved widths; it is fixed at 280 pt today, so its saved width changes nothing yet. The undo note's 5 s (AG15) asks for a frame when it runs out (`undo_note_left`), so an idle window drops it on time.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG54 Texel readout (Task 8, item 1).** A strip under the viewports (in the notes panel), not a panel that follows the cursor. It reads the CPU images the preview holds: the source level of the result on show (the loaded source before any encode) and the result's decoded level, at the mip on show; never the GPU. A side it lacks shows "—". The texel is the one under the cursor at the shown mip; side by side, the cursor's fraction of its pane is used for both panes, and in Split both sides share the one rect, so both always read one texel. The strip is laid out before the viewports, so it shows what the last frame measured and asks for a repaint when that changed. It keeps a fixed five-line height with each line cut rather than wrapped, so the viewports never move as the pointer enters them.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG57 Lens.** "Lens" toggle and the `L` key (Preview area). A 160-point circle at 8× the view's zoom, centred on the centre of the texel under the cursor (the crosshair marks it). Off the picture the texel clamps to the edge; tiled, it wraps. The preview is a wgpu paint callback, not an egui texture, so the lens is a second draw of the pane's slot (source, encoded or heatmap) with its own uniform block and a nearest-sampling bind group created with the slot; the shader keeps only the inscribed circle. No texture is uploaded for it. It is drawn in both panes; in Split each side of the divider shows its own side.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG64 Pin (item 10, plan AG8).** "Pin this result" in the expanded 2D toolbar and the key `P` (`Area::Preview`, action `pin`, on only while `can_pin`). The pin holds the `Arc` of the finished encode, so its decoded images and the GPU upload are shared with nothing re-encoded and nothing copied. The GPU texture for the pin (`Slot::Pinned`) exists only while the pin is compared.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG65 Only a finished encode can be pinned.** `can_pin` needs `EncodedState::Done` and a job on the texture. A stale or encoding result does not match the settings now on show, so its summary would be false.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG66 One pin; when it goes.** Pinning again replaces the pin (the old images are released). `PreviewModel::clear` drops it, so closing the preview, a texture change and a folder open all drop it; a settings change, a re-plan with the same texture, and a size-limit change keep it. Pin ids never repeat within a session (`next_pin` survives `clear`). *Unpin* drops it too.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG67 Compare toggle and summaries.** "Compare: Source | Pinned" shows while a pin is held; pinning turns Pinned on; Pinned needs a pin. Pinned puts the pin on the left of Side by side and Split and the current encode on the right, each with a caption: `Pinned: <summary> · PSNR x dB vs source` and `Current: <summary> · PSNR x dB vs source`. The summary is format id, colour tag, backend, effort, alpha policy, mip levels and filter; the PSNR is the minimum-mip PSNR shown today, or a dash.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG68 Readout and lens while comparing.** The readout adds `Pinned:` (the pinned encode's texel at the mip on view) and `|Δ| pinned vs encoded`; the strip grows from five to seven lines for as long as the pin is compared, so it does not move while pointing. The lens draws whatever each side draws, so it shows both.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG69 Heatmap vs pin.** With the pin compared, the heatmap is |current − pinned| per channel on the same ramp and full scale, labelled "Heatmap |Δ| Current vs Pinned" (otherwise "Source vs Encoded"). It runs on the same off-thread job; the key gains the pin id, so a new pin or the toggle makes a new job. Like a mip change (AG62), a heatmap made for the other comparison is not shown for this one.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG70 Mip mismatch.** If the pinned encode lacks the mip on view, the left side draws "— not in the pinned encode", the readout's pinned lines read "—", and the heatmap has no image (the plain image shows).

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AG71 Memory.** The heatmap key holds only the pin id; the job gets the images, and unpinning or pinning again clears the heatmap job state. The summary uses the settings panel words (its format label, the encoder label, the schema names of effort, alpha policy and mips). While the encode on show is not finished the Current caption says "previous result, settings changed". Dropping the pin releases the CPU images (when no one else holds the `Arc`) and `app.rs` clears `Slot::Pinned`. The test checks the CPU side through a `Weak`; the GPU slot needs a device.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **AG75 Debug views.** The selector offers Lit and UV checker only. Lighting.hlsl (CS AIO 2026-10-03) has no debug-view define (only `LLFDEBUG`, a light-count view), and render3d's API has no output switch, so Albedo, Normals, Roughness, Metallic and AO are left out rather than faked; the NIF request lists the API they need. UV checker: a generated 256 px grey 8×8 checker goes over both variants of every shape's slot 0 with `Renderer::replace_texture` after each load, swap and edit (not through the load resolver: the A/B replay would swap the real texture back). Back to Lit reloads the content with its swaps and edits replayed. The view is not saved.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ5, 2026-11-02)
- **AG97 Open in Textures (item 9).** Every texture path the Meshes view lists (the shape cards' slots and the "Users of" title) gets "Open in Textures" when `item_for_game_path` finds it in the open folder. `SdcApp::route_3d` looks the paths up again only when they or the items change, and runs the click as the 3D panel's "Show in Textures" does (switch view, `palette_pick`: select and scroll into view). The 3D panel's missing-texture button uses the same name (it was "Show in Textures" until Task 13). The Plugin file references window is not covered: its Missing paths are in no mod by definition, and its Unused lines are listing text.

## Note AI — Meshes shape detail (2026-10-14)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note AU6, 2026-10-15)
- **AI2 Shape numbers count shapes from 0 (owner).** The `#N` is the index in the shape list the GUI holds (the scan stores shapes only, ordered by NIF block), so bones and other non-shapes are skipped. The NIF block shows in the hover text. pgpsim has no shape index of its own (it matches by `nif_filter` on the path), so there was no order to match. Whether PGPatcher's texture-set order is block order or a tree walk is not confirmed; see `.superpowers/sdd/meshes/nif-request-shape-index.md`. **Superseded by AU6:** the card number is PGPatcher's 3D index.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **AI4 SLSF1/SLSF2 expanders.** Collapsed headers "SLSF1 0x…" and "SLSF2 0x…"; open lists the set bits by name, lowest first, `Name (bit)`, and an unknown bit as `bit N`. The nif crate exposes only the few bits it uses, so the names are GUI-side tables (`SLSF1_NAMES`, `SLSF2_NAMES`, the nif.xml names). SLSF2 bit 23 reads "Unused01 (TruePBR)". Open state is egui memory, per shape.

## Note AJ — Coat settings under Multilayer (2026-10-14)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CC9, 2026-10-18)
- **AJ1. The coat settings are a group under the Multilayer (coat) checkbox, not in Advanced.** The group holds coat colour, coat specular level, coat roughness, coat strength, coat diffuse, coat normal, coat parallax and inner UV scale, in schema key order. It shows only while Multilayer is on. `coat_color` and `inner_uv_scale` were the only two marked advanced; the schema flag is now off, and Multilayer is no longer an Advanced group. (Superseded in part by CC9 and CC13: the group is titled "Coat" and is always listed, reading "off" while Multilayer is off.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AJ1. The coat settings are a group under the Multilayer (coat) checkbox, not in Advanced.** The group holds coat colour, coat specular level, coat roughness, coat strength, coat diffuse, coat normal, coat parallax and inner UV scale, in schema key order. It is titled "Coat" and always listed, reading "off" while Multilayer is off (Notes CC9, CC13). `coat_color` and `inner_uv_scale` were the only two marked advanced; the schema flag is now off, and Multilayer is no longer an Advanced group.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)

Multilayer off keeps the values.** The group is hidden; the stored coat values stay in `sdc-project.toml` and the writer skips them (its existing rule: a group key is written only while its feature is live). A note under the checkbox reads "N coat values kept, unused while Multilayer is off".

## Note AM — MO2 button and preset creator (2026-10-15)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CE11 and Note FB7, 2026-10-18)
- **AM11 The window.** "Presets…" in the Textures header and the palette action `presets` open it; it also draws in the Meshes view. It is a resizable `egui::Window`, and its rectangle is kept in `gui.toml` as `[layout] presets_window`, like the 3D settings window (AG132). A built-in shows read-only, with Duplicate. The creator's button is "Save preset", since the header has its own Save. A collapsible TOML preview shows exactly the text Save writes. Superseded in part by CE11: it opens below the header and over the centre column, and cannot be taller than the space under the header.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC1, 2026-10-29)
- **AM14 A deleted preset named in `sdc.toml`.** Before this fix, a plan whose `sdc.toml` named a preset that was gone failed outright: an error dialog ("Planning failed: unknown preset 'x'") and no plan. Now the GUI leaves that `preset` out of the plan's inline config (the plan uses the user config's preset, else the Mode's default) and raises the banner "preset <id> not found: using <fallback>". `sdc.toml` keeps the name, so a restored file applies again; picking a preset in the header clears the banner. The check reads the presets folder only for an id that is not built in. The CLI is unchanged: there an unknown preset is still an error. (superseded by FC1)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI16, 2026-11-01)
- **AM12 The header's Preset menu (owner design §2 "appears in the Mode/preset picker").** The GUI had no preset picker (gui.md said so). A **Preset** combo after Mode lists "default (<the Mode's quality preset>)" and every selectable preset, built-in or from the presets folder, whose chain's target is the Mode's or unset; it reads the creator's registry, so a saved preset is listed at once. A pick writes `preset = "<id>"` to `sdc.toml` (default removes it) as one undo step and re-plans. A preset of another target is not offered; one already in `sdc.toml` still shows as the selection.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AM5 Rows and subheaders.** Groups: Colour (category colour), Normal (category normal), Data (grayscale and mixed/packed), Special (HDR and `complex_grass`), each in `Subtype::ALL` order. A row's title is the sub-type and its resolved format. Compression offers `fields::encoding_options` (the `allowed_encodings` pairs, format and tag together), the encoder (only backends that fit the format: Built-in, and NVTT for BC7 and BC6H) and effort; a format pick drops the preset's own backend for that sub-type when it cannot write the new format. Resize & mips: resize, levels, filter (only filters `filter_option_lock` accepts) and coverage, shown for the cutout policies only. Alpha (policy, and threshold for cutout) shows where the base's format keeps an alpha channel. Locks are `fields::field_lock`. Category, content and the sub-type's own rules are never offered.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AM5 Rows and subheaders.** Groups: Colour (category colour), Normal (category normal), Data (grayscale and mixed/packed), Special (HDR and `complex_grass`), each in `Subtype::ALL` order. A row's title is the sub-type and its resolved format. Compression offers `fields::encoding_options` (the `allowed_encodings` pairs, format and tag together), the encoder (only backends that fit the format) and effort; a format pick drops the preset's own backend for that sub-type when it cannot write the new format. Resize & mips: resize, levels, filter (only filters `filter_option_lock` accepts) and coverage, shown for the cutout policies only. Alpha (policy, and threshold for cutout) shows where the base's format keeps an alpha channel. Locks are `fields::field_lock`. Category, content and the sub-type's own rules are never offered.

## Note AN — PGPatcher match and lock (2026-10-15)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC9, 2026-10-29)
- **AN6 What a lock does.** A locked slot's card shows a big red ✕ over the thumbnail, with no caption (owner, 2026-10-15). The card has one hover, in place of the thumbnail's path: "PGPatcher lock (lock_<slot>): the original texture in this spot is used", then, with a scan, the slot's textures in the meshes that use the set's diffuse. It is read on a worker per (diffuse, slot) like the Used-by list, shows "…" until it lands, and a finished scan clears it so it is read again; a read that panics keeps its error as the answer (review M1). A lock key on a set that writes no JSON shows "lock stored, not written (no PGPatcher entry)" instead of the cross (review M2). Its pill member shows a small red ✕. Export still encodes the file, since other sets may use it. PGPatcher applies `slotN` after the locks, so the writer drops a slot path sdc derives itself (a borrowed fill, or a texture not at the set's own path) for a locked slot. An explicit slot command (`slotN` in the settings) is still written and raises the pitfall "slotN overrides the lock (PGPatcher applies slotN last)". The brief's optional "drop the slotN" offer is not built: the slot command's own From this set does it. (superseded by FC9)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI11, 2026-11-01)
- **AN3 Right-click Lock is PGPatcher's slot lock.** The slot menu (card and pill member) gains "Keep original texture (PGPatcher lock)", or "Stop keeping original texture (PGPatcher lock)" when on. It toggles the set's `lock_<slot>` key: on stores `true`, off removes the key (`false` is the default, so it is never stored). It is one `set_json_value` call, so one undo step, and the same key the Texture locks group shows. It is offered only on sets that write a PGPatcher entry (`is_json_set`: a PBR set under CS); elsewhere it is disabled with the reason.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI11, 2026-11-01)
- **AN4 The sdc slot lock is "Pin slot (sdc)".** F3a's lock (`lock = ["slotN"]` in sdc-project.toml, which refuses moves into the slot) is unchanged. Its menu entry is renamed "Pin slot (sdc)" / "Unpin slot (sdc)"; the card and member hovers and the Slot commands dialog ("Pin slot N", "Pinned: …") say pin too.

## Note AO — Input name editor (2026-10-15)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note BH1, 2026-10-16)
- **AO5 Width and the set path.** The box is at least 160 pt with its locked part (its free part at least 56 pt); the free part grows with its text and ghost, up to the room left in the row and 480 pt. The set's path and the box share the header row where the width allows: the path takes what the box leaves, and when that is less than its full width it is shortened with … (the full path on hover) and set in the body size, so the row is no taller than the box; below 40 pt the box starts the next row of the wrapped header (AG126). At 1024×768 in 3D the frame is 499 pt of a 679 pt centre (base 487–489); `set_preview_layout` now asserts 72 %.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FI17, 2026-11-01)
- **AO3 The path lock.** Stops are the start of the text and each separator (`separator_stops`). Lock n keeps everything up to the n-th separator (`lock_cut`); 0 keeps nothing, so the whole path can be edited. The locked part is a dimmed label before the box, the box edits only the rest, Tab completes against the whole path, and the ▾ list shows only paths that start with the locked part. The rail is a thin strip along the box's lower edge: a tick per stop and a handle; a click or drag snaps to the nearest stop. Ctrl+Left/Right move it while the box has focus. It defaults to the last separator of the current name (the folder is locked, the file name is free). The lock is held per set in egui memory for the session and never written to the project. The rail is hidden when the text has no separator.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI17, 2026-11-01)
- **AO4 The live list, and ▾ for browsing (owner via coordinator, 2026-10-15: "a text input with dropdown").** While the box has focus and its text has changed, a list of up to 12 known paths shows right under it, in a foreground `egui::Area` that takes no focus (`live_rows`): the paths that start with the text (case-insensitive, `\` read as `/`; the top one is the ghost's, drawn bold as the best match), or, when none does, the paths under the lock that contain every word of the free part. PGPatcher outputs are never in it (AO8). No row is highlighted until Up or Down moves the highlight, so Enter commits the highlighted row, else the typed text. A click on a row commits that row: the row under a pointer press is recorded before the box sees the press, and the focus loss the press causes commits it instead of the typed text. The first Esc closes the list and keeps the text and the focus (the box's event filter holds Esc while the list shows); a second Esc reverts. A commit or a focus loss closes it. **▾** stays as "show all": the 200-row list with its own filter box and "type more to narrow", under the lock (clicking it ends the box's edit, AO1). Click outside, Esc or a pick closes it (AG125).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI17, 2026-11-01)
- **AO5 Width and the set path.** The box is at least 160 pt with its locked part (its free part at least 56 pt); the free part grows with its text and ghost, up to the room left in the row and 480 pt. The set header shows the box only, with no path heading beside it; with too little room left in the row the box starts the next line (Note BH1). At 1024×768 in 3D the frame is 499 pt of a 679 pt centre (base 487–489); `set_preview_layout` now asserts 72 %.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FI17, 2026-11-01)
  - A pasted path that starts with the locked part (case-insensitive, `\` read as `/`) loses that part, so it is not doubled after the lock.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI17, 2026-11-01)

## Note AP — Preset suffix branches (2026-10-15)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note AP10, 2026-10-15)
- **AP2 A branch is one rule, after the other rules (amended by AP10).** A branch with overrides is a rule `match = { suffix = "<canonical>", subtypes = ["<id>"] }` (built-in) or `match = { glob = "*_<alias>.*", subtypes = ["<id>"] }` (alias, since `match.suffix` sees the canonical suffix), with the fields in `overrides`. An emptied branch's rule is removed, and an empty `rules` with it. A source's matching rules layer in file order (AP10), so a new built-in suffix branch is inserted after the hand-written rules and before the alias branches, and a new alias branch goes last. A branch therefore changes only the fields it sets: a duplicated cs-compact's opaque-colour rule (bc1, resize none) still gives `_d` files BC1 under a `_d` resize branch, and an alias branch adds to its canonical suffix's branch (its files carry the canonical suffix too). Rules of the base presets are an earlier source and apply underneath. Rules sit at file reach before `[files]` and `--set`, so those still win (end-to-end tests). A file saved before AP10 with its branches first is read the same way; only new branches are placed by this order.

## Note AR — Crash 2026-10-15 (staging buffer)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB5, 2026-10-29)
- **AR6 Unsaved edits survive a crash.** Before this change, the panic hook only logged, and unsaved `sdc.toml` / `sdc-project.toml` edits were lost. At the end of each frame, `SdcApp::ui` stores the dirty documents' text (`AppModel::recovery_snapshot`). The panic hook writes each one to `<file>.recovery` beside the original, and the original is left alone. The user restores by hand, so there is no restore UI.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB5, 2026-10-29)
- **AR7 Recovery files: main thread only, banner, removed on save** (coordinator review). Only a panic on the main thread writes recovery files. A worker's panic is caught and shown as "internal error in …" while the app keeps running, so it writes none. A successful save of a document deletes its `.recovery`. When a folder is opened, or the banner is rebuilt, a `.recovery` newer than its document, or one whose document is missing, adds the banner line "Unsaved edits from a crash were kept in <file>.recovery: rename it over <file> to restore, or delete it." A `.recovery` older than its document is ignored. `crash_log::install_in(dir)` takes the log folder, so the test can run the real hook without touching `%LOCALAPPDATA%`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ3, 2026-11-02)
- **AR5 What the app now does.** `crash_log::watch_device` sets wgpu's device-lost callback. The callback writes "wgpu device lost (<reason>, <backend>): <message>" to the run's crash log and a `device_lost` event to the timing log, so the next crash names the reason and the backend. wgpu 30 calls the callback only for Unknown (a driver loss) and Destroyed, never when the device is dropped (`impl Drop for Device`), so a clean exit writes nothing. egui-wgpu cannot recover a lost device and eframe cannot recreate one, so the panic itself still happens.

## Note AT — PGPatcher complex material JSON and the Vanilla complex `_m` (plan rulings, 2026-10-15) (Note AM on nif/main; renumbered at the merge into main)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC11 and Note BV3, 2026-10-29)
- **AT3 File and text.** The file is `<job output>.json` under the export root, so `_em`, renames and packed cpm `_m` follow. (superseded by FC11)
  - The text is `$schema` (the schema `$id`) plus the set, well-typed keys, every number a float, serde_json pretty with a final newline.
  - It is validated against the vendored `complex_material.schema.json` (upstream `0176567`, identical at 2.2.0) by `pgp::validate::validate_cm`.
  - Written after a clean export only, on every target; the JSON is written only when the export had no failures. A missing `.dds`, a path clash or an output outside the export root gets a note.
  - The guard is stricter than the CS PBR and PGPatcher exports, which share `pbr_export.rs` (not changed here): the CM export replaces an existing JSON only when its hash matches `[project] cm_json_sha256`. An existing file with no recorded hash is not adopted, even if identical, and an unreadable existing file is kept with a note. Undo keeps the recorded hash. (superseded by BV3, 2026-10-17: a byte-identical file with no record is adopted and recorded)
  - Untick or orphan: the file is left in place, with a note. It is never deleted.
  - Duplicate set ids in `prepare` and `targets`: the first entry decides.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - Untick or orphan: the file is a stale row in Review, with Delete while it still has its recorded hash (Note FC11).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AT1 Vanilla keeps a complex `_m` (D16).** The `(Vanilla, complex_material) → envmask` fallback and the "_m under vanilla" suggestion are gone.
  - Vanilla uses the CS rule: a Note L alpha (minimum below 251) gives `complex_material` (High, BC7 UNORM with alpha, BC3 option, Note AH unchanged), and anything else gives `envmask` (BC4, no alpha).
  - Side effects: a Vanilla set with a complex `_m` auto-picks the Complex Material shader. A varying-alpha `_m` that used to be skipped (`LOSS-ALPHA-DATA`) or written as BC4 is now BC7, twice the size, and its `profile_sha256` changes once, so `if-changed` re-encodes it. The engine version stays 0.7.0.
  - The vanilla shader ignores the alpha without ENB. The owner accepted this.
  - Two golden tests changed (both owner-approved). `vanilla_fallbacks_are_written_with_allow_cfg_unavailable` drops `iron_m`: three fallbacks. `vanilla_g_is_glow_and_m_with_alpha_is_envmask_with_loss` now expects BC7 `complex_material` plus `VAL-M-GRAYSCALE` (its RGB is grey); the test name predates AT. It also dropped its `skip_code(None)` assert, because that helper panics when there is no skip event.

## Note AU — GUI suggestions, NIF side (plan rulings, 2026-10-15) (Note AN on nif/main; renumbered at the merge into main)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GE8, 2026-10-29)
- **AU2 pgpsim `alphaprop_*`.** The four PGPatcher 2.2.0 keys on top of the 1.2.0 simulator (owner D2; a full 2.2 audit is a later plan). Integers go through `uint_in` (2.2 `unsignedFromJSON`): an out-of-range, negative or fractional number is ignored with a range warning; a non-numeric value (a string or a bool) is ignored silently; neither is ever a Skip. Delete beats create; create starts from nifly's 4844/128.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **AU5 UV checker by slot (amends AG75).** `Renderer::override_slot(0, …)`, kept across loads; Lit no longer reloads. The override coexists with the missing-texture fill (the override wins on screen; clearing it restores the fill, AU3). A swap or A/B flip under the override updates the entry and `Bound` but not the screen (R9); a kind mismatch warns "slot N override…" (G8).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **AU1 SLSF names.** `nif::material::{SLSF1_NAMES, SLSF2_NAMES}` (moved verbatim; bit 8 `Receive_Shadows`, SLSF2 bit 23 `Unused01 (TruePBR)`). `model::meshes` re-exports them.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **AU3 Missing-texture fill (replaces AG76's swap).** `Renderer::set_missing_texture`: colour registers only, t0 and a cube t4 (owner D3); normal, glow, specular and RMAOS stay neutral, and `Bound` is not changed, so a missing data map no longer tints. RFAOS guesses never fill. Each texture entry carries its own missing provenance (`TexEntry.missing`), so the fill follows a slot across material edits and across an override being set and cleared: a slot that was missing before the override is filled again when the override clears, and a slot that was real is never reported missing.

## Note AV — Audit 2026-10-15 fixes

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC13, 2026-10-29)
- **AV2 Thumbnail size (item 2, engine part).** `session::THUMB_MAX` 256 → 128 (as `rnm_preview::THUMB_MAX`): a quarter of the memory, 64 KiB per file, ~640 MiB of thumbnails for a 10k folder instead of ~2.5 GiB. The default 96 pt tile is drawn from ≥ its pixel size at 1x; at 1.5x and above, and for tiles over 128 pt, the thumbnail is upscaled (slightly soft). 192 would keep 128 pt tiles sharp on HiDPI for 2.25× the memory; memory was the audit's point. Nothing else reads the thumbnail (the grid only); the GUI's texture eviction is a separate GUI item. (superseded by FC13)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AV-S1 patch-header write errors (item 10).** A failed write in a `patch-header` batch prints `<file>: write failed: <e>`, sets exit 1 and carries on with the next file; it is no longer Fatal (exit 3). The file is not reported patched.

Set edits and Undo stay enabled while an export runs; if the doc was dirty, the hash is kept in memory and the note "hash recorded; written with the next Save" is shown (once per export), so Save/Discard still decide about the user's edits. A stale on-disk hash only makes the next export ask before overwriting its own JSON. (superseded by FC11)

## Note BN — Trace-debug 2026-10-16 fixes: GUI documents

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC12, 2026-10-29)
- **BN1 A wrongly blamed `sdc.toml` gets its edits back (#9).** When the engine rejects the config, the first failure is still blamed on `sdc.toml` tentatively (spec §7.4), but `ProjectDoc::set_error` now stashes the document and its dirty flag. When the plan without `sdc.toml` fails too, the culprit is elsewhere (the user config or a user preset; `check_chain` messages name no path, so the path test cannot tell), and `unblame()` puts the stashed document back, unsaved edits included, instead of reloading from disk. `reload` clears the stash. Undo history is still cleared on the first failure *(call: the read-only window cannot take edits, and keeping history across it needs more than this fix; owner may revisit)*. A plan that lands settles the blame (`apply_plan` clears `ignored_by_plan`), so a later user config or preset failure can never restore a document the engine rejected (review I1). While the blame is tentative, the stashed edits count as unsaved in `AppModel::is_dirty` and the recovery snapshot, so Reload, close and open still ask (review M1). Tests: `a_rejected_user_config_keeps_unsaved_sdc_toml_edits`, `a_settled_blame_is_not_undone_by_a_later_user_config_error`, `stashed_edits_count_as_unsaved_while_the_blame_is_tentative`. (superseded by FC12)

## Note BQ — Trace-debug minor fixes: engine and CLI (controller, 2026-10-16)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note BQ15, 2026-10-16)
- **BQ14 Not fixed here.** #28 (loose walk hides I/O errors): owner NIF team, and the fix changes `vfs::loose::walk`'s return type, whose callers are in modscan; left for them (BE5's open gap stands). #30 (OpenEXR premultiplied alpha): needs an owner ruling, since some writers store straight alpha in EXR; unchanged.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC10, 2026-10-29)
- **BQ15 OpenEXR alpha is un-premultiplied on load (#30; owner, 2026-10-16: follow the standard; EXR alpha is rare in practice, mostly height or colour without alpha).** Supersedes the #30 part of BQ14. `load::decode` divides RGB by A for an OpenEXR with alpha wherever A > 0, unclamped; A = 0 texels stay as stored. A straight-alpha EXR sets the per-file field `alpha.exr_straight = true` (`[files]`, `--set`, a preset), which the pipeline's load reads (`load_image_as`) to skip it; the field is left out of a profile's JSON while false, so profile hashes and goldens are unchanged (no golden uses EXR alpha). The pipeline load and the GUI encoded and source previews (`pipeline::prepare`) honour it. Plan-time analysis, plan thumbnails, packing sources, RNM layers and bakes load with the default (un-premultiplied). Alpha statistics do not change under the division, but colour statistics do: the grey test has an absolute tolerance, so a near-grey straight EXR with partial alpha may be planned as colour (the safe side) while the encode reads the stored values (review M1). The field has no GUI row; it is set in a `[files]` entry by hand. (superseded by FC10)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BQ5 rfaos and skin_rfaos together (#20).** A config or preset whose `[subtypes]` holds both `rfaos` (the old id) and `skin_rfaos` is refused (`MigrateError::DuplicateSubtypeAlias`), not merged: which table should win is the author's call.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BQ3 NVTT working folder (#17).** nvcompress runs with the level's temp folder as its working folder and gets bare `l<N>.in.dds` / `l<N>.out.dds` names, because it reads argv in the ANSI code page. Its error text no longer names the folder. The test fake mangles non-Latin-1 argument characters to `?` like the real tool.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BQ7 BC1 transparency on every level (#23).** patch-header decodes every stored BC1 level, not only mip 0, before refusing 1-bit alpha (S7).

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BQ10 Stale sidecar removed (#27).** Under Note F / Y2: an encode without `--sidecar` removes the output's existing `.dds.json` when it reads as sdc's record for that output (a JSON object whose `output` is the output's key and that has `profile_sha256`); any other file of that name is not sdc's and stays (the complex-material export test keeps such a stub). A removal error other than NotFound fails the file at Write and removes the output. Owner, 2026-10-16 (review M2): superseded: a removal error other than NotFound is a warning, OUT-SIDECAR-KEPT, and the new DDS stays (the file counts as converted). An up-to-date output keeps its sidecar, which still matches.

## Note BR — Trace-debug minor fixes: GUI model

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note BV7, 2026-10-17)
- **BR6 #43 PGPatcher JSON hash per file name.** New `[project] pgp_json_sha256` table: path under the export root → hash sdc last wrote there, one entry per name the JSON has had. An entry whose file is gone is dropped at the next record. `last_json_sha256` is still written (the current name's hash, for older builds) and is the record read when the table is absent (older projects). An export with a recorded old-name file still on disk notes it: "<old>: the PGPatcher JSON is now <new>; the old file was left in place and PGPatcher still reads it". The file is kept (AG90's keep rule). Renaming back finds sdc's own file, so there is no "edited outside" question. Undo keeps the table like the other hash records; they are put back in export order, so a restore that changes nothing else leaves the document clean. (amended by BV7, 2026-10-17: entries are keyed by absolute lower-case path, and an entry whose file is gone is kept)

## Note BS — Trace-debug minor fixes: GUI UI and preview (Claude, 2026-10-16)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FC8, 2026-10-29)
- **BS7 The header Save waits for a focused field (#54).** As Ctrl+S (AG23): clicking **Save** while a text field has focus saves nothing and says "Save: leave the text field first" in the status line and the Messages log *(call: logged too, because the focus-loss commit's plan replaces the status line at once)*. egui surrenders the field's focus on the release (BN3), after the header has drawn, so the field still has focus when the click lands; it commits on the next frame. Test: `header_save_click_with_focused_field_does_not_save_stale_value` (press and release in separate frames). (superseded by FC8)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FC8, 2026-10-29)
- **BS14 The header Export waits for a focused field (#54 follow-up).** Export saves first (`start_export`), so it follows BS7: a click while a text field has focus does nothing and says "Export: leave the text field first" in the status line and the log; the field commits on the focus loss and the next click exports. Save and Export share `header::field_focused`. The command-palette Export is not guarded: the palette's own search box has the focus when it runs, and a palette command never runs while another field is being edited. (superseded by FC8)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BS3 Worker panics carry their job id (#26).** As AF3 does for the NVTT probe, the preview (source, encode, DDS), heatmap and RNM thumbnail workers catch their own panic, send their id-tagged failure (`worker::or_send`), then unwind on so `spawn_guarded` still raises the error dialog. A stale job's panic is dropped by the id check like any stale result; the per-kind `*_panicked` handlers are gone. The failure text is the panic message, as before.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BS3 Worker panics carry their job id (#26).** The preview (source, encode, DDS), heatmap and RNM thumbnail workers catch their own panic, send their id-tagged failure (`worker::or_send`), then unwind on so `spawn_guarded` still raises the error dialog. A stale job's panic is dropped by the id check like any stale result; the per-kind `*_panicked` handlers are gone. The failure text is the panic message, as before.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **BS5 A folder change resets the Sets column modal and Slot commands (#52).** `AppModel::folder_epoch` is bumped in `open_folder`; when it differs from `ui::State::folder_epoch` (an `Option`: a fresh State adopts the epoch on its first frame), the UI resets all of `DropState` (the pending modal, the slot note and the flash) and `SlotCmdState` *(call: the whole state, not only `pending`, since the slot note and flash name a set id too)*.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **BS8 Reordering a set to its own place is no edit (#55).** `reorder_set` returns whether it moved anything; Move down on the last set records no step, leaves `sdc-project.toml` clean and does not flash.

## Note BV — Pre-v2 fix batch (owner rulings and audit fixes, 2026-10-17)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB1, 2026-10-29)
- **BV6 The export folder is per project (owner ruling, 2026-10-17).** Picking an export folder saves it as `[project] export_folder` in the open project's `sdc-project.toml` (GUI-only state, kept across Undo like the hash records; saved at once, or with the next Save while the file has unsaved edits, BD2). `export_root()` is the project's value, else `gui.toml`'s `export_folder` (what older versions saved for every project, now only a default that picking no longer changes), else `<root>/sdc-out`. With no usable `sdc-project.toml` (none open, or one that does not parse) a pick goes to `gui.toml` as before. Opening another project no longer sends its outputs into this project's folder (audit 08c finding 5). The CLI is unaffected (`-o` names the folder).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CA1 and Note FC5, 2026-10-18)
- **BV10 Export dialogs (audit 08f).** An export queued behind a plan never starts over an open dialog: it is dropped with "Export not started: answer the open dialog, then click Export again", and answering Open-unsaved while an export runs asks to stop it first (`ExportRunning`), as Open does (08f-1). "Proceed anyway" waits for a plan in flight and runs the guards again, like the other answers (08f-2). The end-of-export OverwriteJson question and error dialog never replace an open dialog: the JSON is kept with a note, and the error stays in the drawer and Messages (an unanswered `ExportRunning` is not in the way; it is dropped when the export ends) (08f-6). A refused context-menu pick says why in the status line and Messages (08f-8). An export-thread panic sets one error dialog: `export_ended`'s, else the panic arm's own when the user was leaving, which keeps BI6 (08f-9). (Superseded in part by CA1: ProceedAnyway, ExportBlocked and ExportWarnings are not dialogs. 08f-1 carries over to Review's queued export, CA13, and 08f-2 to `export_from_review`, CA3 and CA7. The drawer is the bottom panel's Files tab, CA16.) (superseded by FC5)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BV3 One rule for replacing an existing file (audit 08a-2).** `engine::dds::may_replace(path, new, ours)`: absent → write; unreadable → kept (never treated as absent); bytes equal to `new` → write, and the writer records it as its own; `ours(bytes)` (the writer's record says it wrote them) → write; anything else → kept, with a note. The PGPatcher, CS PBR and complex-material JSON, the vanilla borrowed-slot copies, Patch header and `--sidecar` all use it. Changes: an unreadable PGPatcher or CS PBR JSON is no longer written over; a byte-identical complex-material JSON with no record is now adopted and recorded (supersedes Note AT's "not adopted, even if identical"); a foreign PGPatcher JSON still asks (P21). `ours` per writer: the JSON files compare the recorded SHA-256; a borrowed-slot copy compares its record, new in `[project] copy_sha256` (absolute lower-case path → hash; a copy now replaces only a file it wrote, so it follows its owner on later exports, and goes through `write_atomic`; P4's "not tracked by the manifest" still holds); Patch header accepts a file that ends with this DDS's payload (an earlier patched copy under any header, S7); `--sidecar` accepts a file that reads as sdc's record for this output (BQ10's test). DDS outputs keep their manifest check in `run`. *shortcut:* a copy record waits for the next Save when `sdc-project.toml` has unsaved edits (BD2); discarding them loses the record, and that copy is then kept until deleted by hand.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BV4 A sidecar write failure keeps the DDS (audit 08d-5).** The new DDS has already replaced the old output when the `--sidecar` write fails, so the failure is an `OUT-SIDECAR-KEPT` warning and the DDS stays (it counts as converted), as for an unremovable stale sidecar (BQ10). A foreign `.dds.json` kept by BV3 is the same warning. The next `if-changed --sidecar` run writes the sidecar from the manifest record: `sidecar_present` now rewrites a missing sidecar or sdc's sidecar of an earlier output (one whose JSON is not this record but has the same `output` and a `profile_sha256`), and leaves a file sdc did not write alone (review m10).

## Note BY — Trace-debug wave 2 fixes (main team, 2026-10-17)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB5, 2026-10-29)
- **BY3 Only a user's save removes a crash `.recovery` file (#3; amends AR7).** Save, and Overwrite on the changed-on-disk question, delete the saved document's `.recovery` file. The saves Tessalith makes by itself (an export folder, BV6; a JSON hash record, BD2) keep it: they do not hold the recovered edits. The banner line still goes once the document is newer than the recovery file. docs/gui.md says so.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CW1, 2026-10-22)
- **BY6 A plan while the scan store is unsettled keeps the mesh evidence (#6; extends BT6).** `AppModel::scan_store_unsettled` is set when a Meshes scan starts and cleared only by a completed scan. While it is set, the evidence read returns the votes, alpha, Tree_Anim and GRAS already held, and "Used by meshes" starts no read (a list already read stays; none read means hidden). *shortcut:* a restart after a cancel starts settled and reads the empty store; a store-level marker would cover it (owner call). Amended by Note CW (CW1, CW2).

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BY1 A `pbr_emissive` switched to BC7 drops an unpinned BC6H-only backend (#1; amends S3).** When the hdr-source fact turns BC6H into BC7, an explicit backend is kept only if it can write BC7 or was pinned (sub-type- or file-scoped `--set`, a rule), the rule `backend_follows_format` already uses. A preset or config `[subtypes.pbr_emissive] encoder.backend = "ispc_bc6h"` (what the preset creator writes for "Built-in") now gives bc7e, with the fact's provenance, instead of CFG-INVALID on every 8-bit emissive. A pinned BC6H-only backend still reports CFG-INVALID.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BY9 A failure after the DDS write removes only a sidecar this run wrote (#15; BV3, BV4).** `FileTracker::sidecar_written` limits `run_one`'s cleanup, so a hand-made `.dds.json` kept as OUT-SIDECAR-KEPT survives a failed manifest append.

## Note BZ — bc7e tuning: the `max` level and patch 0002 (2026-10-18)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CB1, 2026-10-18)
- **BZ5 Objects are rebuilt on Windows.** The Linux ISPC 1.31.0 binary cannot emit COFF, so the vendored `ispc-objs/bc7e` objects and header still carry only patch 0001; `build.rs` detects the patch from the header, the shim has no level 7 without it, and `check_profile` refuses `effort = "max"` with `CFG-INVALID`. Owner step: `tools/regen-ispc.ps1 -Update -Bc7eOnly`, `tools/hash-vendor.ps1`, `cargo test -p encoders-sys`, the golden test. The study's timings come from Linux objects built from the same source and flags (plus `--target-os=linux --pic`), which reproduce the vendored objects' output byte for byte.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BZ5 Objects are rebuilt on Windows.** The Linux ISPC 1.31.0 binary cannot emit COFF, so the vendored `ispc-objs/bc7e` objects and header are rebuilt on Windows with `tools/regen-ispc.ps1 -Update -Bc7eOnly` and `tools/hash-vendor.ps1`, and carry patch 0002 (Note CB1). `build.rs` detects the patch from the header; the shim has no level 7 without it, and `check_profile` then refuses `effort = "max"` with `CFG-INVALID`. The study's timings come from Linux objects built from the same source and flags (plus `--target-os=linux --pic`), which match the vendored objects only on the same ISPC target: bc7e output depends on the CPU's target (Note CB3).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BZ5 Objects are rebuilt on Windows.** The Linux ISPC 1.31.0 binary cannot emit COFF, so the vendored `ispc-objs/bc7e` objects and header are rebuilt on Windows with `tools/regen-ispc.ps1 -Update -Bc7eOnly` and `tools/hash-vendor.ps1`, and carry patch 0002 (Note CB1). `build.rs` stops the build when the header lacks the patch (Note FM15). The study's timings come from Linux objects built from the same source and flags (plus `--target-os=linux --pic`), which match the vendored objects only on the same ISPC target: bc7e output depends on the CPU's target (Note CB3).

## Note CA — GUI layout v2, branch 1 (main team, 2026-10-18)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC9, 2026-10-29)
- **CA5 "Warned" means `export_asks` (R5).** Texture and set warnings (`counts_toward_badge`) never open Review; they are in Problems and, as a count, in Review's Warnings column ("N other warnings - see Problems", A4). (superseded by FC9)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC5, 2026-10-29)
- **CA7 The PGPatcher JSON pre-answer (R6, K12).** An edited PGPatcher JSON is a fourth reason to open Review. Its line offers **Keep the edited file** / **Overwrite it**; neither is preselected. The answer stores the on-disk hash; `finish_json` applies it only when the file still has that hash, else today's `Dialog::OverwriteJson` asks after the export. `start_export` always moves the answer out of `review` into `pending_json_answer`, and `finish_json` takes it on entry, so a cancelled, failed or no-JSON export never leaves a stale "Overwrite" for a later one. No choice made: today's question after the export (BV10 08f-2 carries over to `export_from_review`). (superseded by FC5)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CD5 and Note FC8, 2026-10-18)
- **CA10 Ctrl+M and the Messages tab (R8).** Ctrl+M and the header's Messages button open the Messages tab, or collapse the panel when it already shows Messages. `Messages::is_open` means "the Messages tab is showing" (set by `sync_messages_open`), so unread counting is unchanged. The floating Messages window is gone; toasts stay, anchored to the window's bottom-right, so over an open panel they cover its bottom-right corner (Review's Export included). They take no clicks, so Export still works under them; moving them above the panel waits (controller, final review). (Superseded in part by CD5: there is no Messages button in the header; Ctrl+M or the Messages tab opens the tab.) (superseded by FC8)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FC8, 2026-10-29)
- **CA14 The BS14 focus guard applies to Review's Export.** Review's Export saves first (`start_export`), so a click with a text field focused says "Export: leave the text field first", as the header Export does, and does not start the export (`header::field_focused`). (superseded by FC8)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC9, 2026-10-29)
- **CA18 The set editor's Warnings block becomes one line (R10).** "N problems in this set - Show in Problems" opens Problems filtered to the set (`set_problem_count`). A Textures column count "N need a decision" and a set's warning badge are buttons that open Problems narrowed (Undecided / Set). The recipe lines and "no file matches" lines stay. (superseded by FC9)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **CA4 Pitfalls: a tick each, plus Acknowledge all (R4; owner, 2026-10-17).** Each pitfall row has an Acknowledge tick and the Warnings column has **Acknowledge all**. Export stays disabled ("Acknowledge 2 warnings first") until every current pitfall is ticked. Ticks are keyed by the issue's exact text, so a changed or new pitfall needs a new tick. They count for one export only: cleared when the export starts, when Review closes and when a folder opens. Nothing is saved (no `sdc.toml`/`gui.toml` field).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **CA11 Problems (A1, K8, K9, R9).** Rows are built once per change (key: the views key, `evidence_rev`, `export.revision` and whether an export runs) and only the rows on screen are drawn. Order: blockers, "Failed: ...", "Needs a decision: ...", `export_asks` pitfalls, texture warnings, set warnings, RNM notes, then the last export's per-file warnings once it has ended. An acknowledged code is no longer a Decide row, but the warning stays listed (K8). A row is one non-wrapping line. Its fix buttons (**Go to set**, **Set type…**, **Decide…**, **Acknowledge**) and the code are laid out at the right edge first and stay visible; the sentence is cut with an ellipsis to the room left, and hovering it shows it in full (final review I2: before, a long row pushed its buttons off-screen at 1280x800).

## Note CC — GUI layout v2, branch 2 (main team, 2026-10-18)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB2, 2026-10-29)
- **CC2 Width rulings (2.2, 2.3).** Slot columns are compact: the file name truncates (name and size on hover), Flags are icon toggles with hovers (accessible names "Match on this" and "Opaque" unchanged; their role changed from CheckBox to a selectable Button), Status is a dot only; target about 440 px. The rows sit in a horizontal `ScrollArea` clipped to their column as the fallback: at a narrower centre they scroll sideways and are never drawn under a neighbour (the first build drew them under the PGPatcher panel, which took their clicks). The File cell takes the column width minus the other columns' widest content from the last frame, clamped to 60-240 px with an 8 px margin (without the margin a row exactly as wide as its column flipped the scroll bar every frame). The set panel's default width is 300 px, which is also its minimum (the brief's 420 left the rows 308 px; only 300 fits them at 1400 wide without sideways scroll), and its content uses `ScrollArea::both` (a vertical-only area let the PBR Landscape card, about 388 px, widen the panel past its range and the layout never settled). Cost: that card and the skin card scroll sideways at the default width. Panel range is `300..=(avail - CENTRE_MIN/2).max(300)` (K20), one `Id::new("set-panel")` for every set so a dragged width carries over (A10).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB2, 2026-10-29)
- **CC8 Header values (2.4).** Features: "{n} on" or "none" (n counts `feature_on` in `eff`). Subsurface: Off, Standard, Foliage, or "Standard + Foliage (conflict)". Coat, Emissive, Parallax, Glint, Fuzz: "on" or "off". NiAlphaProperty: "set" or "off". Matching: "{n} match(es)" (all rows, including an empty one added by "+"), or "matches nothing" in AMBER. CS PBR records: "1 record" or "{n} records" (txst and mato blocks present, 0-2). The PBR Landscape card and "PGPatcher JSON settings" show no value (A12).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC9, 2026-10-29)

An off group's body is empty except Coat, whose body holds the kept-values note (A13), and Subsurface, whose body holds the selector (CC12). A group's id is keyed by its on/off state (`("pgp", g, on, set)`), so a group opens when its feature turns on (K16). (superseded by FC9)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC9 and Note FB2, 2026-10-29)

Features still counts it in "{n} on" (it counts `feature_on`). Subsurface's header shows `sub_mode`, so a stored but blocked Standard reads "Standard" while the group stays closed. (superseded by FC9)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note CD4 and Note FB2, 2026-10-18)

(superseded by CD4)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **CC9 Feature groups are always listed, reading "off" while off (A3 header-value design).** Coat, Emissive, Parallax, Glint and Fuzz used to be hidden while off; now they are listed, closed and "off". An off group's body is empty except Coat, whose body holds the kept-values note (A13), and Subsurface, whose body holds the selector (CC12). A group's id is keyed by its on/off state (`("pgp", g, on, set)`), so a group opens when its feature turns on (K16). A feature that is on but blocked reads `on, blocked by {feature}` in amber (Note FC9).

The Advanced drawer's content is the panel's last tab.
