## Note CO — v2 Generate from normal map (owner, 2026-10-21)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC11, 2026-10-29)
- **CO39** Provenance lives in a top-level `[generated]` table of `sdc-project.toml` (item key → inline table of `model::from_normal::Provenance`; keys match by Unicode lowercase, Note BF). A spec gap: `[files]` is in `sdc.toml`, which the engine parses with `deny_unknown_fields`, so a key there would be a config and schema change visible to the CLI; the engine needs only the name. `set_generated` writes nested inline tables itself (`json_to_toml` writes no objects) and drops `null` members. (superseded by FC11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC11, 2026-10-29)
- **CO51** Provenance follows the JSON hash records' save rule through one helper (`AppModel::save_record`, split out of `save_hash_record`; P14): saved at once when `sdc-project.toml` was clean, else written with the next Save. Generate then shows "provenance recorded; written with the next Save" as `status_note` (it stays in front of the status line through the re-plan), not in `export.notes`. If the user never saves, the file comes back as unrecorded ("exists but was not generated here"), as the hash records do. (superseded by FC11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC3, 2026-10-29)
- **CO70** `GEN-STALE` is a GUI-only Info code (114th on this branch; main's `ENC-WEIGHTS-IGNORED` is also 113th there, so the count is renumbered at merge). `GenInfo::stale` carries it; the Height row's dot hover shows "stale: {reason}", and the texture's message list (`AppModel::item_messages`, which the settings panel draws) adds it as `Warning::new(Code::GenStale, reason)`. It is never pushed into `TextureItem::warnings` and is never a Problems row; `GEN-REPLACED` likewise stays on hover (owner answers 2 and 7). (superseded by FC3)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC3, 2026-10-29)
- **CO71** Placement under layout v2 (Q1; owner answers 2 and 7): the slot row's File cell hover gains "Generated from {normal}" (", replaced by {name}" when replaced); the Flags cell gets a weak "generated" label whose AccessKit name and hover are "Generated from {normal}"; a stale file turns only that row's status dot amber (`Status::Warning`, label "warning") even when the tile is ready. `pill`, `tile_ready`, `flagged`, the set card dot and Export's dot do not read it. For the `_m`-alpha height (no slot row, P20) the `_m` block's alpha fill line gets the hover and a weak "stale: {reason}" line under it; `FillRow::FromFile` gained the source's item `key` for that. (superseded by FC3)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CO76, 2026-10-21)
- **CO73** Delete generated file (CE3): offered on the Height row's menu when the row holds a generated file or the set has a generated height the engine replaced (GEN-REPLACED, found by set key, P5), after Generate (P12), and as a "Delete generated file" submenu of the set card's menu listing every generated file of the set with its stale reason on hover (P20). The box ("Delete generated file", 420 px) has "Delete {short name}" (Destructive, red outline) and "Cancel" (the Enter default); the body keeps the whole name (CE19). Controller ruling for Task 7: it deletes only a file whose current sha256 matches its record, so it never deletes a file Tessalith did not write or one the user edited. The brief's wordings stay ("…It was edited since.", "…Tessalith has no record of generating it."), but for those files Delete is disabled with the reason on hover and "Not deleted: {reason}." under the body. This differs from P13's "both buttons enabled", so the box is drawn with `dialog_hover` (the same frame as `dialog`, plus the disabled hover). The texts and the refusal are read once when the box opens; the hash is read again on Delete, and a file that changed meanwhile is kept with "{name} changed since; it was not deleted". On Delete: `remove_file`, then `set_generated(key, None)` outside the undo history with P14's save-if-clean rule (status note "provenance recorded; written with the next Save" when deferred), then a re-plan. It is not undoable.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CO116, 2026-10-21)
- **CO112** P6 for curvature: written at the set's albedo size (its Diffuse slot's own file), shown read-only, because Also's diffuse reads the albedo and the curvature together (TA22). `SIZE_LOCKED` when the normal is smaller than the albedo (or another aspect), or the albedo is above 2048 (`AO_MAX`, the curvature cap). With no albedo, any size the normal offers up to 2048. With only Curvature ticked from the set menu, the Size takes its size (as CO95 does for AO).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **CO74** Track C hook (X-S6, P9): `checks::texture_checks` prefixes every `CHK-RANGE` and `CHK-PARALLAX` message of a job whose input stem `is_generated` with "generated: ".

## Note CR — NIF v2 Phase C: named clips (NIF team, 2026-10-21)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GD8, 2026-10-29)
- **CR6 Which clips exist (C-P3).** `read_model` lists only live sequences: when the file has at least one `NiControllerManager`, those some manager lists; with no manager, all of them. Remove unlinks the clip and leaves the block in the file, and an unlisted sequence never plays in game. Any remove releases each controller it drove that no other live clip drives (the last clip that drives a controller): it clears 0x20 there and puts back the interpolator ref. An unreadable listed sequence refuses Remove and Split (C-P29).
  - Export drops these orphans: Note CQ.
  - Removing the last clip now also unlinks the emptied manager and its MTTC (the game crashes on an empty manager): Note CU8.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CZ15, 2026-10-22)
- **CR8 Convert to clips (C-P13, C-P28, C-P30).** (Placement superseded by Note CZ15.) Convert takes a property's free controllers (known class, Active, not managed, keys present, no Play Backwards) into one clip, named `SpecialIdle` by default (`CONVERT_NAME`, with the hover `CONVERT_HOVER`). They must share cycle and frequency bits and have phase 0, else "These controllers run on different timings: they cannot share one clip." The clip takes the union of start and stop; keys are not rescaled. Each controller's start and stop must be finite before the union (C-P30). Controllers that are keyless or of another class stay free. `convert_check` can say Ok where the op still refuses (zero keys, a broken chain); the button tries the op and shows the refusal.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CZ15, 2026-10-22)
- **CR10 Clips UI (C-P17', C-P25, C-P28, C-P33).** (Placement superseded by Note CZ15.) Rename and Remove sit under the Preview list and act on the picked clip. New clip defaults to Clamp. Enter never removes a clip, and a corrected rename clears its refusal. A nameless clip says "This clip has no name: it cannot be renamed or removed here." (C-P33). NIF colours are stored sRGB (CS linearises EmitColor, spec 6.2) while egui's picker takes linear RGB, so the Shader-tab picker and the key swatch convert at the widget edge only (C-P17'); a picker outside 0-1 or NaN is disabled (C-P25). The colour strip uses the swatch's mapping, so a key and its strip agree (C-P17, C-P16): `strip_colours` is pure and unit-tested, an HDR sample (any channel outside 0-1) gets a dashed band along the strip's top and an outlined swatch, and the strip has its own 12 px allocation under the graph.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GD8, 2026-10-29)
- **CR13 Open items.** F14 (C-P15): no op edits an existing clip's cycle type, frequency or range, and keys added past a clip's stop never play; to change them the user removes and re-adds the clip. A later `SetSequenceTiming` is the owner's call. F17 (C-P18): the refusal "This clip has several interpolators for this controller: editing them needs Phase C." now reads "...: they cannot be edited.". The key swatch and strip show the stored colour, not the in-game colour (CS multiplies by Emissive Multiple), so a dim strip beside a bright game colour is expected. Removing the last clip on a controller gives it that clip's keys, so the remove order decides which keys come back (C-P29 as ruled): remove a converted SpecialIdle last to get the original curve back; removed first, its curve stays only in an orphaned block (final review M4).

## Note CQ — Export drops orphan blocks our edits made (owner, 2026-10-21)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GD2, 2026-10-29)
- **CQ5 Doubt means no compaction.** A reachable appended block of another kind, a block that does not read at its exact size, a ref past the file or a file that does not parse makes `compact` return Err, and the export writes the patch uncompacted. The ref-free kinds are read too (key data, texture sets, text keys, extra data at their exact size) before they count as ref-free. The export still succeeds and records the hash of the bytes it wrote; one plain line under **Export NIF** says "Exported without cleanup: <reason>." until the next export or edit (fix round 1, coordinator ruling).

## Note CU — Parked NIF editor fixes (owner, 2026-10-21)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CU9, 2026-10-21)
- **CU4 Any unreadable entry on the root's controller chain refuses (CR15 M2).** `root_manager` refused only when the unreadable entry was itself a NiControllerManager. An unreadable controller of another class in front of the manager ended the walk, so AddSequence put a second manager in front of the real one. Now any entry before the chain's end that does not read refuses AddSequence, Remove and Rename ("The root's controller chain cannot be read."), and `clip_key` gives None. A class `nif::ctrl::read_ctrl` does not read at all (one not ending in `Controller`, such as the particle `...Ctlr` classes) on the root's own chain refuses too. That was not measured on the corpus; the CR2 census found every vanilla manager's next block to be an MTTC. Test: `add_sequence_refuses_an_unreadable_controller_in_front_of_the_manager`. Extended past the first manager by CU9.

## Note CV — Trace-debug fixes 2026-10-21 (main session, 2026-10-21)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC10, 2026-10-29)
- **CV8 An exact slot with no known path shows an empty pill in the JSON editor (trace 18; Note V, item V10).** "+ key > Match > exact slot N" wrote `matchN = ""` for a slot Tessalith has no path for, and the editor draws no pill for an empty match, so the click looked like it did nothing. It now adds a pending pill (nothing is stored until a path is typed or picked), as "path contains" already does; the set's Matching header keeps its own empty rows (V10). Slot 1 is unchanged. A `matchN = ""` row stored before this fix still shows no pill in the editor; remove it by hand (it matches nothing). (superseded by FC10)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **CV3 NVTT timeout and cancel kill the whole process tree (trace 5; points at AF3).** The per-level timeout and cancel killed only the program `--nvtt` named. A launcher or wrapper left the real encoder running, the one-at-a-time slot (NV7) was freed while it still ran, and the temp folder could not be removed. The level loop now uses the same `kill_tree` as the probe. Stock `nvcompress` is unchanged.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **CV15 The bulk Encoder row honours the NVTT-not-found lock (trace 13; Note NV).** Choosing NVTT in the bulk panel wrote it to every selected texture when NVTT was missing, and Export was then blocked. Such textures are now skipped with "NVTT not found: set its path in Advanced"; a texture already on NVTT and a Built-in pick are unaffected.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **CV21 A path that is not Unicode no longer stops `gui.toml` saving (trace 31).** One folder name with a lone surrogate in `recent` (or the export, NVTT or Meshes folder) made every settings save fail, losing the session's settings. That path is now left out of the file. Cost: it is not remembered after a restart.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **CV21 A path that is not Unicode no longer stops `gui.toml` saving (trace 31).** One folder name with a lone surrogate in `recent` (or the export or Meshes folder) made every settings save fail, losing the session's settings. That path is now left out of the file. Cost: it is not remembered after a restart.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **CV5 The env-map gate asks for the colour member's own key (trace 17; Notes CN17 and CN68).** CN17 says the vote key is the output stem "without a §6.1 suffix"; CN68 says the GUI asks for the colour member's own key (`colour_mesh_key`, the output stem as written). They differ only when the colour member is `_d`: the vanilla file keeps the name (`steel_d.dds`, Note BQ colour-term rule), the GUI asked for `textures/arm/steel_d` and the engine looked up `textures/arm/steel`, so the vote was never found (DERIVE-NO-ENVMAP). The file name that gets written is what a vanilla mesh references, so CN68 is the intended form: with an albedo the engine uses the stem as it is; a set with no albedo (its anchor is the `_n`) still drops the suffix, as before, and the GUI does not ask for it. Sets without `_d` are unchanged.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **CV2 `patch-header --dry-run` no longer prints the real code (trace 4).** A dry run printed `DDS-HEADER-PATCHED` ("header patched") with exit 0 while the file was untouched, so a script counting that code over-counted. It now prints `<file>: would patch: <fixes>` and no code; a real patch still prints the code, after the write. Cost: anything that read the code from a dry run must read the text instead.

## Note CZ — Meshes page redesign (owner, 2026-10-22)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GD2, 2026-10-29)
- **CZ2 Export NIF is a footer (T3).** The block is a `Panel::bottom("meshes-edit-export")` inside the edit panel, drawn before the tab strip, so a long tab never pushes it away. It reads: a status line (green dot and "Ready", or red dot and "Blocked: <reason>"), "Writes <data path> to <export path>" (middle-elided, the whole line on hover and in the accessible name), "The source file is never changed. Change the folder with Export to ▾ in the header.", the button, then the last result, cleanup and placement lines as before. A read-only session (no export folder) keeps the block and shows `NO_EXPORT_FOLDER` as its blocked reason instead of hiding it. Test: `the_edit_panel_fits_beside_the_3d_view_with_the_header_and_bottom_panel` (footer inside the panel, button below the tabs), `a_read_only_session_shows_the_no_export_folder_reason`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC5 and Note GD2, 2026-10-29)

Export NIF ignores a click made by those keys on the focused button; the guard dialog ignores an Overwrite click on a frame where Enter or Space was pressed. A pointer or AccessKit click still writes. The shared `ui/dialogs.rs` is a main-team file, so its other Destructive buttons are unchanged: the guard lives on the NIF side. A main-team follow-up can move it into `dialogs.rs` for every Destructive button. Test: `enter_or_space_on_focused_export_or_overwrite_writes_nothing` (a mutation that drops the Overwrite guard fails it). (superseded by FC5)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GD1, 2026-10-29)

The red count it compares with is kept per egui context, not per session: reopening Edit on a file with as many red rows as the last session leaves the list closed (the status line and Show still lead to it). Rows name the field in words as the tabs do ("SLSF1 flags", "UV offset U"), not the Rust `Debug` name. `Trailer(n)` reads "Shader type value n+1": its real name depends on the shader type, which an op does not carry (a `shortcut:` in `edit_panel.rs`). Test: `a_red_row_opens_the_pending_list_and_rows_read_as_words`; `export_button_and_guard_dialog_enter_keeps_theirs` opens the list before it looks for Revert.

NIF edits export with Export NIF in the edit panel." and keeps its count line. The accent and the layout are unchanged. Test: `the_meshes_view_keeps_the_header` (`meshes_header.rs`).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CZ14 and Note CZ25, 2026-10-22)
- **CZ10 Visible rows and Details (T2).** Visible: the shader, the set texture slots (with "Open in Textures" and a red "→ missing"), one weak "(N empty slots)" line, the alpha line ("No NiAlphaProperty" when the shape has none), and in edit mode "Controllers: N: title, title (clip)" or "none" (no line for a shape whose shader is not a BSLightingShaderProperty, whose controllers the editor does not read; `NifEdit::card_ctrls`, built with the cards in `reapply` from the shader's and the alpha's controller lists; outside edit mode there is no controller data in the scan, so no line). A closed "Details" section holds the block and 3D index, SLSF1/SLSF2, the geometry line, which mod each texture comes from and the raw slot strings. Each Details header is named "Details of NIF block N" (rule 8.4). The empty slots moved from "(empty)" per slot to one line.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note CZ20, 2026-10-22)
- **CZ19 Heel height (T5b; T5b-heel-height.md "In-game results" and "Editor rules (revised)").** A Known entry "Heel height (RaceMenu)" (`KnownValue::Heel`, a row of its own: `heel_row`) on a shape only; on the root it is off with "The game ignores heel height here. Put it on a shape." (the editor has no shader extra list, so a shader is never offered). Set heel height is one undo step: HH_OFFSET (NiFloatExtraData) = Z, an EditExtra of the shape's first HH_OFFSET when it has one (a second is ignored in game), else an AddExtra; when the shape has an SDTA, its first SDTA's last "NPC" entry gets the same Z, other entries kept (`sdta_with_npc_z`; an SDTA that is not a JSON list is left as it is, a `shortcut:`); no second SDTA is ever added, and a new one only when "Also write SDTA (OStim and others read it)" is ticked (off by default). The field starts at HH_OFFSET, else the SDTA's last NPC Z, else 0. Warnings come from `validate` (nif crate) under the rows: heel data on the root or on a property (`HEEL_NOT_ON_SHAPE`), and heel data on a shape after the first shape that has some (`HEEL_EARLIER_WINS`; shape order = model order). The heel row repeats the second for a shape that has no heel data yet. `NifEdit::weight_sibling` (set when the session opens, when the scan has the other `_0`/`_1` file, `weight_sibling_of`) adds "Apply the same value to the other weight file (…)."; there is no cross-file write. Calculate: Z = −(lowest vertex Z) rounded to 0.1, `heel_z_of`: a skinned shape's vertices through the inverse of its NiSkinData skin transform (`nif::geom::skin_to_root`, new), an unskinned one through its node chain (`render3d::parse` world). Corpus check (`heel_height_calculation_against_the_corpus`, #[ignore], SDC_ACHERUS, read-only, loose NIFs under mods): 215 shapes with stored heel data; within 0.5: 146, within 1.0: 182, within 2.0: 202, median 0.40, max 8.70. The large misses are ground models (`_gnd`, `GND`, `_go`) and one BodySlide ShapeData source, where the stored value is not used in game; on the 140 worn models: within 0.5: 100, within 1.0: 130, all within 2.0 (max 1.60). Calculate ships, with the value shown and editable before Set. Tooltip from the brief. Tests: `heel_height_*` and `weight_sibling_names_the_other_weight_file` (nif_edit.rs), `heel_height.rs`, `heel_data_off_a_shape_or_on_a_later_shape_warns` (nif validate), `skin_to_root_inverts_the_skin_transform` (nif geom); builder scene `heel_scene`.

## Note DB — Version 1.0.0, first official release (owner, 2026-10-22)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DM7, 2026-10-26)
- **DB1 Version.** The workspace version moves 0.9.0 → 1.0.0 for the first official (non-alpha) release (owner: "bump engine to 1", "this will be an official release build"). 0.9.0 was never released, so the CHANGELOG's 1.0.0 section covers everything since 0.8.0-alpha.2. As in CH1, `ENGINE_VERSION` follows `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` and is part of `is_up_to_date`, so every `--on-existing if-changed` run re-encodes each recorded output once after upgrading. No preset or pixel output changes; goldens are unchanged. `docs/presets-changelog.md` gets a 1.0.0 entry and README names the version. The release zip is built without a suffix (`Tessalith-1.0.0-win-x64.zip`) and the commit it is built from is tagged `v1.0.0` locally; the owner pushes. (Superseded for later versions by Note DM: per-type encode versions.)

## Note DC — Photoshop DDS plugin (owner, 2026-10-24)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DF3, 2026-10-25)
- **DC6 (R5) Input scale.** The plugin passes float RGBA in [0, 1]: 8-bit `v / 255`, 16-bit `v / 32768` (Photoshop's 16-bit range is 0..32768). Transfer `SourceTransfer::Stored`. (Amended by DF3: a 32-bit Colour document passes Photoshop's linear samples unscaled with `SourceTransfer::Linear`.)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note DC39, 2026-10-25)
- **DC9 sRGB sets content and tag.** Superseded by DC39 (sRGB sets only the tag). The sRGB control sets both colour fields: On -> `color.content = srgb`, `color.tag = srgb`; Off -> `linear`, `unorm`. Normal and Linear mask are always Off, so linear / unorm. A `cs-quality` sub-type whose tag is `n/a` would keep its colour fields untouched; none of Diffuse, Normal or GenericData has one today. So Colour + sRGB Off is linear content, not the preset's Diffuse `srgb` / `unorm`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DD6 and Note DC31 and Note DC42, 2026-10-25)
- **DC12 The dialog shows the filter the encoder uses; a size limit (review I1, I2).** (Amended by Note DD6: the size rule and its text now live once in `psdds_check_size`; the wording is unchanged.)
  - `psdds_resolve` takes the document's `has_alpha`. For Colour, it builds the profile with a non-box probe filter and asks the engine's own rule, `engine::filter::filter_allowed`. That function is unchanged and is the gate `mips::effective_filter` uses; no copy of the rule exists. Where the rule refuses, the filter greys and shows Box: sRGB Off (linear content), BC4/BC5 (sRGB forced Off; Colour no longer offers them, DC32), and alpha kept in BC3/BC7/RGBA8 (Blend gives alpha-weighted mips). `psdds_encode` uses the same resolved settings.
  - `psdds_encode` refuses a width or height above 16384, or a pixel count whose byte size exceeds `isize::MAX`, before reading the caller's buffer. The error reads "This image is larger than 16384 pixels on a side, the most Skyrim supports." This keeps a huge document from aborting Photoshop on a failed allocation.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note DC20, 2026-10-24)
- **DC14 Open stays off until Task 7.** PiPL `CanRead` is false, so Photoshop never routes Open to the plugin before the read selectors exist. `ReadExtensions` is already listed. Superseded by DC20: Task 7 sets `CanRead` true.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DC21 and Note DC31, 2026-10-24)
- **DC19 Save options and remembered settings (Task 6).**
  - The dialog is shown in OptionsStart (PIFormat.h: the place to "get any file options from the user"). Cancel returns `userCanceledErr` (-128); a non-zero OptionsStart result stops all later selectors, so no file is written. A dialog that cannot be created (`GetModuleHandleExW` fails, `DialogBoxParamW` returns -1 or 0) reports "the Tessalith DDS options dialog could not be shown." through `errReportString` (review M1).
  - The chosen settings reach the write selectors through PluginMain's `data` value, which Photoshop keeps for the plugin across calls (SDK Plug-in Module Interface). It points to one `Globals` (settings + the per-save image buffers), allocated on first use and kept while the plugin is loaded; the buffers are freed at the end of each write. The Globals are intentionally never freed: freeing them in WriteFinish would break the OptionsStart -> Write handoff of a later save (review M4). If the host skips OptionsStart, WriteStart loads the remembered settings (every save: the flag is cleared with the per-save buffers, final review M2).
  - The dialog keeps the user's raw picks; greyed controls show `psdds_resolve`'s effective value but keep the raw one, so Normal -> Colour restores sRGB On. The raw values are stored and passed to the write, which resolves them again. A quality index from another format is kept raw and shown (and encoded) as the format's default, as DC8 rules.
  - Storage: Descriptor Registry, persistent, key `3845e6ae-fbf3-46dc-9b93-f505f11cd107`, six integers under four-char keys. (A seventh, `disc`, came with DC36; when missing it loads as 0.) On load (validated by `psdds_resolve` with the document's has_alpha), a missing key, a value `psdds_resolve` refuses, or missing suites give `psdds_defaults(Colour)` without an error. Settings are stored on OK only.
  - The dialog's has_alpha is the write path's own `alpha_plane` on the OptionsStart record (`planes` is documented as set before OptionsStart; `transparencyPlane` and `documentInfo` are not stated, and `alpha_plane` tolerates a missing `documentInfo`).
  - The host's playInfo is honoured (review I1, PIActions.h dialogPlayEnum): with `descriptorParameters` present, the dialog shows only for `plugInDialogDisplay`. For `plugInDialogDontDisplay` and `plugInDialogSilent` (scripts, actions, batch runs with dialogs off), OptionsStart shows nothing and saves with the remembered settings. Without `descriptorParameters` (an interactive Save) the dialog always shows. Amended by DC21: not when the document carries revertInfo.
  - shortcut: the descriptor's contents are neither read nor written, so a script or action cannot carry its own settings; they always come from the remembered ones. Upgrade when recorded actions need their own settings.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DC23, 2026-10-24)
- **DC20 Open (Task 7).** PiPL `CanRead` is true (supersedes DC14).
  - ReadStart takes the file size from `dataFork` (`GetFileSizeEx`) and refuses anything over `kMaxDdsFileBytes` (pixels.h: 16384² RGBA8 with a full 15-level mip chain, plus magic, header and DX10 header = 1,431,655,912 bytes) before allocating: "this file is too large to be a DDS this plugin can open." It then reads the whole file with `ReadFile`, decodes the top mip with `psdds_decode` and drops the file bytes before handing pixels over. Peak memory is about file + 2 x image (psdds holds the decoded pixels and its RGBA copy while the file bytes are alive): roughly 3.4 GB for a 16384 x 16384 RGBA8 file with mips. A decode refusal (HDR, cube map, array, truncated, unsupported) shows psdds's text, first letter lower-cased after Photoshop's "because". Out of memory on a read selector reads "there is not enough memory to open this DDS."
  - FilterFile (PIFormat.h) claims a file only when it has the DDS magic and its header (and DX10 header) describe something Open can read; anything else gets `formatCannotRead`, so Photoshop shows its own message or tries another plug-in. Declined: BC6H and float DXGI formats, legacy float FourCCs (D3DFMT 111-117), cube maps (caps2 0x200, DX10 misc 0x4) and texture arrays (DX10 array size > 1), so another installed DDS plug-in can open those (`dds_header_openable` in pixels.cpp). A file shorter than the headers is still claimed, so the reader can say why it fails. ReadStart also refuses an empty file and a zero-width or zero-height image with a clear message.
  - The document is 8-bit RGB, 72 ppi. Pixels go to the host as planar bands of whole rows (`deinterleave_rgba8_to_planes`, `band_rows` with the 64 MiB cap shared with Save, DC17); ReadPrepare sets `maxData = 0`.
  - Alpha: 4 planes when the decoded alpha has any value below 255, otherwise 3. psdds reports `has_alpha` for every BC3/BC7/RGBA8 file; a fully opaque one opens without transparency so a re-save keeps the Colour mip filter instead of the alpha rule's Box (DC12). With 4 planes, `transparencyPlane = 3` when the host passed -1 (PIFormat.h: "set to -1 by hosts that support transparency"), so the document opens as a layer with transparency; a host passing 0 gets plane 3 as an alpha channel. Threshold superseded by DC23 (alpha >= 254 is opaque).
  - shortcut: no ICC profile is attached to sRGB files and `convertMode` / `preferredColorModes` are ignored; Photoshop assigns its working space. Add a profile if colour-managed users see shifts.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note DC22, 2026-10-24)
- **DC21 revertInfo: a plain Save after Open reuses the remembered settings (Task 6 review M3).** PIFormat.h `revertInfo`: NULL at ReadStart means "set up a handle with what is needed to read the file without querying the user and, if possible, to write it"; non-NULL means revert, do not query; options calls must leave it able to revert. Open has no options, so the handle's presence is the information: ReadStart (after a successful decode) and every OptionsStart that does not cancel give the document a 4-byte host handle (`hostNewHdl`, else `handleProcs->newProc`; "TDDS" written when the lock procs exist). Photoshop owns and disposes of it. OptionsStart without descriptor parameters skips the dialog when `revertInfo` is set and saves with the remembered settings (Descriptor Registry, DC19); a script asking for the dialog (`plugInDialogDisplay`) still gets it. Consequences: Ctrl+S on a document opened from DDS, or already saved as DDS, shows no dialog; Save As to DDS from such a document may also skip it, depending on whether Photoshop passes the document's `revertInfo` there (owner check). The settings handle is not per-document: the remembered settings are the last ones chosen anywhere. Superseded by DC22: the skip and the handle are removed.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DD6, 2026-10-25)
- **DC23 Open treats alpha >= 254 as opaque (final review I2).** `opens_with_transparency` (pixels.cpp): 4 planes with transparency only when psdds reports alpha and some alpha byte is below 254; otherwise 3 planes. The engine guarantees only alpha >= 254 for opaque BC7 when `alpha.exact_opaque` is off (Notes A, C, D, Spike S5b), and Tessalith's own opaque BC7 diffuse files decode with 254 on many texels; without this they opened as transparent layers and a re-save switched to the alpha rule's Box filter (DC12). Cost: an image whose alpha uses only 254 and 255 opens opaque (one alpha step lost). Reversible. (Amended by Note DD6: the rule is `psdds_opens_with_alpha`, called by Photoshop and the other hosts.) (Amended by Note FC6.2: on save, every host says once when alpha only 254-255 is written opaque.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DD3, 2026-10-25)
- **DC25 The plugin pins itself in memory (owner crash report, 2026-10-25).** Photoshop 2025 26.9.0.15 crashed after a save: BEX64, `TessalithDDS.8bi_unloaded`, 0xc0000005, offset 0x9483. With the PDB of that exact build (PE timestamp 0x6ADDC216), offset 0x9483 is the instruction after `call WaitOnAddress` in `std::sys::sync::condvar::futex::Condvar::wait`. A stack walk of the threads left after a save shows 16 rayon workers (the engine's global pool, started by `engine::memory::encode_image`) parked there via `rayon_core::registry::WorkerThread::wait_until_cold`. Photoshop unloads the plugin after the save; the workers keep sleeping in its unmapped code and crash on their next wake-up. A harness that loads the .8bi, saves, calls FreeLibrary and alerts those threads crashes with 0xc0000005 at exactly +0x9483. (Amended by Note DD3: the pin moved into psdds itself and applies to every host that loads the DLL.)
  - Fix: on the first PluginMain call, `GetModuleHandleExW(GET_MODULE_HANDLE_EX_FLAG_PIN | FROM_ADDRESS)` pins the module, so FreeLibrary never unmaps it; it stays until Photoshop exits, and a later load reuses it and its already-running pool.
  - Why pin and not shut the pool down: the global pool is a leaked static registry with no shutdown; a private `rayon::ThreadPool` drops asynchronously (its threads exit after `drop` returns, still running module code), and rayon has no synchronous join. Joining our own spawn-handler threads after every save would work but needs every engine path wrapped in `install`, and would still leave other module-lifetime state (Rust TLS destructors, the static CRT) exposed to an unload. Pinning removes the whole class with one call. Cost: the .8bi (about 4 MB of code plus idle threads) stays loaded for the session.
  - Ruled out: the dialog (DialogBoxParamW is modal and ends before OptionsStart returns; no window class is registered), other threads (psdds starts none; the engine's `thread::scope` joins), CRT atexit/TLS callbacks (they run during unload, inside mapped code, and would fault elsewhere).
  - Regression test: `photoshop/tests/unload_test.cpp`, run by `build.ps1 -Test` (which now builds the plugin first): load, save, FreeLibrary, wait 2 s; it fails if the module is unmapped while the save's threads still run.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DC29 and Note DC38, 2026-10-25)
- **DC26 PiPL claims layer support so Save As lists the format (owner request, 2026-10-25).** Owner: Tessalith DDS showed only under Save a Copy, while Intel Texture Works showed under plain Save As. The 2026 SDK does not state the rule: it only says format plug-ins "appear in the format pop-up menu in the Open, Save As and Save a Copy dialogs" (PGPlugInTypes), and documents no key for it. Evidence instead: Intel's PiPL (resource `PIPL` 16000, read as data from the owner's IntelTextureWorks.8bi) carries `fmsl` = 0x80, which PIPL.r defines as `FormatLayerSupport` with `doesSupportFormatLayers`; ours had none. Its other differences (`fmtf` 0x3E, which adds CanCreateThumbnail; `mxch` 24 for every mode against our 4; Grayscale, Multichannel and 32-bit modes) are not taken. Since Photoshop 22.4 (Adobe's release notes, not the SDK), Save As lists only formats that keep the whole document; claiming layers is the one PiPL difference that can explain Intel's listing.
  - PiPL.json sets `"FormatLayerSupport": true` (pipl-schema.json). The plugin still saves the composite from WriteStart/Continue, as before.
  - The six layer selectors (ReadLayer and WriteLayer Start/Continue/Finish, PIFormat.h 35-40) are answered with `noErr`, `data = nullptr` and an empty rect, so no layer pixels are ever requested. ReadStart sets `layerData = 0` (PIFormat.h: on read the plug-in sets it "to tell host how many layers are in the document"), so Open still gives one composite image.
  - Cost: the format now appears in Save As for layered documents too, although layers, extra alpha channels and spot channels are dropped, which Save a Copy would have warned about. README says so and keeps the Save a Copy / "Enable legacy Save As" route.
  - Unverified without Photoshop: whether 26.9 still sends the composite through WriteStart when the PiPL claims layers, and whether Open is unaffected. Owner checks these. To undo, remove the PiPL key.
  - Possible second lever if a document with two or more alpha channels still lands only in Save a Copy: `FormatMaxChannels` ("so that Photoshop can warn the user that alpha channels will be discarded"). Not changed.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DC29, 2026-10-25)
- **DC27 A save that never gets its image fails and writes nothing (follow-up to DC26).** DC26's open question: if Photoshop sent a layered save only through the WriteLayer selectors, the plugin would have returned success with no bytes, leaving an empty or stale file (a Save As over an existing .dds would destroy it).
  - `write_finish` reports "Tessalith DDS could not read the image from Photoshop, so nothing was saved." (errReportString) when the save state still exists, i.e. the last band never arrived (WriteFinish is called "if and only if formatSelectorWriteStart returned without error", also after Escape). Errors from WriteContinue are reported there and not overwritten.
  - `write_layer_finish` reports the same when no WriteStart succeeded in this save. Basis: PIFormat.h `layerData` is set "prior to calling Start", so layers follow WriteStart. A `write_started` flag (last field of Globals) is set by WriteStart and cleared by OptionsStart and the new `write_prepare`. shortcut: a host that sends layers with neither OptionsStart nor WritePrepare sees the previous save's flag; add a per-save token if that order is ever seen.
  - The DDS is written in one place only, after the encode succeeded (the last WriteContinue), so nothing touches the file before the bytes exist. `SetEndOfFile` after that write drops an old tail if the host passed an existing file without truncating it.
  - The SDK does not say whether Photoshop keeps or deletes the destination when a format plugin returns an error; it only says that on WriteStart "the file to be written is open, and the file pointer is positioned at the start of the file". The plugin therefore relies on writing nothing, not on the host's clean-up.
  - Test: `photoshop/tests/host_test.cpp` (renamed from DC25's `unload_test.cpp`, which it still contains) case `save_without_image_fails`. With a 20,000-byte existing file opened without truncation: layer selectors only, and WriteStart then WriteFinish, must return the error and leave the file byte-identical; composite then layers must save a DDS with no old tail.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note DC38, 2026-10-25)
- **DC29 FormatLayerSupport reverted; DDS stays under Save a Copy (owner test, 2026-10-25; supersedes DC26 and DC27's layer part).** Superseded by DC38 (the merged composite is read through channel ports). In Photoshop 26.9, with `FormatLayerSupport`, a Save As of a 4096 x 4096 document with one non-background pixel layer ended with DC27's "could not read the image" error. File > Save a Copy, which worked before, failed the same way with and without its Layers checkbox. So claiming layers moves every save of a layered document onto the layer selectors, and the host does not deliver the merged image through WriteContinue.
  - What the SDK shows: PIFormat.h documents only `layerData` ("Set by host prior to calling Start to tell plugin how many layers are in the document. Set by the host prior to calling WriteLayerStart to tell the plugin which layer it is sending data for") and the per-layer fields (`isVisible`, `blendMode`, `opacity`, `isGroupDivider`, `layerName`). The layerformat sample writes its header in WriteStart, then one record per layer from WriteLayerStart/Continue (pulling rows with `advanceState`), and its reader skips `numLayers - 1` records after the first. Its file therefore holds exactly one record per layer: for a layered document, WriteContinue does not deliver a separate composite. No documented way to get the merged result on the layer path exists.
  - Choice: revert. On the layer path the plugin would have to composite itself (blend modes, opacity, masks, groups, adjustment layers); the coordinator ruled that out, and a single-layer-only path would break Save a Copy of every multi-layer document. Without the key, Save a Copy gets the flattened composite as before. README: Save a Copy, plus Preferences > File Handling > "Enable legacy Save As" for those who want it under Save As.
  - Removed: the PiPL key, the layer-selector handlers, `layerData = 0` on ReadStart, and `write_layer_finish` with its `write_started` flag. Kept from DC27: WriteFinish's "nothing was saved" error when the last band never arrived, the single write after the encode, and `SetEndOfFile`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DD6, 2026-10-25)
- **DC31 Save decides alpha from the pixels; the dialog opens after the pixels are read (owner report "the mip filter is always greyed out", controller ruling 2026-10-25; supersedes DC12's and DC19's has_alpha-from-planes rule and DC19's dialog placement).** A document whose content is a normal (non-background) layer always has a transparency plane, so the planes said `has_alpha = 1` and the encoder took `AlphaPolicy::Blend` with its Box-only mips, even for a fully opaque image. (Amended by Note DD6: the save-side scan is `psdds_alpha_used`, called by every host.)
  - Rule: Open's DC23 threshold applied to the float image. Alpha is kept only when some alpha is below 254/255: an 8-bit byte below 254, or a 16-bit sample below 254/255 of 32768 (`floats_have_transparency`, limit 254/255 − 1e-6). Otherwise `has_alpha = 0`, and psdds removes alpha as for an RGB document. Tessalith's own opaque BC7 floor (254) therefore re-saves opaque.
  - Flow:
    - OptionsStart only loads the remembered settings and applies the playInfo rule (DC19/DC22) to set `show_dialog`.
    - WriteStart/Continue read every band into the float image, as before.
    - On the last band, the dialog opens with the pixel-derived `has_alpha`, so greying shows what the encoder will do. OK stores the settings, then the image is encoded and written. Cancel returns `userCanceledErr` before anything is written (WriteFinish then has no state and returns noErr).
    - Scripts and batches with dialogs off still skip the dialog.
  - SDK basis for UI inside the write: the PiPL key docs say formats that "show UI after the formatSelectorWriteStart entry should not allow background save". The plugin does not claim `SupportsBackgroundSave`, so UI there is allowed. On WriteStart "the file to be written is open, and the file pointer is positioned at the start of the file". The SDK does not say whether the host truncated it; the plugin writes only after the encode (DC27). Cost: the dialog appears only after Photoshop has handed over the image (a moment for 16k). Open question for the owner: whether Photoshop leaves an empty file when Cancel is pressed during a save to a new name.
  - Tests: `pixels_test` `save_alpha_rule` (8/16-bit edges). `host_test` `dialog_after_pixels` plays the dialog from a second thread: an opaque layer document has an editable filter and a Kaiser pick gives different bytes from Box; alpha 254/255 counts as opaque; real transparency greys the filter at Box; Cancel returns -128 and the existing file is unchanged.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DC42, 2026-10-25)
- **DC38 Save As lists Tessalith DDS; a layered save reads Photoshop's merged composite through channel ports (owner probe 2026-10-25; supersedes DC29's revert and restores DC26's PiPL key).** Scope: `F:\ai\dds\photoshop-plugin\2026-10-25-layer-merge-scope.md` (Route A); probe branch `probe/layer-merge` d8d38e55, kept as a record.
  - Probe evidence (Photoshop 26.9, 16-bit RGB 4096 x 4096, layered documents): with `FormatLayerSupport` the format is listed under plain File > Save As. Every layered save had `layerData` = layer count on OptionsStart and WriteStart, a non-null `documentInfo` (version 0..6, mode RGB48, depth 16, layers 5), `channelPortProcs` version 1 with `readPixelsProc`, and merged Red/Green/Blue `ReadChannelDesc`s at depth 16 with the document's bounds (tiles 728 x 722); 4 x 4 test reads succeeded. `mergedTransparency` was null (those documents had a Background layer). Host order: OptionsStart, WriteStart, one WriteLayerStart ("Background"), WriteFinish. Merged and target reads use the same scale (an opaque white target layer read 32768; a single-layer document's merged and target samples were equal), so 16-bit ports are 0..32768 like the FormatRecord bands. A second build adding `FormatLayerSupportReadOnly` (`slRO`) kept the band path but was **not** listed under Save As, so it was dropped.
  - SDK basis: PIFormat.h `documentInfo` "The document info for the document being written", `channelPortProcs` "Suite for passing pixels through channel ports"; PIGeneral.h `mergedCompositeChannels` / `mergedTransparency`, `ReadPixelsProc` (equal source and destination rectangles: unscaled), `PixelMemoryDesc` `colBits` (each channel is read straight into its slot of the interleaved band).
  - PiPL.json: `"FormatLayerSupport": true`. ReadStart sets `layerData = 0`, so Open still gives one composite; the six layer selectors answer noErr with no pixels.
  - Save with `layerData > 0` (Save As and Save a Copy, Layers on or off): WriteStart reads the merged composite in bands of up to 64 MiB through `readPixelsProc` at the document depth, converts with the band path's `to_rgba_floats` (8-bit /255, 16-bit /32768), then runs the last-band step unchanged (alpha from the pixels DC31, Discard transparency DC36, the dialog after the pixels, encode, one write, `SetEndOfFile`) and leaves `theRect32` empty, so the host sends no WriteContinue. Alpha source, the same policy as the band path: `mergedTransparency`, else the first non-spot entry of `alphaChannels`, else none. Cancel and every error return from WriteStart, which frees the save state itself (WriteFinish follows only a successful WriteStart).
  - `layerData == 0`: the band path as before.
  - Refused with DC27's "could not read the image from Photoshop, so nothing was saved", file untouched: no `documentInfo`, no channel ports, fewer than three merged colour channels, a channel without a port or with another depth or size than the image, a failed or short read. A fault inside the host's read is caught (SEH) and fails the save. The trace (DC28) names the reason and, on WriteStart, describes `documentInfo` and the merged, merged-transparency and alpha channels.
  - Memory: unchanged; the float image (4 GiB at 16384²) dominates and the band buffer is reused for the port reads.
  - Tests: `host_test` `merged_composite_save`: 8-bit merged save with no bands asked; 16-bit 0/32768 checks give the same DDS as 8-bit 0/255 checks (pins 32768 = white); 16-bit merged equals the 16-bit band path on the same samples; an alpha channel listed after a spot channel gives the same DDS as merged transparency and differs from opaque; a failed read, no `documentInfo`, a depth mismatch and a missing merged channel each return the error with a 20,000-byte existing file unchanged.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC6, 2026-10-29)
- **DC40 Review fixes for Route A and presets (2026-10-25).**
  - Layered save with no alpha source: not refused. Real traces (4096x4096, 16-bit, 5 to 10 layers, always a Background layer) show `mergedTransparency` null, `transparencyPlane` 0 and `alphas` 0 even for layered documents, so none of those fields can tell "no Background layer" from "Background present"; every probed document had a Background. `ReadLayerDesc::transparency` (null for a Background layer) is the one candidate but is unobserved. So the save keeps writing an opaque DDS; WriteStart traces "layered save without an alpha source" with transparencyPlane, layers and alphas, and the `doc` trace line now lists the first 8 layers (`layerlist {"name" T0/T1}`). Upgrade to a refusal once a trace of an erased, Background-less document shows a reliable field. Owner step: save such a document and check the transparent area. (Superseded by Note FC6.3: the save still writes opaque, but no longer silently; the Save dialog says so.)
  - A preset file that is not read in full (open fails other than not-found, over 1 MiB, short read) is never written: `LoadPresets` reports it (`PresetFileInfo::complete`), Save preset and Delete show "The preset file could not be read, so presets can't be changed". A missing file counts as complete (seeds). Lines that do not parse (typo, invalid UTF-8, future fields, comments) are kept verbatim and written back after the parsed presets.
  - The dialog remembers the picked preset and keeps showing it while its effective settings match; Delete and Save use it; an edit falls back to the first match. Discard transparency is ignored in the match when the document has no alpha.
  - WriteStart's early refusals reset the per-save flags (`have_settings`, `show_dialog`) like every other end of a save. CleanPresetName's NBSP and U+3000 are written as escapes (no dependence on the compiler code page). `make_dirs` skips the `\\server\share` prefix so a UNC %APPDATA% works.
  - Tests: `dialog_test` `presets_file_is_protected` (locked and oversized files incomplete, bad lines survive a save), `preset_names_and_matching` (picked twin, discard without alpha, escapes). Not tested: the dialog's Save/Delete message and the WriteStart flag reset (no harness reaches them), UNC creation.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC6, 2026-10-29)
- **DC44 PiPL allows 24 channels in RGB modes, so Save As lists documents with alpha channels (owner report 2026-10-25; follows DC26's second lever).** Owner: after DC42 the alpha channel saves correctly, but Tessalith DDS no longer shows in plain Save As for that document. `FormatMaxChannels` was 4 for RGB and RGB48, and a document with Alpha 1 has more channels than that, so Photoshop's Save As (which lists only formats that keep the whole document) leaves the format out. Intel Texture Works declares 24 for every mode (DC26), and it is listed. Both RGB entries are now 24, and the other modes stay 0 (EnableInfo limits the modes). Cost: Save As no longer warns that alpha channels after the first are dropped; the first one is the DDS alpha (DC42). To undo, set the entries back to 4. (Cost superseded by Note FC6.4: the Save dialog now gives that warning itself.)

## Note DD — Shared psdds.dll for host plugins (owner, 2026-10-25)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DF2, 2026-10-25)
- **DD2 ABI version 2, exact match.** `PSDDS_ABI_VERSION` is 2 and `psdds_abi_version()` returns it; `psdds_version()` returns the workspace (engine) version as static UTF-8. A host refuses a DLL whose version is not exactly its own. The header's bump rule: any change to its declarations or documented behaviour. 2 is the header with the 7-field `PsddsSettings` (DC36), the `psdds_preset_*` exports (DC35) and `psdds_resolve`'s DC39 behaviour (sRGB sets only the tag, so an opaque Colour save keeps an editable filter), plus this branch's additions; 1 was the 6-field struct and never shipped (S10; cost if wrong: a renumber). DC39 changed documented behaviour after S10 set the baseline, but no build exposing the earlier behaviour has shipped, so 2 is kept. Each host ships its own copy of the DLL, so exact match costs nothing. No Windows version resource, no cancel hook and no BGRA8 entry point (R18; DC7 stands until the Paint.NET plan measures a problem). Export count: 11 from the Photoshop plugin plus 9 new = 20. (Amended by DF2: ABI 3.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DF3, 2026-10-25)
- **DD5 One clamp rule, in psdds.** `clamp01` (NaN to 0, -inf to 0, +inf to 1, else clamp to [0,1]) is applied by `psdds_encode` in its existing copy and by `psdds_rmaos_encode` to every source and the normal. The engine does not clamp: `pack_rmaos` briefly did (Task 5) but the file path never does, so it was removed in Task 6 to keep the packing rule single (M8; this supersedes R6's sentence that the engine clamps). In-range input is unchanged, so Photoshop's bytes cannot change. Pinned by `tests/clamp.rs` (RED before: the engine does not clamp level 0 or the mips averaging). (Amended by DF3: for a 32-bit Colour document, `psdds_encode_depth` keeps RGB above 1.0 only for BC6H and RGBA16F (the engine's float path handles NaN, negatives and the half range) and clips it to 0..1 for the 8-bit formats (DF3). Depth 8 and 16 and every old export still clamp, so their bytes are unchanged.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DF4, 2026-10-25)
- **DD6 The host helpers are the one copy of the host-side rules.** `psdds_check_size(w, h, err, cap)` is the size rule (DC12 text for a side over 16384; a zero side says "This image is empty"); `psdds_encode`, `psdds_decode` and `psdds_rmaos_encode` call it and return its text unchanged (M3). `psdds_dds_openable` is the openable rule ported from `pixels.cpp` with its test cases. It and `psdds_decode` refuse a `len` over `isize::MAX` (0, or an error) before building a slice, like the alpha helpers, so a caller's bad length cannot abort the host (final review 10). `psdds_opens_with_alpha` is DC23 (open: alpha byte below 254), `psdds_alpha_used` is DC31 (save: some alpha below 254/255, limit `254/255 - 1e-6`; the result goes to `psdds_encode`'s `has_alpha` and to `psdds_resolve`), and `psdds_max_file_bytes` is the bound a host applies before reading a file (16384^2 RGBA8 with the full chain, plus magic and headers; `psdds_decode` does not check it). Photoshop's `pixels.cpp` functions are one-line calls into them and `pixels_test` links `psdds.lib`; every other host takes its has_alpha from the same calls, so a Tessalith BC7 with alpha 254 opens opaque and re-saves opaque everywhere. `psdds_max_side` was declined (R1): the refusal wording is a rule too. Cost: Photoshop's `kMaxDdsFileBytes` stays a compile-time constant and a Rust test pins the same number; `decode` calls `check_size` only for headers with both sides non-zero, and keeps `read_dds`'s own error for empty or truncated headers; the one side over 16384 and the other 0 case gives that read error instead of the size text. DC12's size text and DC23/DC31's thresholds are unchanged: this note moves their implementation, not their wording. (Amended by DF4: `psdds_dds_openable_float` is the sibling rule that also claims the float formats `psdds_decode_depth` opens; `psdds_dds_openable` is unchanged.) (Amended by Note FC6.2: the hosts add the alpha 254-255 note on top of `psdds_alpha_used`; the rule and the ABI are unchanged.)

## Note DE — chaiNNer nodes (owner, 2026-10-25)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC10, 2026-10-29)
- **DE1 Distribution.** A drop-in folder `tessalith_dds` inside `packages/chaiNNer_standard`, because chaiNNer imports every `.py` under that package and imports nothing from a package it was not told about. `chainner\install.ps1` copies the nodes, `psdds.dll` and the notices there. It builds the folder beside the target and swaps it in, and checks the staged DLL (psdds version and ABI, through chaiNNer's own Python) before the swap, so a failed copy or a wrong DLL leaves the old install in place. It refuses while chaiNNer is running. **Re-run it after every chaiNNer update:** an update installs a new `app-<version>` folder and deletes the old one with the nodes in it. Cost: chaiNNer has no setting for another node folder, so there is no way around the re-run. An upstream pull request stays a later option. (superseded by FC10)

## Note DF — Photoshop plugin: 16-bit, 32-bit and HDR (owner, 2026-10-25)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DE23, 2026-10-25)
- **DF8 chaiNNer nodes follow ABI 3.** `chainner/tessalith_dds/_psdds.py` now needs ABI 3 and its tests expect the new mismatch text and the Greyscale mask BC4 seed with discard 1. Discard transparency is effectively ticked for BC1, BC4 and BC5 in psdds (DF2); chaiNNer's own UI still shows the box editable, because the nodes add no rule (DE). Output bytes are unchanged, since R2 already dropped alpha for those formats.

## Note DI — Paint.NET DDS file type (owner, 2026-10-25)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DI37, 2026-10-25)
- **DI4 Checkboxes and layout (DC33, DC37, DC43).** sRGB and Mipmaps are checkboxes. Rows: Preset with Save preset and Delete; Texture type with sRGB; Format with Discard transparency under sRGB; Mipmaps with Filter; Quality. Texture type and Format lists have one width. Tab order is Preset, Save preset, Delete, Texture type, sRGB, Format, Discard transparency, Mipmaps, Filter, Quality (DI39 adds Alpha is transparency after Discard transparency, mnemonic A). Labels and mnemonics are `dialog.rc`'s (P, v, D, T, s, F, r, M, i, Q), including "Discard t&ransparency". Initial focus is on Preset. Message texts match `dialog.cpp` exactly, including its final full stop where it has one (Paint.NET adds none). A preset pick runs the full refresh (rebuild Format and Quality, then greying), as `dialog.cpp` does. Layout replaced by DI37 (one column).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC6, 2026-10-29)
- **DI9 Missing or mismatched psdds.dll fails when the Save dialog is built.** The widget constructor and `OnCreateDefaultSaveConfigTokenT` call psdds, so a missing or ABI-mismatched DLL stops the dialog from opening with a raw exception, not a clean save error. Decided in Task 6 and deferred to the owner check (Task 9); cost if wrong: a raw error text. .NET also caches a failed native load, so after the DLL is replaced Paint.NET must be restarted. The README says so. (Superseded by Note FC6.5: one plain message with the path and the expected ABI, in Paint.NET's own error box.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DI38, 2026-10-25)
- **DI13 Discard transparency (DC36, DC43).** A checkbox, always enabled (amended by DI38: ticked and greyed for BC1, BC4 and BC5), in the right column on the Format row directly under sRGB; the seventh raw value. Ticked, the encode is given `has_alpha = false` (psdds removes alpha anyway) and the pixel rule decides nothing. The preview still measures, so the widget has the value after an untick (the alternative, a second pass over up to 1 GiB of floats on a UI event, was declined). Layout replaced by DI37 (one column).

## Note DG — GIMP DDS export plug-in (owner, 2026-10-26)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DG17, 2026-10-26)
- **DG1 Route and ABI.** The plug-in is Python (GIMP 3.0's own interpreter) calling `psdds.dll` through `ctypes`, in the folder `plug-ins\tessalith-dds\` (`tessalith-dds.py`, `psdds_ctypes.py`, `tdds_pixels.py`, `psdds.dll`). The DLL is loaded by absolute path from the plug-in's own folder; one copy per install. On first use the wrapper calls `psdds_abi_version()` and refuses any value other than `EXPECTED_ABI` (3, Note DF2/DF3), with a short message. The environment variable `TESSALITH_PSDDS_DLL` overrides the DLL path; it exists for the tests only, and the run scripts restore it. No rule, number or message of psdds is copied into `gimp/` (greying, formats per type, seeds, alpha, clamp, size limits all come from psdds); a grep for `16384|254|1431655912|Max side` finds nothing there.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC6, 2026-10-29)
- **DG3 Priority -1 and delegation.** Load and export are registered with magic `0,string,DDS`, extension `dds`, priority -1, so Tessalith is the default `.dds` handler and "DDS image" stays selectable by type. GIMP has no runtime decline (an error from a load does not fall through), so the load procedure calls `file-dds-load` through the PDB for files `psdds_dds_openable` refuses (HDR, cube maps, arrays) and returns its result. Open is the top mip as 8-bit RGB(A); alpha of 254 or 255 everywhere opens opaque (`psdds_opens_with_alpha`, DC23). Open of HDR files stays with GIMP's DDS plug-in; psdds's DF4 float open is not used here. GIMP marks a loaded image dirty again after any load procedure returns (file-dds too), so this is not fixable in the plug-in. GIMP caches a registration until the `.py` file's mtime changes, so a missing or wrong-ABI DLL leaves export unregistered (printed to stderr) until the plug-in is reinstalled. (Amended by Note FC6.5: export registers anyway, and Open and Export return a message that names the DLL's path and the ABI the plug-in needs.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC6, 2026-10-29)
- **DG9 Run modes, owner decision 2026-10-26 (branch review I1).** INTERACTIVE shows the dialog; Cancel writes nothing. WITH_LAST_VALS (File > Overwrite, Export to) and NONINTERACTIVE show no dialog; GIMP loads its remembered values over the passed arguments (measured). **Overwrite and Export to run with the last values without a dialog, also for a freshly opened DDS** (review I1 pointed out that such an image has no per-image values, so GIMP falls back to the global last values: an opened BC5 normal map can be re-saved with the settings of the last diffuse; the Photoshop DC22 hazard). The owner chose to keep owner decision 4 and not to add a first-time dialog. Cost: that silent re-save. The README warns of it and names File > Export As. Task 7 checks it on an opened normal map. (Superseded by Note FC6.1 for a DDS opened in this session: its first Overwrite or Export to shows the dialog with the file's own settings.)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note DG17, 2026-10-26)
- **DG15 Package check.** `gimp\package.ps1` runs `crates\psdds\build.ps1`, stages, zips to `dist\TessalithDDS-GIMP-<workspace version>.zip` plus `.sha256.txt` (`dist\` is not committed), then asserts: the zip holds exactly that file list (every `.py` the plug-in folder has, which the plug-in imports); the packaged `psdds.dll` equals the built one byte for byte; `COPYING` is the full GPL-3.0 text and `LICENSE` the MIT text; the notices carry the MPL-2.0 text; and the packaged wrapper loads the packaged DLL, which proves `psdds_abi_version()` equals `EXPECTED_ABI` (3). It needs `cargo-about` and fails when it is missing. The DLL path is `target\psdds\x86_64-pc-windows-msvc\release\psdds.dll`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC12 and Note FC8, 2026-10-29)
- **DO5 All slots and the auto switch.** The list's **All slots** box lists `known_textures(None)`. A pick goes to `input_name::pick_slot`: the selector's slot when the scan saw the path there or never saw it in slots 1–8, else the lowest slot it was seen in. So a normal picked from "All slots" under Match 1 sets `match2` and moves the selector to Match 2; a diffuse picked under Match 2 renames the set. (superseded by FC12)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI17, 2026-11-01)
- **DO3 The empty-list line tells the truth.** When the scan has paths but none fall under the lock, the ▾ list says "No known paths under <prefix>: move the path lock left to see more." "No known paths yet" stays for an empty scan.

## Note DS — Cubemap support v1 (owner + builder, 2026-10-26)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC7, 2026-10-29)
- **DS12 Cube checks in inspect and validate (Task 10).** `cube::checks::inspect_issues(game_path, file)` adds four messages to `sdc inspect`'s header issues and, as `VAL-HEADER` warnings that fail the verdict, to `sdc validate`: a cube with one mip (width > 1), a 1x1 cube whose +Y texel is non-black, a cube in an `_SRGB` format, and a 2D file where a cubemap is expected (a `textures` component directly followed by `cubemaps`, so a `cubemaps` folder above `textures` does not count, or a stem ending `_e`, ASCII case-insensitive, either separator, Note BF). The last one changes validate verdicts for 2D `_e` and `cubemaps\` files: they now FAIL. This is intended, because the game reads those as cubes. Other 2D files are unchanged. The marker keeps its colour on +X and +Y black, so any `--color` reads as a dynamic marker and raises no issue. `sdc validate` judges a cube file as `cubemap` when no `--subtype` is given. (superseded by FC7)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC12, 2026-10-29)
- **DS26 A portrait photo is cropped, and the photo faces east (final review M9; amends DS16).** `photo_panorama` keeps the photo's middle `w` rows when it is taller than wide (`h > w`): its top and bottom are lost, with no warning, and there are no fill rows when `h >= w`. That stays the behaviour (a single photo is an approximation); the wiki's Single photo section now says so, tells the user to crop to choose, and says the photo's centre faces east (`cubemap.yaw = -90` turns it north). DS16's wording is amended. The wiki also lists `photo` (0.6) as a blur default in the Blur section, and Troubleshooting's DIM-SMALL row says a cube face under 4 cannot be acknowledged (DS20). (superseded by FC12)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **DS12 Cube checks in inspect and validate (Task 10).** `cube::checks::inspect_issues(game_path, file)` adds four messages to `sdc inspect`'s header issues and, as `VAL-HEADER` warnings that fail the verdict, to `sdc validate`: a cube with one mip (width > 1), a 1x1 cube whose +Y texel is non-black, a cube in an `_SRGB` format, and a 2D file where a cubemap is expected (a `textures` component directly followed by `cubemaps`, so a `cubemaps` folder above `textures` does not count, or a stem ending `_e`, ASCII case-insensitive, either separator, Note BF). The last one is a warning in 1.x: the verdict stays `ok` unless `--strict-cubemaps` makes it FAIL (Note FC7). Other 2D files are unchanged. The marker keeps its colour on +X and +Y black, so any `--color` reads as a dynamic marker and raises no issue. `sdc validate` judges a cube file as `cubemap` when no `--subtype` is given.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
