## Note DV — Existing Advanced Skin sets use skin_albedo (owner, 2026-10-28)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note DW, 2026-10-28)
- DV4. **Pins hold the file, not the sub-type (controller ruling on review issue 3).** On an Advanced Skin set the diffuse reads as `skin_albedo` unless the user set a non-diffuse sub-type, pinned or not. A pin (`lock = ["slotN"]`) holds which file fills the slot; it is GUI data that never reaches the engine. The GUI still writes nothing into a pinned slot (DU6's switch and DU7's revert skip it), but the engine's reading follows the shader. This supersedes DU9's and DU11's pin exception for the reading: pinning a diffuse, then choosing Advanced Skin, reads it `skin_albedo` (nothing written); a pinned diffuse that DU made `skin_albedo` keeps that written value when the shader changes. docs/gui.md says so under Pin slot and the Advanced skin card. A borrowed diffuse is not a member of the borrowing set in the engine, so it is never re-typed (as DU9).
  Superseded by DW.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC11, 2026-10-29)
- DV7. **Limits.** shortcut: `join_skin_wet` runs before set edits and explicit shaders, so an `x_wet` does not join a set that is skin only by its shader; move the explicit shaders before it if such a project shows up. Step 6's packing prediction still adds `rmaos` to the set's suffixes, as for a written `packing.recipe = "rfaos"`. The old `_rmaos.dds` stays in the export folder (as DR9). (superseded by FC11)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- DV7. **Limits.** shortcut: `join_skin_wet` runs before set edits and explicit shaders, so an `x_wet` does not join a set that is skin only by its shader; move the explicit shaders before it if such a project shows up. Step 6's packing prediction still adds `rmaos` to the set's suffixes, as for a written `packing.recipe = "rfaos"`. The old `_rmaos.dds` is listed in Review as a stale output (Note FC11).

## Note DY — Helper textures (owner + builder, 2026-10-28)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FB12, 2026-10-29)
- DY16. **GUI: the Helpers group and pill; W11 overturned for listing.** Every `plan.helpers` report is one `ItemKind::Helper` item, never a tile: a "Helpers (n)" group under the tiles, one dense row each (20 px thumbnail, name, role chip, "→ <targets>" or "not used", full text on hover; hand-set role chip `✎ <role>` in `status::AMBER`). Layer files are helper rows now (Note W11 overturned for listing; `rnm_notes` unchanged). A "Helpers <n>" pill on the filter row shows or hides the group, on by default, saved as `hide-helpers` in `gui.toml`. The column draws only the rows in view (`show_viewport`).
  - DY16a. The pill's AccessKit name is "Helpers filter"; its visible "Helpers <n>" is the node's value.
  - DY16b. A helper whose file failed to load shows `Failed(error)` (the report alone would read "not used").
  - DY16c. The helper's own `PackingInput` job's warnings move onto the helper item, so Problems still lists them.
  - DY16d. Helper rows ignore the Sort combo (always set key, then name); the search matches the item key as for tiles.
  - DY16e. "Not in a set" counts and filters helpers like any item.
  - DY16f. The Generate dialog's member checks (`from_normal.rs`) treat helper items as file-backed images.
  - DY16g. **Ctrl+A selects tiles only**, like the arrow keys (builder call, accepted by the reviewer). Mouse Shift-click ranges and the selection summary run over the tiles then the shown helper rows (`shown_keys()`). **Keyboard ranges stay on tiles** (controller ruling, re-review): `grid_move` uses its own tile-only order, so Shift+arrow from a helper anchor never pulls helper rows in.
  - DY16h. **Helper member chips on set cards stay as built** (controller ruling, Tasks 7 and 9; owner confirmed 2026-10-28): helper items are set members, so cards show layer and alpha-fill helpers as small member chips. Cost if wrong: one follow-up to hide them.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- DY8. **Sizes (Ruling 7).** A suggested alpha fill of another size is skipped and the helper is not used ("512x512, target 1024x1024: sizes differ"), so an existing project never starts failing. An assigned one turns the target into an Error job with PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH (`size_mismatch_warning`, both sizes). That helper counts as used (it went into the error) and the error job keeps `fill`, so the run, which never runs it, ends the helper with "filled into <output>" (builder calls, accepted). A size that changes between plan and run fails the target at load: "`<helper>`: size changed since planning; re-run". Packed channels keep today's rule (recipe Error).

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - shortcut: the filled analysis is not cached, so every re-plan loads each filled target and its helper again; the pass-1 thumbnail stays the unfilled source.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - shortcut: helper rows are mouse-only; keyboard navigation covers tiles.

## Note GC — Meshes view: flat NIF list (NIF team, 2026-10-29)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GC19, 2026-10-29)
- **GC14 Review fix 1: a row read never lands across a scan start.** `start()` drops a row read still out, because it may read the origins the scan disables; the lists on screen stay until the scan ends. A NIF a reload was holding is never selected while a scan runs; a scan that ends without completing (cancelled, failed, busy) looks for it in the lists on screen, and the next reload keeps holding it (`selected_nif.or(reselect)`). Test `a_row_read_held_across_a_scan_start_is_dropped_and_an_unfinished_scan_keeps_the_lists` ends the scan with Busy (a held `scan.lock`), the deterministic way to end one unfinished; a real cancel raced a three-NIF scan to completion.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **GC11 The visible list is cached (rule 9.1).** The key holds the row lists and the texture's users as `Arc`s compared with `Arc::ptr_eq` (held, so a freed list's address cannot come back under the cache), the picked mod's name, the chip and the search needle. The selection is not an input. `row_builds` (`#[doc(hidden)]`) counts builds; inputs changed in the same frame build once.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **GC2 Two full reads replace `winning_nifs`.** `Store::winners()` (each path's game-winning copy, by path) and `Store::losers()` (every other copy, by path then mod), each with its origin's name. No schema change. Measured on 100k paths (`winners_read_time_on_100k_paths`, `#[ignore]`): 100-111 ms debug, 95-117 ms release, over the ~100 ms bar, so the rows are read on a worker thread from a read-only store after the store is set and after every scan or reload; the list says "Reading the scan…" until they land, "No scan yet." with no store. `MeshesModel::hold_rows_read` (`#[doc(hidden)]`) is the test seam that holds the read open.
ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **GC5 Mod chips move onto the mesh rows.** **BSA**, **game Data** and **Generated by PGPatcher** follow the mod name in each row's chips; `DERIVED_HOVER` went with the tree. `mod_chips` scans `mods` per drawn row (a `shortcut:`; only on-screen rows are drawn).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ6 and Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **GC16 Review fix 3: every chip of a row stays reachable.** A row draws its chips warnings first: Problem, the out-of-date patch, LE, the edit badge, then the status, PGPatcher and the mod's own chips (generated by PGPatcher, BSA, game Data). Three long chips show short in the row: "Patched by PGPatcher (in game)" as "In game", "PGPatcher patch out of date" as "Stale patch", "Generated by PGPatcher" as "Generated"; each keeps its full text as its AccessKit name (rule 8.1). The cell still clips what does not fit, so hovering anywhere on the chips lists every chip in full, plus the copies line ("Overridden by B · 3 copies") and the problem's text (rules 3.4, 9.3); that one hover replaces the status chip's and the Problem chip's own. Test `a_patched_stale_row_keeps_its_warning_in_sight_and_its_hover_lists_every_chip` checks that each warning's rect holds its whole shown text (a cut label is elided, so its rect shrinks), since egui keeps no clipped rect for a plain label.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **GC5 Mod chips move onto the mesh rows.** **BSA** and **game Data** follow the mod name in each row's chips; `DERIVED_HOVER` went with the tree. `mod_chips` scans `mods` per drawn row (a `shortcut:`; only on-screen rows are drawn).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ6 and Note GJ8, 2026-11-02)
- **GC16 Review fix 3: every chip of a row stays reachable.** A row draws its chips warnings first: Problem, LE, the edit badge, then the status, PGPatcher and the mod's own chips (BSA, game Data). The long status chip "Patched by PGPatcher (in game)" shows short in the row as "In game" and keeps its full text as its AccessKit name (rule 8.1). The cell still clips what does not fit, so hovering anywhere on the chips lists every chip in full, plus the copies line ("Overridden by B · 3 copies") and the problem's text (rules 3.4, 9.3); that one hover replaces the status chip's and the Problem chip's own. Test `a_patched_row_keeps_its_warning_in_sight_and_its_hover_lists_every_chip` checks that each warning's rect holds its whole shown text (a cut label is elided, so its rect shrinks), since egui keeps no clipped rect for a plain label.

## Note FB — GUI review C fixes and owner redesign (main team, 2026-10-29)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC4, 2026-10-29)
- **FB5 Crash edits restore themselves on the next open (review C3, amendment E; supersedes AR6 and BY3, and amends AR7: no banner except for a read-only document; owner, 2026-10-29).** A crash's `.recovery` file is loaded into its document when the folder opens (and on Reload): `crash_log::recovery_text(file, doc_text)` gives `Pending(text)` only when the file is newer than its document (or the document is gone), is not empty or whitespace-only, and differs, with line endings read as `\n`, from both the file on disk and the loaded document. `AppModel::apply_recovery` restores each `Pending` text through `restore` as one Undo step `Restore crash edits`, so the document is dirty exactly when it differs from the saved file (FB3), and toasts `Restored unsaved edits from the crash` with an `Undo` button (a new optional `ToastAction` on a toast; its Area takes clicks only while a toast has one). The toast's Undo is `undo_crash_restore`: it takes the step back and deletes the `.recovery` files of the documents that really changed (a file that does not parse is never deleted), because the user turned the edits down; once another edit sits on top of the step it changes nothing and says so in a toast. A plain Ctrl+Z keeps the file, so the next open restores it again. BY3 is superseded as a ruling; its save rule is restated here: only a user's save deletes the file, and the automatic saves keep it. An empty or equal file is deleted at once, with no toast and no step; an older one is left alone. A read-only document (one that does not parse) is not touched: its file stays and the read-only banner names it, `Unsaved edits from a crash are in sdc.toml.recovery; they come back when sdc.toml is fixed and reloaded.` Fixing the file by hand makes it newer than the `.recovery` file, so a document that was read-only while its file waited skips the age test on the next Reload (`held_recovery`), which keeps that sentence true. The device-loss dialog now says `Unsaved edits were kept and come back the next time you open the folder.` (the FC plan amends it later). `shortcut:` a `.recovery` file that does not parse as TOML restores nothing, shows nothing and stays on disk; give it a notice if one is ever seen. (amended by FC4)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI5, 2026-11-01)
- **FB1 No inherited export folder; the header Export is neutral until the project is clean (review C2, amendment B; supersedes BV6's fallback clause and A12's dot-only state, owner, 2026-10-29).** `AppModel::export_folder_chosen()` is the folder the user chose for this project: with a usable project document (loaded and not read-only) its `[project] export_folder` and nothing else, so the `gui.toml` value from another mod is never inherited; without one (no folder open, or an `sdc-project.toml` that does not parse) the pick lives in `gui.toml` as BV6 says, and that counts as chosen, or the user could never export. `export_root()` is that folder, else `<root>/sdc-out`, which is now only a name: `review_gate` returns `No export folder chosen` right after the read-only check, Review's Blockers column draws it as a red row with a `Choose…` button, and a click on the header Export with no folder chosen opens Review instead of exporting a clean project into `sdc-out`. The header button reads `Review {n} files` in the plain button colour while `export_is_clean()` is false (the gate is not Ok, anything is undecided, planning or a vote is still running, or there are no files to write), and `Export {n} files`, accent-filled, once Review would write without skipping anything (`header_export_text`; an empty or still-planning project is never clean). The DDS header patch and the JSON editor's path need a chosen folder too: `patch_dds_header` returns `No export folder chosen` and writes nothing, the Patch buttons are off with that reason, and the JSON editor shows the same words instead of a `sdc-out` path. A12's green state dot is deleted; the hover now also says what Review still has open. A click still opens Review (CA1), which a clean project skips as before (C2). The `Export to: …` chip is drawn only when a folder is chosen; until then the same menu is a plain `More ▾` (it also holds the marker cube, the Also ticks and the JSON editor, so hiding it whole would have taken the marker cube out of reach); its `Open export folder` entry is off with the reason. NIF edits need a chosen export folder too (`nif_export_root` is `Some` only when `export_folder_chosen()` is `Some`, so they fall back to the MO2 `sdc NIF Edits` mod instead of `<root>\sdc-out`; the NIF team's GD5 builds on it). Every export-folder dialog (the menu and the Blockers row) opens in the app-wide folder saved in `gui.toml`, if there is one (`PickerSpec::start_in`): a pick in a normal project goes to its `[project] export_folder` and never updates that value, so this is not a last-pick memory. Tests: `tests/export_folder.rs`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FB8 No codes in the UI: rows and banners say what to do; the code is on the hover and in the Messages log (review C10, C12, amendment D; supersedes the code text of A1's Problems rows (owner, 2026-10-29)).** A Problems row shows `code_sentence(code)` (`model::code_text`, in the GUI crate; the engine's `codes.rs` and the schemas are untouched): one table with a sentence that names the action for every Warning, Blocking and Error code (a unit test fails for a new code without one; Info codes never get a row and have none). `LOSS-ALPHA-DATA` reads "This alpha channel carries data the format would drop — choose what it means". A code with no sentence shows the engine's message with any code taken out (`without_codes`: a leading `CODE: ` goes with its colon). The row's hover is the engine's own message and, under it, the code (`Problem::codes_text`); the small grey code at the row's right edge is gone. A failed texture's row (`Failed: …`) and a decision row (`Needs a decision: …`) name their codes in words, and the codes they stand for are kept in the new `Problem::also` for the hover. The same sentence replaces the bare code on Review's acknowledge row and in a texture's "Needs a decision" box (code on its hover), and in the settings panel's `Error:` line. Banners: `refresh_banner` shows `without_codes` of every line, and logs each line that names a code to the Messages tab as the engine wrote it, once per appearance (`banner_logged`). C12: the five alpha meanings share `settings_panel::alpha_hint` (Remove, Cutout, Blend, Cutout + Blend, Data; Data's says the alpha is kept as data, written as it is and not as see-through) as the hover of every alpha button: the Decide… menu in Problems, Review's inline buttons and the settings box. The Alpha field combo in the settings grid has no per-option hover and keeps none. Round 1 (review): no panel prints a code any more. The settings panel's "Warnings" list, the set editor's and the RNM section's warning lines show `shown_for(code, message)` (the sentence, else the engine's message without its code) with the message and the code on the hover (`status::code_hover`, the one place a code is printed in a panel); the two Info lines beside "Treat as OpenGL (flip green)" (`IN-GREEN-UNSTATED`, `CHK-GREEN-GUESS`) show their message and keep the code on the hover; the Advanced drawer's NVTT line says "acknowledge its version warning"; a texture that cannot be previewed gives the engine's reason without its code; the DDS header patch result line is shown without its code (hover and Messages log keep it, `patch_dds_header` now logs it). A banner line that leads with a code shows that code's sentence and then the engine's detail (`code_text::banner_line`); the log entry takes its severity from the code (Info, Warning for Warning and Blocking, Error). `without_codes` also drops the backticks around a code and the doubled space it leaves. The Alpha field combo (settings grid, preset editor) shows `alpha_hint` on each option (`combo_for`), so the five meanings have one source everywhere. What still prints a code, and why: the Files tab's warning hover, the sets column's and the tiles' tooltips, and the new hovers (hover-only); the Messages log (by design); the Troubleshooting wiki catalogue and docs (reference text); the Meshes view (NIF team). Tests: `tests/no_codes.rs` (`no_code_text_in_problems_rows`, `no_code_text_in_the_banner`, `a_failed_row_and_a_decide_row_say_it_in_words`, `every_decide_entry_has_a_hover`, `the_settings_panel_and_the_set_editor_say_it_in_words`, `the_alpha_combo_options_have_hovers`) and the `code_text` unit tests; `a_failed_item_is_a_red_row`, `the_checks_filter_shows_only_check_rows`, the OpenGL hint test in `checks.rs` and the header patch test in `ui_smoke.rs` now expect the words.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - **Problems rows.** `Problem` gains `suggested`, a Decide row's suggested sub-type (CA11's row model). A row with one draws `[Set type: {type} ✓] ▾` (two buttons with no gap, in reading order in the right-to-left fix area): the main part decides the texture as the suggestion; ▾ opens the picker and keeps the accessible name *Set type…*, since it is what the plain button was. A row without a suggestion keeps the plain **Set type…**, which opens the picker. Decide… rows stay menus: the engine's alpha question carries no suggested answer (`Need::AlphaMeaning` has none), so no Decide row has one to accept; the split applies the day it does.

## Note GE — pgpsim simulates PGPatcher 2.3.1 (NIF team, 2026-10-29)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GE10, 2026-10-29)
- **GE2 `nif_filter` exception.** `shape_matches` ignores `nif_filter` when the mesh path, `/` folded to `\` and ASCII lower-cased, starts with `meshes\actors\character\facegendata\facegeom\` or `calientetools\bodyslide\shapedata\` [Src 2.2.0 TP:354-358, 593-596; NU:866-880; measured T5a, T8]. modscan's re-check calls the same fn. The Textures view's set preview still pre-filters scan rows by `nif_filter` in `set_preview::group_rows` (not changed; sdc's own entries rarely carry a filter, and the scan has no BodySlide ShapeData) (superseded by GE10).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GE11, 2026-10-29)
- **GE5 Wrong-typed match keys are `SimResult::Error` (R4).** Chosen over `Skip` with a flag: the outcome differs (the run stops), so the type says so and every match on `SimResult` must handle it. Text `PGPatcher 2.3 crashes on this JSON: <key> is not a <type>` (`pgpsim::CRASHES`), measured: a non-string `match_diffuse` throws at load (T1a), `path_contains` per shape (T1b), and both stop the whole run with empty output. Keys: `texture`, `match_normal`, `match_diffuse`, `rename`, `path_contains`, `nif_filter`, `match1..8`, checked before the texture check. `coat_color` as an object of 3+ keys in a multilayer entry throws at apply (T6), so it is checked after the texture check and not for a `delete` entry. In game (Meshes edit) draws the line red (`is_error`) as `In game: <text> (<file>).` and stops at that file: no lower file wins and the shape keeps its edited material. Limits: like the old `Skip`, only an entry that matches the shape by another key is seen (a lone `{"match_diffuse": 5}` matches nothing), and a crashing file below the file that patches the shape is not seen (a `shortcut:` in `pg_entries.rs`) (superseded by GE11). The set preview maps `Error` like `Skip`; sdc's own entries never carry a wrong type.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **GE16 Final review fixes (ruling G).** M1: the crash text carries its article, `<key> is not a string` or `coat_color is not an array` (the `<key> is not a <type>` form in GE5 and GE11 reads that way). M2: a crashing run never reverts, so a PG_STOCK shape gets no amber revert line, only the crash lines (test `a_crashing_json_drops_the_pg_stock_revert_line`). M3: `app.rs` keeps frames coming (the visible-worker poll) while `MeshesModel::pg_reading()`, so the set preview's red lines appear when the read lands on an idle Textures view. No test: a pending read cannot be held from outside `MeshesModel` without a test hook, and the line is one more operand in the poll list that `v2_jobs_poll.rs` already covers for another worker. M8: GE2 and GE5 carry superseded markers.

## Note FC — DECISIONS review fixes (main team, 2026-10-29)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FC14, 2026-10-30)
- **FC10 Hand remedies become row buttons (theme 10; supersedes T8's alias copy and hand rename, P8's bare read-only, item 27, CN43's "fix or delete", X10's "tag by hand" and X3's hidden picker, BQ15's hand-set key, CV8's "remove it by hand", DE1's silent loss; owner, 2026-10-29).** Each button writes on its own click only (Review Focus 3), never on draw, Enter or a plan; a refusal goes to the status line.
  - **T8.** `presets export` copies the preset's text as loaded with `toml_edit`, which is now an `engine` dependency (`"0.25"`, the gui's requirement, already in `Cargo.lock`; fix round 1, controller ruling). It sets `id` in place, keeping that line's comment, and removes only `[suffix_aliases]`, so comments and order stay and the copy loads beside the original. A preset the schema migration changed has no kept text; it is re-serialised from its migrated table, also without aliases. `PresetRegistry` keeps the text only when the migration left the table unchanged. This supersedes Note T8's "copies `suffix_aliases` as they are" and its help line. Also in the GUI: a CFG-PRESET-SKIPPED whose reason is an alias clash is a Warning row `Preset {id} clashes with {other} on aliases` with `Remove aliases from {id}`: it deletes that file's `suffix_aliases` table through `toml_edit` (comments and order kept), writes with `write_atomic` and plans again. Only a file directly in the user presets folder, and refused while an export runs (`EXPORT_RUNNING`, as `Reset also.json`). This button and `Clear read-only flag` write to disk, so they answer a pointer click only: `problems::click_only_button`, the FC5 guard without the red outline, ignores Enter and Space on a focused button. A test presses both keys, and swapping the guard for a plain button fails it. `shortcut:` the id and the other preset are parsed from the engine's message (`presets::check_aliases`), pinned by `bottom::tests::alias_clash_reads_the_engine_reason`.
  - **P8.** An unreadable `sdc-project.toml` is a red (Blocks) Project row `sdc-project.toml cannot be read: {first line of the parser's error}`, the whole error on hover, with `Open in editor` (`ShellRequest::Open`, the OS default program) and `Reload` (`request_reload`, which asks first when something is unsaved). `fix_button` now returns the fix it asks for, so one row can have two buttons. The row's Reload is named `Reload sdc-project.toml` for AccessKit (rule 8.4): the banner keeps its own Reload and its line, because the sets column and editors read `banner` as their disabled reason. Sets stay read-only as P8 rules.
  - **Item 27.** A save refused by the read-only flag (`SaveError::ReadOnlyFlag`) raises no dialog: it sets the status line `{file} is read-only: not saved`, opens Problems and leaves a Warning Project row `{file} is read-only: changes cannot be saved` with `Clear read-only flag`. The flag is read with the banner (open, reload, save), two stats, never per frame. The button clears the attribute only on this project's `sdc.toml` or `sdc-project.toml` (the path is checked against the documents), then reloads the documents when nothing is unsaved. With unsaved edits it does not reload (that would drop the edits the refused save was for): they stay, and the status line says to Save. A deviation from the brief's "then reloads", on the safe side.
  - **CN43.** `also_finished` loads the PBR folder's record once per Also export (`OwnedFiles::load`; ALSO-OWNED-UNREADABLE has no job, so it reached no row). An unreadable one is a Warning row, code ALSO-OWNED-UNREADABLE, whose sentence is now `also.json cannot be read — Reset also.json starts a new record`, with `Reset also.json`: outlined red and ignoring Enter and Space (FC5), greyed while an export runs. It deletes only `<PBR folder>/_sdc/also.json` as the model computes it, and only while that is the record found unreadable. Ruling (controller): the manifest holds no Also outputs; after Reset each one asks once. `also.json` records only the files written outside the manifests (report, zips, sums; CN39), so nothing rebuilds it. The next Also export starts an empty record and adds what it writes. Each report, zip or sums file already there is foreign: `may_replace` keeps it with a note, which is what "asks" means here, as CN39 rules. The row's hover and gui.md say exactly that. The engine's ALSO-OWNED-UNREADABLE note now ends "until it is reset: use Reset also.json on its row in Problems (or delete the file)". `also_finished` reads `folders.first()`, not `[0]`. The engine is unchanged (`also/mod.rs` gets a comment).
  - **X10, X3.** `policy::offered_for(target, subtype, grass)` offers `complex_grass` where `policy::grass_context(job)` holds: the set's shader is `complex_grass` or `pbr_grass`, or the game path does not start with `textures/` (no `textures` folder above the file, so the GRAS auto-detect can never tag it). `offered` is `offered_for(.., false)`, unchanged. Every sub-type list passes the item's context: the type picker (the texture's Sub-type field and Problems' Set type…), and through `settings_panel::offered_groups(target, grass)` the Undecided box's combo and Review's undecided-row combo (fix round 1). A diffuse whose analysis passes `grass::looks_like_grass` (1:2, the detection corner, a normal-map bottom half) is an Info row `Looks like complex grass, but is not tagged as grass` with `Tag as grass`, which writes `subtype = "complex_grass"` through `edit_subtype` (an explicit user value, one undo step). The trigger is the analysis, not the file name: a grass-named file of another shape gets no row.
  - **BQ15.** `alpha.exr_straight` (already a config field; no schema change) has a `Straight alpha` tick box in the Alpha group, only for an `.exr` source (`fields::exr_straight_view`; not in `FIELDS`, so bulk edits never list it). An `.exr` source with varying alpha and the field off is an Info row `Colour divided by alpha on load (premultiplied EXR)` with `Straight alpha`, which sets the field.
  - **CV8.** A set's stored `match{n} = ""` (n 2 to 8; `match1 = ""` is the diffuse-off toggle) is an Info row `match{n} is empty and matches nothing` with `Remove match` (removes the key; greyed while `sdc-project.toml` is read-only).
  - **DE1.** README line only. The nodes live in chaiNNer's `app-<version>` folder, which an update deletes, and chaiNNer has no setting for a node folder outside it; so no code of ours survives to check a marker, and `install.ps1` writes none. The README and the Host-Plugins page say the group simply disappears and is the sign to re-run `install.ps1`. No Python change.
  - **Tests.** `engine/tests/preset_export.rs::preset_export_keeps_comments_and_drops_aliases` (RED first); `presets::export_round_trips_and_drops_aliases` (was `..._keeps_aliases`). `tests/by_hand_rows.rs`: `clear_read_only_writes_only_on_click` (Windows; Enter and Space first; the flag is cleared by a drop guard), `a_bad_project_file_is_a_row_with_open_in_editor_and_reload`, `reset_also_json_only_under_sdc_and_writes_a_new_record`, `every_subtype_combo_offers_complex_grass_only_in_a_grass_context`, `tag_as_grass_writes_the_subtype`, `straight_alpha_writes_the_key`, `remove_match_clears_the_key`, `an_alias_clash_row_removes_the_copys_aliases`; `engine/tests/complex_grass.rs` pins `offered_for`. `sets_doc::a_non_conflict_save_error_names_the_file` now expects no dialog and the status line.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI4, 2026-11-01)
- **FC1 The GUI's automatic decisions are recorded in the project (theme 1; amends U3/U5: High auto-accepts and evidence-driven sub-type and alpha decisions are recorded in the project; supersedes AM14's fallback banner; CN59's `scanned: false` stands as intended (no modscan in sdc.exe, owner 2026-10-29); the CLI accept default stays `none` (owner, 2026-10-29)).** When a plan chain settles (`finish_plan`, after `settle_types`), `record_auto_decisions` (`model/auto_record.rs`) writes what the plan decided on its own into `sdc.toml` (`set_file_value`, inside one `batch_edit`): every image or DDS item (not packed into another, not skipped) whose sub-type is an accepted High suggestion (`fields::auto_subtype_reason`; mesh-vote shader members and GRAS grass are High suggestions too, so `pbr_grass`/`complex_grass` evidence is covered) gets `subtype`, and every item whose `alpha.policy` came from the meshes (`mesh:alpha`) gets `alpha.policy` and, when the meshes set it, `alpha.threshold`. Only a field with no entry yet is written. It is one undo step, `Set {n} types automatically` (`values` when an alpha value is among them; a threshold rides on its policy and is not counted), and one Info row with the same count, `{n} types set automatically (High confidence)`, with **Undo this change** while the step is the top one and every `key: value` line on hover (`Problem.detail`, new). Nothing written, no step and no row. The document is dirty after the first plan that decides.
  - **Fix round 1 (controller rulings R1-R4, binding; supersede the advisor's "stays automatic" design of the first commit).**
    - **R1 A recorded value is a plain project value**, exactly as if the user had accepted it. The GUI plans from the document's text, so `sdc convert` on the saved file plans the same value with the same provenance (`project_file`), and the manifests match. Later evidence (a scan, a mesh vote) never rewrites a recorded value; it shows as the existing suggestion and decision UI shows it for an explicit value.
    - **R5 (fix round 2, amends R1) User actions re-type an untouched recorded value.** A recorded sub-type is untouched while it equals what auto-accept picks from the plan (`auto_pick`: the job's top name-based High suggestion, which the engine keeps beside a `[files]` sub-type, with or without the set's current shader's `member_subtype`). The test is read from the plan, so it holds after a reopen. For three user actions such a value counts as not set, as before FC1: a set-shader pick writes, in the shader's own step, the new type of the untouched members it re-types into `[files]`, locked members included (fix round 3 below); PBR follow switches an untouched member like an inferred one (`follow_of`); and saving the presets (`presets_saved`, which the presets window calls) remembers the untouched values, and the settled plan writes the new pick for those whose pick changed (a new alias), as `key: old → new` in the recording's step. A value that differs from the pick is the user's and is kept. Later scan evidence (mesh votes, GRAS) still never rewrites a recorded value. Fix round 3: the CLI never reads a set's shader (`sdc-project.toml`), so a shader pick writes the untouched members' new type (`eye_n: msn`) into `[files]` in its own step instead of clearing it (a member in a locked slot too, since the engine's shader re-type ignored locks before FC1 and the lock guards only its encoding, `a_texture_in_a_locked_slot_is_untouched_and_reported`; this replaces round 2's lock exception), and choosing Auto re-types too: the untouched members (equal to the pick under the shader being left) whose pick without a shader differs are cleared and dropped from `auto_written`, so the next settled plan records what it picks then. The pending presets re-type lives in the history, so every history reset drops it, and only a read-only project loses it (a running export or a crash restore on top keeps it for the next recording). Tests: `a_shader_pick_records_the_member_type_it_re_types`, `choosing_auto_re_types_an_untouched_member_after_a_reopen` (both check the CLI plan of the saved text), `pbr_follow_retypes_an_untouched_recorded_member`, `encoding_follows_type.rs` and `a_saved_remap_reaches_the_plan_without_a_restart` with accept High; `a_mesh_vote_at_the_first_plan_is_what_gets_recorded`. The Z11 scan test in `complex_grass.rs` still accepts suggestions only from the scan's plan, since under R1 and R5 a scan never re-types a recorded value.
    - **R1 No loop.** `History::auto_written` holds every `(key, field)` written under this history, and a held one is not written again, so the plan the recording starts writes nothing (test: exactly one step after the plan settles) and an Undo of the step stays undone. A new history (open, reload) starts over.
    - **R2 Its own step.** The recording is always its own undo step, never folded into the user's edit that led to it; the row's Undo (`UndoLast`) takes back only that step, while it is the top one (test `a_recording_after_a_user_edit_is_its_own_step`). Nothing is recorded while an export runs, or while the crash-restore step its toast can still undo is on top (Note FB5; `crash_restore_on_top`, which identifies the step by its depth and the `history.pushes` it left, not by its label).
    - **R3 Open owner item: a mesh `remove` is not recorded.** A mesh `remove` on a varying-alpha colour map is not recorded (`shortcut:` comment): written as an explicit policy it stops cs-compact's opaque-colour rules (owner O1, `policy.rs` `mesh_remove`), so the format would change; `sdc convert` asks for that file's alpha meaning. The owner decides whether to record it once the engine treats an explicit `remove` like the mesh's. The env-map `_m` derivation (CN59/CN60) has no project field and stays GUI-only; this closes CN59's open question.
    - **R4 No `TagAsGrass` yet.** The grass remedy (X10/X11), its row and the X3 picker change are Task 10's (FC10); this note adds no `Fix` arm for it.
  - **AM14.** The GUI still plans with the fallback so the window is not empty, but `review_gate()` returns `Preset {id} is not installed` (checked before the blocker count, so `export_is_clean` is false) and Problems shows a red Project row with that text and **Choose preset…**, which opens the Preset menu under the header's Preset combo: a popup the header owns (`egui::Popup::menu`, the same menu body), since egui 0.36 has no public way to open a combo. The request carries its pass number and is dropped after two passes (as FC3's `focus_request`). The banner line is gone. `missing_preset()` runs once per `replan` (the gate asks every frame). The CLI keeps exit 2.
  - **`sdc.toml` with comments only.** toml_edit keeps such a file's text as trailing decor and wrote the first `[files]` entry above it; `entry_mut` now moves it in front of the new entry and `drop_entry` puts it back, so the user's first line stays first.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI7, 2026-11-01)
  - **NIF edits.** `app.rs` hands `AppModel::set_nif_review_rows` the NIF rows each frame (the NIF team's line). Review lists `NIF edits (n)` at the top of its footer, so between the Warnings column above it and the files rows: `path · mod · n unused blocks removed · written to <export folder>`. A blocked row is a warning instead: the amber `NIF edit skipped: {reason}` in the Warnings column, counted in `Warnings (n)`, never blocking the export. The NIF footer's `Review…` only sets `open_review_request`; `AppModel::poll` opens Review on the next frame. A NIF row opens Review on Export and keeps `export_is_clean` false, since Review's Export is the only way NIF edits are written. Review's NIF answer goes out as `nif_answer_request`; `close_review` sets `nif_clear_answers_request` (GD29), except while a run Review started still owes the NIF export (a Reload mid-export), since those answers are that export's (rule 7.4). Review's Export arms `nif_export_queued`; a cancelled run clears it; `after_export` (the one end of both the plain and the Also run, after its zips) makes `take_nif_export_due()` true once. A failed texture export still writes the NIF edits (they do not depend on it). `nif_export_result` writes a Messages line and an export note (Review is closed by then). The header Export hover no longer mentions Export NIF.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FI17, 2026-11-01)
  - **One accent.** The RNM outline and the slot flash use `visuals.selection.stroke` (teal); `RNM_OUTLINE` is gone (the flash already did). The path lock (`input_name.rs`, the rail and handle) keeps no blue: it is a control, not a status (red blocks, amber warns, green is ready; a lock's position says nothing about readiness), so it takes the accent, lightened on hover and darkened while dragged. The `LOCK_*` constants are gone.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FC3 Anything the user must act on is a Problems row with its action; checks are one count, not amber (theme 3; supersedes Y4's "no notice", CN67/CN20, CO70/CO71, CL5/CL7's "never rows", DP7d's "no button", DT35's hint, R-fills-2's card note, Z11's status line; amends CL18: checks are not amber and not ready signals; owner, 2026-10-29).** The rule: a result is a Problems row when it has a consequence the user must act on, and its action is the row's button. Info with no action stays off the list (hover and Messages only). Engine code levels do not change (`codes.rs` untouched); the GUI's row table in `model/bottom.rs` decides. `Problem.missing` is folded into `Problem.fix: Option<Fix>`.
  - **Levels.** `Level::Info` is added; like `Level::Check` it never counts against ready (`counts_as_ready_problem`); the Problems tab number stays the count of every row shown, Check and Info rows included (fix round 1 ruling). `At::Nif(path)` is added. Check and Info rows draw a weak-colour dot (`Status::Check`, `Status::Info`, labelled "check" and "info"), not amber. The separate `Checks {n}` button is gone: Problems shows one count, and a `Checks` pill toggles the check filter (it keeps a set filter: Set ↔ SetChecks).
  - **Rows added, with their buttons.** `IN-UNREADABLE` (engine Warning, collected into `PlanView.unreadable`) is a Project row with **Open folder** (the parent folder parsed from the message; no button when it cannot be parsed). `DERIVE-NO-ENVMAP` / `DERIVE-NIF-SUGGEST` (engine Info) are Warning rows on the export's file rows with **Open report** (the Vanilla/CM report path, `AppModel.also_report`, cleared with `also_written`), or **Show in Messages** (`Fix::ShowMessages`) when no report was written, so the row still carries an action (fix round 1). `GEN-STALE` is a Warning row on the generated map (`At::Texture`) with **Regenerate…** (`GEN-REPLACED` and `GEN-AO` stay off the list); it now makes its set's dot not ready: this supersedes CO71's ready part (CO71 kept the set ready while a generated map was stale). `CHK-GREEN-GUESS` is a check row with **Treat as OpenGL**. The last-export `CHAN-NORMAL-LENGTH` row keeps **Rebuild Z**, greyed with "Rebuild Z is on for this file: export again to rewrite it" once `reconstruct_z` is set. The cube-layout row gets **Set layout ▾** inline (the same menu as the Undecided row; DT35's hint line is deleted). P13/P26 PGPatcher path blockers get **Fix path** (selects the set and focuses that field), and the P26 feature clash gets **Turn {feature} off**. R-fills-2's dropped fills and Z11's cleared format are `Level::Info` rows (the set-editor card note and the header status line are deleted) that persist until undone or re-planned, with **Undo this change** only while their step is the top of the history (`History.pushes` counter; the Problems cache keys on it). Each new code has a sentence in FB8's `code_sentence` table.
  - **NIF rows.** `AppModel::set_nif_problems(Arc<Vec<NifProblem>>)` stores the Meshes view's problems; the Problems cache invalidates on a new `Arc` (`Arc::ptr_eq`). `NifLevel::Blocks/Warning/Info` map to `Level::Blocks/Warning/Info`, `at: At::Nif(path)`, fix `ShowNifTab(path, tab, field)` (**Open the {tab} tab**), which sets `AppModel.nif_tab_request`. The `app.rs` line that feeds `set_nif_problems` from `MeshesModel::nif_problems()` is the NIF team's (GD); this note adds only the AppModel side. NIF Info rows with a `ShowNifTab` action are rows, per the plan (`Info → Level::Info`; fix round 1 ruling); the NIF team has a handoff line to update `worklist.rs`'s doc comment, which still says Info is hover and Messages only.
  - **Writes only on their own click (Review Focus 3).** Every fix button is off while a plan is in flight or the file it writes is read-only (`fix_off`), and Enter never runs one. **Regenerate…** writes nothing itself: it opens the generate dialog with that map ticked (test `the_stale_row_regenerates_only_from_its_own_click` checks the file bytes are unchanged).
  - **Deviations from the brief.** `Fix` has only the arms this task constructs (clippy denies dead variants): `RemoveMissing`, `Regenerate`, `RebuildZ`, `OpenReport`, `ShowMessages` (a DERIVE row with no report written), `OpenFolder` (added: IN-UNREADABLE needs a folder, not a report), `TreatAsOpenGl`, `TurnFeatureOff(set id, Feature)`, `FixPath(set key, field)` (the field is needed to focus the right box), `UndoLast`, `ShowNifTab`. Decide, Acknowledge, Go to set and the cube layout menu stay drawn from the row's level and place (`RowPick::Layout` applies the layout), so no `Decide`/`Acknowledge`/`GoToSet`/`SetCubeLayout`/`ChangeType` arm; tasks 1, 10 and 11 add theirs. **Fix path** focuses the PGPatcher text fields and match boxes. No test reaches IN-UNREADABLE through a real plan (an unreadable folder cannot be made portably on Windows); the row is tested from a planted warning. Tests: `tests/problems_rows.rs`, plus the FC3 tests in `checks.rs`, `bottom_panel.rs`, `cubemap_gui.rs`, `generate_from_normal.rs`, `packing_fills.rs`, `encoding_follows_type.rs`, `set_cleanup.rs`.
  - **Fix round 1 (review of FC3).** A `Fix path` request is one-shot: it carries its pass number and is dropped once taken or after 10 passes (`pgp_fields::focus_request`), and it opens the Advanced group only for a field that group draws (`pgp::prefix::is_path_key`: `rename`, `match1`-`match8`), so the group can be collapsed again. A `slotN` path error has no box to focus and gets no `FixPath`: its row offers **Go to set**. A set row with a fix (the P26 clash, a missing set entry) keeps **Go to set** beside it. The NIF button names the edit panel's own tab (*Shader*, *Alpha*, *Controllers*, *Extra data*). **Regenerate…** on a map no longer in a set says so on the status line instead of doing nothing. `unreadable_folder` still parses the engine's `skipped: <path>: <error>` message (the engine sets no `data`; a unit test pins the shape; `shortcut:` comment). Tests restored: `checks_are_not_amber_and_not_blocking` asserts a green set dot and `export_is_clean()` on a one-check fixture; the stale test asserts the set was ready before (its rmaos defaults filled); the stale-row test holds the plan in flight unconditionally and presses Enter on the focused row.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FC7 An upgrade says what re-encodes and why; validate's cube check on a 2D file warns in 1.x (theme 7; amends DM8 with the notice; supersedes DS12's FAIL-by-default in 1.x; AG123/AG133, R-skin-1, DV1/DV6, DY10a/DY15, CL40 and CN3/CN4 are not re-ruled, they surface as counts; owner, 2026-10-29).**
  - **Engine.** `manifest::recode_causes(&Manifest, &Plan) -> Vec<RecodeCause { why, count }>` reads the manifest (`read`, never `load`'s compaction) and the plan's `Ready` jobs, and nothing else: no manifest field, golden or `encode_version` arm changes. Per job, first match wins: no manifest at all, or a record with no `encode_version` whose `engine_version` is not `1.0.0` (no baseline, Review Focus 5) is `FirstExport { version }`; a record whose encode version is not the sub-type's now (`api::encode_current`, a 1.0.0 record counting as 1.0.0) is `EncodeVersion { subtype, from, to }`; a record written by another engine version whose profile hash differs (`profile_sha256`, so the bake fold of R12/CL40 is not a change) is `ProfileChanged { subtype }`; a job with no record, in a manifest no record of which this engine wrote, is `NewOutput { subtype }`. Counts are per distinct cause.
  - **"After an upgrade" (controller ruling).** `ProfileChanged` counts per record, only against a record an older engine wrote; `NewOutput` counts manifest-wide, only while no record of the manifest was written by this engine. A record keeps its engine version until that file next re-encodes, so after an upgrade that changed nothing for a file, a later settings edit to it is still listed as "their settings changed" until it re-encodes once; a new texture goes quiet once this version has written any record. Owner item: refreshing a record's engine version on an up-to-date skip would close that, but it is a manifest write, out of FC7's scope. The CLI prints the lines only under `--on-existing if-changed`. A source edit is never a cause. Deviation from the brief: `RecodeWhy` has a fourth arm, `FirstExport`, since the no-manifest line has none of the three.
  - **The line.** `RecodeCause::line()`, one text for the GUI and the CLI: `{n} files re-encode: {why}` (`1 file re-encodes`), with `the {readable name} encoder changed in {to}` (the readable name is the sub-type id with spaces, as `subtype_label`: "the normal encoder changed in 1.1.0"), `their settings changed`, `new output type`; and `First export with {version}: {n} files`, never 0 (no ready job, no line).
  - **Review.** The plan computes the causes on its thread (`PlanView.recode`, one manifest read per plan; `AppModel.recode`). Each cause but `FirstExport` is a Warning ask at the top of `export_asks`, project-wide (no set heading, `At::Project` in Problems), so it has the existing tick and Acknowledge all, the header's Export opens Review for it, and the gate's `Acknowledge {k} warnings first` waits for it, as for any other warning (rule 7.3). The PGPatcher JSON editor's issues list stays the sets' pitfalls. `FirstExport` is a weak line at the top of the Warnings column, not an ask, so a new project's first export is not stopped (C2). The rows go when an export starts (`start_export_pass`); the next plan says what is left (`shortcut:` a cancelled export drops them until then).
  - **CLI.** `sdc convert` prints the same lines to stderr after the plan, before the run. `shortcut:` the Also path (`[export] also`) plans inside `run_also` and prints none.
  - **`sdc validate`.** The DS12 check a 2D file can raise (a 2D `_e` or `textures\cubemaps\` file) is a `VAL-HEADER` with severity `warning` on that instance (the code's level is unchanged) and keeps the verdict: `ok`, exit 0. `--strict-cubemaps` makes it FAIL (exit 4) as DS12 had it. Failing it by default is MAJOR (2.0, DM8). A cube file's own three checks (no mips, lit 1x1, sRGB) still fail: they judge a cube, not a 2D file.
  - **CN3/CN4.** A `*_curves`/`*_curvature`/`*_cavity` map no recipe reads (`PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` with `pack::ALSO_ONLY`, now a const) is an Info Problems row `Read only by Also export; not written on its own` with no button, instead of the generic "remove it" warning. The alias refusal reads `suffix alias '{alias}' is an exporter vocabulary term` (no spec section).
  - **Step 1.** Live: `validate` failed a 2D `_e` file (exit 4, `validate_warns_on_2d_e_files_by_default` RED); no re-encode notice in `crates/gui/src`; the CN3 row was not in place (the curves file was a Warning row with the generic sentence).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FC9 One word per concept, a Words section in `docs/gui.md`, one accent (theme 9; supersedes CA18/CD10/CD11/CA5's four words for "undecided", AN6's red X, CC9/CC10's "off" for a blocked feature, CE2/CF18's blues; CL15's last stray name goes with them; owner, 2026-10-29).**
  - **Undecided is the one word.** The Textures line reads `{n} undecided`, the pill `Undecided {n}`, a Problems row `Undecided: …`, the settings box and a tile's hover `Undecided`, an export row `skipped: undecided`; the buttons stay `Decide…`. The Sets pill `Needs attention` is `Not ready` (the opposite of `Ready`). The `gui.toml` filter key `needs-decision` is a stored name and stays.
  - **Sub-type labels from one table.** `model::labels::subtype_name` is the table (the id with spaces unless it says otherwise): `skin_rfaos` reads `Skin RFAOS`. `subtype_label` (UI) and `encoding_follow::type_name` (the Z11 note) both read it, so a note and a dropdown agree. The id is on hover in the type picker and the dropdowns. The picker's search lower-cases the label. `engine::manifest` (`recode_causes`, FC7) still writes the id with spaces for the CLI line ("the skin rfaos encoder changed"): the engine is not this task's, and the GUI text there is the engine's sentence; an owner item.
  - **Half size has one meaning.** FC2 left the Resize option `Half size (larger side above 512)` beside Review's answer `Half size` (which drops the top mip). The option now reads `Halve if larger than 512` (value `half` unchanged), so the words "Half size" mean Review's answer and the Advanced tick only. The check is `Low detail` (was `Fake resolution`, FC2's last stray name; code `CHK-LOW-DETAIL`, key `low_detail` unchanged); "mip 0" is out of the Advanced hover and stays in Review's hover (FC2). No test pinned the old Resize wording.
  - **The PGPatcher lock is not an error.** AN6's red X (a big ✕ over the slot thumbnail, a small ✕ on the Sets card chip) is a grey padlock over a dimmed thumbnail with the caption `kept by PGPatcher` (painted, so the row does not move; its AccessKit node is a Label named `kept by PGPatcher`, with the text as its value too since a Label's text is read from the value), and a small grey padlock on the chip. The hover texts keep their wording.
  - **A blocked feature is on.** CC9/CC10 read `off` for a feature that is on but blocked. Its group header now reads `on, blocked by {feature}` in amber (Subsurface: `Standard, blocked by hair`); the group stays closed and ticked. There is no Features count to change: FB2's C14 already removed it, so the state shows once, in the group's own header.
  - **One accent.** The RNM outline and the slot flash use `visuals.selection.stroke` (teal); `RNM_OUTLINE` is gone (the flash already did). The path lock that once took the accent is gone (Note FI17); the `LOCK_*` constants are gone.
  - **The glossary.** `docs/gui.md` has a `## Words` section: undecided, not ready, blocker, warning, check, pin, PGPatcher lock, field lock, half size, the Resize wording, set, helper, sub-type labels, one accent. The wiki GUI-Guide uses the new words (not published).
  - **Tests.** `tests/vocabulary.rs` (`no_stray_words` RED first, `the_pgpatcher_lock_reads_kept_by_pgpatcher`, `a_blocked_feature_reads_on_blocked_by`); the pins of the old words moved with them (`ui_smoke`, `textures_filter`, `sets_filter`, `problems`, `no_codes`, `bulk_ui`, `dialog_keys`, `helpers_list`, `bottom_panel`, `packing_fills`, `gui_guide_screenshot`).
  - **Step 1.** Live: every stray (`need a decision` at `columns.rs:309`, `Needs decision` at `:709`, `Needs a decision` at `bottom.rs:393` and `settings_panel.rs:845`, `Needs attention` at `sets_column.rs:409`, `skin rfaos`, the red ✕ in `set_editor.rs` and `columns.rs`, `RNM_OUTLINE`, `LOCK_BLUE`). The Meshes view was searched and left alone (NIF team): `ui/meshes.rs:21` has a `BLUE` constant (`from_rgb(90, 140, 220)`); owner item for the NIF team, no stray word is there.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FC11 Stale outputs Tessalith wrote can be deleted from Review; provenance and JSON hashes are recorded at write time (theme 11; supersedes AG90's "until the user deletes them", AT3/AA2/AD5's "left in place with a note", DR9/DV7's "by hand", CO39/CO51's next-Save provenance, BD2/P21's next-export ask; for JSON only the kinds listed under "JSON stale outputs" below; philosophy 4 is unchanged: only a file that still matches its baseline gets a button; owner, 2026-10-29).**
  - **The baseline is the stamp.** `ManifestRecord` has no output hash; its `output_stamp` (size and mtime, Note Y1) is the baseline. No manifest field is added. A file is ours when its stamp equals the recorded one; a record with no stamp is never ours.
  - **`engine::manifest::stale_outputs(recorded, plan, root) -> Vec<StaleOutput { path, reason, ours }>`.** A reader like FC7's `recode_causes` (`read`, never `load`'s compaction). It lists each record under `root` whose file still exists and that no job, skipped file or failed file of the plan claims (all statuses: an undecided texture's output is not stale), from a source under the plan's inputs, so another project's records in a shared export folder are left out. `StaleWhy::NotWritten` (the source is still there: a renamed set, AA2; a recipe switch's old `_rmaos`, DV7; a skin set's `_f`, DR9, or its old `textures\pbr\` path, AG90) or `SourceGone`. An unreadable manifest or a cancelled (partial) plan lists nothing. Keys are case-folded; a listed path is shown as spelled on disk (`manifest::on_disk_case`, a `canonicalize` kept only when it folds to the same key; a junction or short name keeps the folded spelling).
  - **Review.** The footer has `Stale outputs ({n})` under `NIF edits (n)`: one `Sides` row per file, `path · no longer written` (or `· its source is gone`), the full path on hover. An `ours` row has a red-outlined `Delete` (`problems::destructive_button`, now `pub(super)` with an optional accessible name, so each reads `Delete <path>`, rule 8.4): pointer click only, never Enter or Space, greyed with `EXPORT_RUNNING` while an export runs, never run by Export. A non-ours row reads `edited since Tessalith wrote it: left alone` (`review::STALE_NOT_OURS`) and has no button. `AppModel::delete_stale_output` deletes only a path the last plan listed as ours, refuses while an export runs, and reads the manifest again at the click: a stamp that no longer matches turns the row non-ours and keeps the file. The record stays in the manifest; the next plan does not list a file that is gone. Not a gate: `review_gate`, `export_is_clean` and the column counts are unchanged, and the rows do not open Review on their own. Reachability (fix round 1): while any `ours` row exists, Problems has one Info row at project level, `{n} old outputs Tessalith wrote are no longer exported` (`bottom::stale_row_text`), whose `Review…` (`Fix::ReviewStale`) opens Review. `AppModel.stale` is set by each plan (`PlanView.stale`, beside FC7's `recode`) and cleared when a folder opens.
  - **JSON stale outputs (fix round 1).** A JSON file with a recorded SHA-256 that the project no longer produces is a stale row too: a PGPatcher JSON under an earlier name or with no PBR set left (AG90), a CS PBR JSON no block binds (AD5), a complex-material `<output>.json` no set writes (AT3). The recorded hash is the baseline: the file still hashing to it is ours (Delete); otherwise the row reads `edited since Tessalith wrote it: left alone`. `AppModel::refresh_stale_json` builds them from the three exports' `prepare` and `history::orphans` (`stale_json` maps each row to its hash) when a plan settles and after an export's JSON step, never per frame; the click checks the hash again. A PGPatcher JSON entry (AG90) still leaves the JSON at the next export; its DDS under `textures\pbr\` is a manifest record and is listed.
  - **Not covered.** A legacy `last_json_sha256` with no per-name record (it cannot be tied to a path), the borrowed-slot `copy_sha256` records (their DDS copies are manifest-less and their hash is not tied to a plan output), and a DDS's `--sidecar` `.json` twin (no record). The export notes still name them as before.
  - **The sidecar.** `<project root>/_sdc/generated.json`, beside `sdc-project.toml` (generated maps are project inputs; the export root can be unset or change), a JSON object with the same keys as the document: `generated` (CO39's table) and the hash tables `last_json_sha256`, `pgp_json_sha256`, `cs_pbr_sha256`, `cm_json_sha256`, `copy_sha256`. `SetsDoc` writes it with `write_atomic` whenever one of those records is set (`set_generated`, `set_last_json_sha256`, `set_*_shas`), also while the document is read-only; it writes nothing when the value is unchanged. A missing, unreadable or unwritable sidecar degrades to the document's copy, as before: it never fails an open or an export.
  - **One source of truth.** The getters (`generated`, `last_json_sha256`, `pgp_json_shas`, `cs_pbr_shas`, `cm_json_shas`, `copy_shas`) read the sidecar's table first, so every existing hash check (PGPatcher, CS PBR, complex material, borrowed-slot copies) and the "generated here" test read it. Undo's `restore_keeping_json_sha` keeps the document's own copies (it no longer goes through the recording setters). A recorded value bumps the document's revision, so the caches keyed on it see it.
  - **Reconcile on open (quiet, fix round 1).** `SetsDoc::open` and `reload` (so also Discard) read the sidecar and copy each table it holds into the document through the doc-only setters, then mark the result saved: opening or discarding never leaves unsaved changes, and the readable copy in `sdc-project.toml` catches up at the next real Save. **The sidecar wins** over a hand edit of the same table in `sdc-project.toml` (owner): the hand edit is replaced in memory and at that Save.
  - **P21 superseded.** A read-only `sdc-project.toml` still gets the hash, in the sidecar: the note is `hash recorded in _sdc/generated.json only: sdc-project.toml is read-only` (`model::SIDECAR_ONLY`), for the PGPatcher, CS PBR, complex-material and copy records. BD2's "hash recorded; written with the next Save" note stays: it is about the readable copy.
  - **Tests.** `engine/tests/stale_outputs.rs` (`stale_outputs_lists_only_recorded_files`, `a_stamp_mismatch_or_no_stamp_is_not_ours`), `gui/tests/review_stale.rs` (`review_delete_removes_only_on_click` with Enter and Space on the focused button, `generate_then_close_without_save_is_still_ours`, `json_hash_survives_a_discard`); fix round 1: `stale_outputs::a_recipe_switch_lists_the_old_packed_output`, `review_stale::{a_stale_output_shows_a_problems_row_that_opens_review, a_pgpatcher_json_no_longer_made_is_listed_by_its_hash, the_sidecar_wins_and_reconcile_is_quiet}`, `json_hash_survives_a_discard` made deterministic (strips the toml's hash lines instead of racing the export; deleting the sidecar is its control), and the stale-row asserts in `cs_pbr_export::an_unbound_file_is_reported_not_deleted` and `cm_json::unticking_leaves_the_file_in_place_with_a_note`; `cm_json::a_read_only_document_records_no_hash` is now `a_read_only_document_records_the_hash_in_the_sidecar`, and `a_read_only_document_takes_no_cm_edit` checks the sidecar holds the hash.
  - **Step 1.** `ManifestRecord` (`manifest.rs:276-313`) still has no output hash. A renamed or dropped output kept its DDS with only a note, and Generate then close without Save lost the provenance: shown by the RED runs (no `stale_outputs`; the reopened model had no provenance; after a discard the PGPatcher JSON read as `Edited`).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FC12 No surprising defaults or silent data effects (theme 12; supersedes AG92/AG93, BN1's clear, CN45's skip-with-note in the GUI, DS26's 'no warning', DO5/DO6's auto-switch and unstored selector; CI23's routing stands, now labelled (owner, 2026-10-29); CM27 is the NIF team's, handled by GD6).**
  - **Cancel keeps the project (AG92, AG93).** `open_folder` moves the open folder's undo history and selection into `opening` (beside the previous folder and Recent) instead of clearing them; `history.pushes` carries on (FC3). `cancel_open` re-opens the previous folder as before and puts both back (`pushes` never goes back). A completed open drops them with `opening`. Cancel's hover: "Stops opening and goes back to the previous folder, with its selection and undo history".
  - **A config error keeps Undo (BN1).** When the engine rejects `sdc.toml` after an edit, `plan_failed` no longer clears the history: the top step's label gets ` (sdc.toml rejected it: {message})` (`model::REJECTED`, added once). `undo` of a step so labelled first lifts the blame (`ProjectDoc::blamed`, then `unblame`, `ignored_by_plan` off, banner refreshed), so the step's `before` lands in a usable document and the plan runs with it. When the retry without `sdc.toml` fails too (the user config's fault), the history also stays and the suffix is taken off again. A document rejected when it loads has no such step and is unchanged.
  - **Undo names its history (CI23).** `ui::header::undo_button(nif, undo, textures) -> UndoButton { text, name, hover, enabled }`, from GD4's `undo_target`: `Undo` / `Redo` for the Textures history (hover `Undo: {step}  (Ctrl+Z). Acts on the Textures history (sdc.toml and sdc-project.toml).`), `Undo NIF edit: {step}` / `Redo NIF edit: {step}` while a NIF edit is the target (hover ends `Acts on the NIF edit history; the Textures history is untouched.`; nothing to undo: `Nothing to undo in the NIF edit history`). A button text over 40 characters ends in `…`; the hover keeps it whole (rule 3.4). The AccessKit name is the text for the Textures history and `Undo (NIF edit history)` / `Redo (NIF edit history)` for the NIF one, so it never equals the NIF panel's own `Undo NIF edit` (rule 8.4; controller ruling, fix round 1). Ctrl+Z and the palette route as before. Tests that found the header button by the exact name `Undo` now scope to the header and also take the NIF name (`nif_edit.rs::header_button`).
  - **Zip limits are a Blockers row (CN45, CN93).** `engine::zip` is public: `estimate(sizes) -> ZipEstimate { entries, bytes }`, `ZipEstimate::fits()`, `MAX_ENTRIES` (65,535) and `MAX_BYTES` (`u32::MAX`), the limits `write_zip` already refused. The bytes are the outputs' uncompressed sizes, an upper bound for a Deflate zip and the size of a stored one. With **Also write a zip** on (`zip_on`: an Also tick and the zip setting), `AppModel::zip_estimate` counts every file `finish_also` puts in the PBR zip: the output of every item with a job (not NeedsDecision, PackedInto or Skipped; the zip takes this run's outputs by job key) at `out_size::estimate_job` from the sizes the plan read, plus the JSON the export writes into the folder (the PGPatcher JSON from `pbr_export::prepare`, the CS PBR and complex-material files from their `prepare`, at their text size; `_sdc` is never zipped). The entry count is exact; a test pins it to a real zip's. `review::zip_block(estimate, limits)` turns one at or over the limits into `Zip too large for the ZIP format ({n} files, {size}); untick "Also write a zip" or split the export`, a general row of `export_blocks`, so `review_gate` waits on it and nothing is written. `ViewKey` gains `zip` (`zip_on`), so the cached row follows the tick on the next frame. The limits are an `AppModel` field set to `MAX_ENTRIES`/`MAX_BYTES`, lowered only by tests (`set_zip_limits`). `write_zip`'s `TooLarge` and its note stay as the backstop. `shortcut:`s: only the PBR zip is counted (the Vanilla and CM zips hold at most about as many files, all derived from the PBR sets), an output with no size read and the PGPatcher JSON count 0 bytes; headers and Deflate growth are not counted. CN93's in-RAM build stands, with the trigger "a streaming writer when a user hits it". The CLI keeps CN45/CN49's behaviour (the zip is skipped with a note and exit 1): `sdc` plans each Also phase inside `run_also`, so it has no sizes before the write (controller ruling; owner item).
  - **A portrait photo is a Warnings row (DS26).** `pbr_export::export_asks` lists, after FC7's re-encode rows, `Only the middle {w}×{w} of {name} is used` (`name` is the texture's path, so two same-named photos are two acknowledgements) for each cube job with `cubemap.layout = "photo"` whose source (size from the plan's analysis) is taller than wide, with Acknowledge like every ask. `cube/layout.rs::crop` and `photo_panorama` are unchanged (the size alone tells), so the goldens are untouched.
  - **The Match selector stays put (DO5, DO6).** It starts from the match the user chose for the set, kept in `gui.toml` as `match_slots` keyed by `input_name::match_key(root, set id)` (the root with `/` and lower case, then `fold_id`), else from the set's lowest unused match (`active_matches`; Match 2 for a set whose only match is its derived diffuse; 1 when all eight are used). An unknown or removed set falls back to that. Its frame-to-frame value is egui memory keyed by the project root and the set id, so another folder's set with the same id reads its own value. Only the user moves it: a pick from **All slots** goes to the selected match (`pick_slot` is removed), and choosing another match writes `gui.toml` (through `save_settings`, `write_atomic`). `path_picker::list` asks for its rows' height (`min_scrolled_height`), so a list that grows inside the open menu (All slots ticked over a short slot list) is not clipped to the menu's last height: egui sizes a popup from its last frame, so the new rows sat outside the clip and a click on them closed the menu instead (found while writing the selector test; every picker gets the same fix).
  - **FC8 minor (ledger).** Undo and redo drop `weighting_follow` (`apply_snapshot`), so a weighting re-write queued for the plan of the step undone does not run over it.
  - **"Don't save" then Cancel.** `resolve_open_unsaved` drops the edits and clears the history before `open_folder`, so Cancel brings back no step whose state is gone (pinned by a test).
  - **Tests.** `gui/tests/open_cancel.rs` (`cancel_keeps_the_previous_project`, `a_completed_open_replaces_the_history`, `a_config_error_keeps_undo`, `undo_label_names_the_nif_history`), `engine/tests/zip_limits.rs` (`zip_over_the_limit_blocks_before_the_write`), `review_tab::{portrait_face_is_a_row, zip_over_the_limit_is_a_blocker_row}`, `input_name_edit::match_selector_starts_from_the_set_and_stays` (replaces DO5's `picking_a_normal_from_all_slots_sets_match2_and_switches_the_selector` and `a_pick_matches_in_the_slot_the_scan_saw_it_in`); `plan_progress` takes the new hover; two `input_name_edit` tests and `popups.rs` pick Match 1 first; `history::a_plan_rejection_clears_the_history` becomes `a_plan_rejection_keeps_the_history`; `nif_edit`'s panel-fit test finds exactly one `Undo NIF edit` (the panel's) and the header's `Undo (NIF edit history)`. Fix round 1: `open_cancel::cancel_after_dont_save_brings_back_no_dropped_step`, `also_export::a_zip_over_the_limit_blocks_and_its_estimate_counts_every_entry`, `input_name_edit::the_match_selector_is_per_project`; `portrait_face_is_a_row` uses two same-named photos.
  - **Step 1.** All six live at f045d01a: Cancel reset the history and selection (hover said so), `plan_failed` cleared the history, no zip estimate existed (`write_zip` refused at write time), no portrait row, the selector started at Match 1 and switched on a pick (the RED runs).

Failing it by default is MAJOR (2.0, DM8). A cube file's own three checks (no mips, lit 1x1, sRGB) still fail: they judge a cube, not a 2D file.

  - **T8.** `presets export` copies the preset's text as loaded with `toml_edit`, which is now an `engine` dependency (`"0.25"`, the gui's requirement, already in `Cargo.lock`; fix round 1, controller ruling). It sets `id` in place, keeping that line's comment, and removes only `[suffix_aliases]`, so comments and order stay and the copy loads beside the original.
