ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FC2 Half size is a Review question, answered before the export (theme 2; supersedes CL24, CL27 M2, CL43 and CL26's dialog; amends CL20: Hold measures only; CL15's names collapse to "Half size"; owner, 2026-10-29).**
  - **Engine.** `LowDetailMode::Hold` measures and records `CHK-LOW-DETAIL` but writes the full chain, byte for byte what an Off run writes (test `hold_measures_but_writes_the_full_chain`). An up-to-date output is still measured again (CL20 answer 2) and stays `up to date`, with the warning (`Processed::UpToDate(Option<Warning>)` replaces `Processed::Held`). `SkipKind::LowDetail` stays in the event schema so older logs parse, but it is no longer emitted; its doc line and the `schema/event.schema.json` description say so. Goldens and `encode_versions` are unchanged: they run Off, and Hold never wrote held files, so no golden covered them. The CLI has no low-detail flag (`BatchRequest::low_detail` stays `Off` in `sdc`), so CLI output does not change. GUI output does: with the switch on, a flagged texture used to be withheld and is now written at full size, the same bytes as the CLI (theme 1's parity). That is an output change for a released GUI setting, a semver point (DM8) for the owner; no version is bumped here. P10's "an Also export holds nothing" downgrade is gone with the hold: an Also export measures like any other.
  - **Where the rows come from.** `AppModel::half_size_rows()`: the last export's `CHK-LOW-DETAIL` records (the Files rows' warnings, the ones Problems already shows), each mapped to its texture and kept only while the current plan's job is still eligible (`engine::checks::low_detail_profile_eligible`: no resize, no `drop_top_mip`, colour or normal). So the first export of a file always writes full size, and a texture the plan no longer flags drops out before anything is written (Review Focus 2, test `an_unflagged_texture_gets_no_drop_top_mip`). A texture whose source changed since that export drops out too: the export keeps the source stamp of each job it ran on (`ExportModel::sources`), and a row whose job's stamp differs, or whose item is `changed_on_disk`, is not asked (review r1 #1, test `a_source_changed_since_the_export_gets_no_drop_top_mip`). Cached under the view key and the Problems inner key (rule 9.1). Only while Fake resolution and its switch are on (controller ruling): with the switch off, Fake resolution warns in Problems as before and Review asks nothing. That is who was asked before FC2, since only Hold held files; a default user gets no new blocking question.
  - **Review.** `Review.half_size: BTreeMap<String, Option<HalfAnswer>>` (`HalfAnswer { Half, Full }`), unanswered by default. A group in the Undecided column, `{n} textures added nothing at full size last export`, one row per texture: its name (a link that selects it) and a menu `<answer> ▾` with **Half size** and **Full size**; an unanswered row reads `Half or full size? ▾` (controller ruling: an unanswered row and a Half answer must look different). The menu's accessible name is `{key}: {answer}`, one per row (ui-rules 8.4). The code and "mip 0" are hover text. An answer writes nothing, so the menu is always on; only **Half size** is off while `sdc.toml` cannot be saved (`Review.half_unwritable`, from `sdc_toml_writable()`, taken when Review opens and on its Export, never per frame), with "{path} is marked read-only, so the half-size choice cannot be saved." on hover. A Half answer is refused then, and one given before the file became read-only is dropped on Export, so the row asks again; Full exports at full size (review r1 #2, test `a_read_only_sdc_toml_keeps_full_answerable_and_exports_full_size`). Unanswered rows count in `Undecided (n)`. `review_gate()` returns `Answer half or full size for {n} textures` after the acknowledgements (last: it is the one question only Review asks), so `export_is_clean()` is false while any row is unanswered, and the header's Export opens Review whenever there are rows.
  - **Export.** Review's Export first settles the plan and checks the sources (`continue_review`), then in `Continue::Review`, after the guards and the gate pass, writes each `Half` answer whose row is still asked as `[files."…"].drop_top_mip = true` through `set_file_value`, one undo step labelled *Half size* (`write_half_answers`, which also checks `toml_writable()`). It is a user answer, so it is an ordinary edit step; `History::auto_written` is not touched. The click then waits for the plan that edit starts, comes back to `Continue::Review`, runs the guards and the gate again on it (the written rows are no longer eligible, so nothing is written twice), and exports once. The answers persist as Review's other answers do: in the document, saved to `sdc.toml` by the save that starts every export (`start_export_pass`); a click the gate refuses writes nothing. When the plan after the write refuses the export (a guard, the gate, or a question opened meanwhile, `queued_export_dropped`), or Review is closed before the export starts, the *Half size* step is undone, provided it is still the top step (`Review.half_step` holds its undo depth and `history.pushes`); so Close writes nothing (review r1 #3, test `a_refused_or_closed_export_after_the_half_size_write_undoes_it`). `Full` writes nothing. Close and Esc write nothing (Esc does nothing in Review, N3). Answers are for that export only (the export takes the Review), so a texture answered Full is measured and asked again next time (CL20 answer 2).
  - **Removed.** The "Export at half size?" box and its held second pass: no dialog after the export, no second pass, no queued half-size continue, and no deferral of a close or open behind it. Stop during an export cancels at the next file (pinned by `stop_cancels_at_the_next_file`), and "Cancel and close" (`resolve_export_running(AfterExport::Close, true)`) cancels at the next file and then closes, as for any export (test `cancel_and_close_stops_the_export_at_the_next_file`). The dialogs' list-height constant is now `dialogs::DIALOG_LIST_SHARE` (was `HALF_SIZE_LIST_SHARE`; CO59 names the old one).
  - **Copy settings (CL26).** A saved half size is a tick like any other field (`Half size: true`); the "Copy the half size too?" dialog is gone (test `copy_settings_ticks_half_size`).
  - **Names (CL15).** "Half size" is the one name. The Advanced row is *Half size* (was *Half size (drop mip 0)*). The gui.toml switch keeps its key `[checks] drop_top_mip` and reads **Measure whether full size adds anything**: it picks Hold (measure up-to-date outputs too, and ask in Review) over Warn. `CHK-LOW-DETAIL` and "mip 0" are hover text. `sdc_toml_writable`'s read-only text keeps naming the half-size choice: it is the disabled Half's hover (the Also rows map it to their own text). The clash with the Resize option "Half size (larger side above 512)" goes to Task 9's vocabulary work (controller ruling).
  - **Step 1.** The Hold mapping had moved from `mod.rs:2013` to `start_export_pass`; the dialog (`dialogs.rs:62`) and the engine's withhold assertion (`low_detail.rs:128`) were live.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - **Overwrite answers.** `review_gate` ends with `Answer Keep or Overwrite for {name}` while an edited PGPatcher JSON (`JsonLine` `DiskState::Edited`) has no answer for its path and its current hash (`JsonAnswer::answers`: a file changed after the answer is asked again before the export; `JsonLine.sha` is read with the cached lines, so within 1 s), or a `NifReviewRow` with `foreign: Some(_)`, no `blocked` and no `answer` exists (`AppModel::unanswered_overwrites`, after the half-size question). So `export_is_clean` is false too. The question is a Blockers row (`<name> was edited outside Tessalith`, `<path> was not written by Tessalith`) with Review's two radios, neither preselected, and counts in `Blockers (n)` until answered; the footer's JSON lines show the state only. `Dialog::OverwriteJson`, `pending_text`, `resolve_overwrite_json` and `keep_pending_json` are gone: a JSON with no answer for its bytes when the export ends (changed during the export) is kept with the note `PGPatcher JSON not written: … export again to choose`, also a Messages warning. That also ends #44, #48 and N1's handling of the open question.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - **NIF edits.** `app.rs` hands `AppModel::set_nif_review_rows` the NIF rows each frame (the NIF team's line). Review lists `NIF edits (n)` at the top of its footer, so between the Warnings column above it and the files rows: `path · mod · n unused blocks removed · written to <export folder>`. A blocked row is a warning instead: the amber `NIF edit skipped: {reason}` in the Warnings column, counted in `Warnings (n)`, never blocking the export; a row blocked only by the missing export folder is instead listed under `NIF edits skipped until an export folder is chosen (n)` (Note FI7). The NIF footer's `Review…` only sets `open_review_request`; `AppModel::poll` opens Review on the next frame. A NIF row opens Review on Export and keeps `export_is_clean` false, since Review's Export is the only way NIF edits are written. Review's NIF answer goes out as `nif_answer_request`; `close_review` sets `nif_clear_answers_request` (GD29), except while a run Review started still owes the NIF export (a Reload mid-export), since those answers are that export's (rule 7.4). Review's Export arms `nif_export_queued`; a cancelled run clears it; `after_export` (the one end of both the plain and the Also run, after its zips) makes `take_nif_export_due()` true once. A failed texture export still writes the NIF edits (they do not depend on it). `nif_export_result` writes a Messages line and an export note (Review is closed by then). The header Export hover no longer mentions Export NIF.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FC11 Stale outputs Tessalith wrote can be deleted from Review; provenance and JSON hashes are recorded at write time (theme 11; supersedes AG90's "until the user deletes them", AT3/AA2/AD5's "left in place with a note", DR9/DV7's "by hand", CO39/CO51's next-Save provenance, BD2/P21's next-export ask; for JSON only the kinds listed under "JSON stale outputs" below; philosophy 4 is unchanged: only a file that still matches its baseline gets a button; owner, 2026-10-29).**
  - **The baseline is the stamp.** `ManifestRecord` has no output hash; its `output_stamp` (size and mtime, Note Y1) is the baseline. No manifest field is added. A file is ours when its stamp equals the recorded one; a record with no stamp is never ours.
  - **`engine::manifest::stale_outputs(recorded, plan, root) -> Vec<StaleOutput { path, reason, ours }>`.** A reader (`read`, never `load`'s compaction). It lists each record under `root` whose file still exists and that no job, skipped file or failed file of the plan claims (all statuses: an undecided texture's output is not stale), from a source under the plan's inputs, so another project's records in a shared export folder are left out. `StaleWhy::NotWritten` (the source is still there: a renamed set, AA2; a recipe switch's old `_rmaos`, DV7; a skin set's `_f`, DR9, or its old `textures\pbr\` path, AG90) or `SourceGone`. An unreadable manifest or a cancelled (partial) plan lists nothing. Keys are case-folded; a listed path is shown as spelled on disk (`manifest::on_disk_case`, a `canonicalize` kept only when it folds to the same key; a junction or short name keeps the folded spelling).
  - **Review.** The footer has `Stale outputs ({n})` under `NIF edits (n)`: one `Sides` row per file, `path · no longer written` (or `· its source is gone`), the full path on hover. An `ours` row has a red-outlined `Delete` (`problems::destructive_button`, now `pub(super)` with an optional accessible name, so each reads `Delete <path>`, rule 8.4): pointer click only, never Enter or Space, greyed with `EXPORT_RUNNING` while an export runs, never run by Export. A non-ours row reads `edited since Tessalith wrote it: left alone` (`review::STALE_NOT_OURS`) and has no button. `AppModel::delete_stale_output` deletes only a path the last plan listed as ours, refuses while an export runs, and reads the manifest again at the click: a stamp that no longer matches turns the row non-ours and keeps the file. The record stays in the manifest; the next plan does not list a file that is gone. Not a gate: `review_gate`, `export_is_clean` and the column counts are unchanged, and the rows do not open Review on their own. Reachability (fix round 1): while any `ours` row exists, Problems has one Info row at project level, `{n} old outputs Tessalith wrote are no longer exported` (`bottom::stale_row_text`), whose `Review…` (`Fix::ReviewStale`) opens Review. `AppModel.stale` is set by each plan (`PlanView.stale`) and cleared when a folder opens.
  - **JSON stale outputs (fix round 1).** A JSON file with a recorded SHA-256 that the project no longer produces is a stale row too: a PGPatcher JSON under an earlier name or with no PBR set left (AG90), a CS PBR JSON no block binds (AD5), a complex-material `<output>.json` no set writes (AT3). The recorded hash is the baseline: the file still hashing to it is ours (Delete); otherwise the row reads `edited since Tessalith wrote it: left alone`. `AppModel::refresh_stale_json` builds them from the three exports' `prepare` and `history::orphans` (`stale_json` maps each row to its hash) when a plan settles and after an export's JSON step, never per frame; the click checks the hash again. A PGPatcher JSON entry (AG90) still leaves the JSON at the next export; its DDS under `textures\pbr\` is a manifest record and is listed.
  - **Not covered.** A legacy `last_json_sha256` with no per-name record (it cannot be tied to a path), the borrowed-slot `copy_sha256` records (their DDS copies are manifest-less and their hash is not tied to a plan output), and a DDS's `--sidecar` `.json` twin (no record). The export notes still name them as before.
  - **The sidecar.** `<project root>/_sdc/generated.json`, beside `sdc-project.toml` (generated maps are project inputs; the export root can be unset or change), a JSON object with the same keys as the document: `generated` (CO39's table) and the hash tables `last_json_sha256`, `pgp_json_sha256`, `cs_pbr_sha256`, `cm_json_sha256`, `copy_sha256`. `SetsDoc` writes it with `write_atomic` whenever one of those records is set (`set_generated`, `set_last_json_sha256`, `set_*_shas`), also while the document is read-only; it writes nothing when the value is unchanged. A missing, unreadable or unwritable sidecar degrades to the document's copy, as before: it never fails an open or an export.
  - **One source of truth.** The getters (`generated`, `last_json_sha256`, `pgp_json_shas`, `cs_pbr_shas`, `cm_json_shas`, `copy_shas`) read the sidecar's table first, so every existing hash check (PGPatcher, CS PBR, complex material, borrowed-slot copies) and the "generated here" test read it. Undo's `restore_keeping_json_sha` keeps the document's own copies (it no longer goes through the recording setters). A recorded value bumps the document's revision, so the caches keyed on it see it.
  - **Reconcile on open (quiet, fix round 1).** `SetsDoc::open` and `reload` (so also Discard) read the sidecar and copy each table it holds into the document through the doc-only setters, then mark the result saved: opening or discarding never leaves unsaved changes, and the readable copy in `sdc-project.toml` catches up at the next real Save. **The sidecar wins** over a hand edit of the same table in `sdc-project.toml` (owner): the hand edit is replaced in memory and at that Save.
  - **P21 superseded.** A read-only `sdc-project.toml` still gets the hash, in the sidecar: the note is `hash recorded in _sdc/generated.json only: sdc-project.toml is read-only` (`model::SIDECAR_ONLY`), for the PGPatcher, CS PBR, complex-material and copy records. BD2's "hash recorded; written with the next Save" note stays: it is about the readable copy.
  - **Tests.** `engine/tests/stale_outputs.rs` (`stale_outputs_lists_only_recorded_files`, `a_stamp_mismatch_or_no_stamp_is_not_ours`), `gui/tests/review_stale.rs` (`review_delete_removes_only_on_click` with Enter and Space on the focused button, `generate_then_close_without_save_is_still_ours`, `json_hash_survives_a_discard`); fix round 1: `stale_outputs::a_recipe_switch_lists_the_old_packed_output`, `review_stale::{a_stale_output_shows_a_problems_row_that_opens_review, a_pgpatcher_json_no_longer_made_is_listed_by_its_hash, the_sidecar_wins_and_reconcile_is_quiet}`, `json_hash_survives_a_discard` made deterministic (strips the toml's hash lines instead of racing the export; deleting the sidecar is its control), and the stale-row asserts in `cs_pbr_export::an_unbound_file_is_reported_not_deleted` and `cm_json::unticking_leaves_the_file_in_place_with_a_note`; `cm_json::a_read_only_document_records_no_hash` is now `a_read_only_document_records_the_hash_in_the_sidecar`, and `a_read_only_document_takes_no_cm_edit` checks the sidecar holds the hash.
  - **Step 1.** `ManifestRecord` (`manifest.rs:276-313`) still has no output hash. A renamed or dropped output kept its DDS with only a note, and Generate then close without Save lost the provenance: shown by the RED runs (no `stale_outputs`; the reopened model had no provenance; after a discard the PGPatcher JSON read as `Edited`).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FC12 No surprising defaults or silent data effects (theme 12; supersedes AG92/AG93, BN1's clear, CN45's skip-with-note in the GUI, DS26's 'no warning', DO5/DO6's auto-switch and unstored selector; CI23's routing stands, now labelled (owner, 2026-10-29); CM27 is the NIF team's, handled by GD6).**
  - **Cancel keeps the project (AG92, AG93).** `open_folder` moves the open folder's undo history and selection into `opening` (beside the previous folder and Recent) instead of clearing them; `history.pushes` carries on (FC3). `cancel_open` re-opens the previous folder as before and puts both back (`pushes` never goes back). A completed open drops them with `opening`. Cancel's hover: "Stops opening and goes back to the previous folder, with its selection and undo history".
  - **A config error keeps Undo (BN1).** When the engine rejects `sdc.toml` after an edit, `plan_failed` no longer clears the history: the top step's label gets ` (sdc.toml rejected it: {message})` (`model::REJECTED`, added once). `undo` of a step so labelled first lifts the blame (`ProjectDoc::blamed`, then `unblame`, `ignored_by_plan` off, banner refreshed), so the step's `before` lands in a usable document and the plan runs with it. When the retry without `sdc.toml` fails too (the user config's fault), the history also stays and the suffix is taken off again. A document rejected when it loads has no such step and is unchanged.
  - **Undo names its history (CI23).** `ui::header::undo_button(nif, undo, textures) -> UndoButton { text, name, hover, enabled }`, from GD4's `undo_target`: `Undo` / `Redo` for the Textures history (hover `Undo: {step}  (Ctrl+Z). Acts on the Textures history (sdc.toml and sdc-project.toml).`), `Undo NIF edit: {step}` / `Redo NIF edit: {step}` while a NIF edit is the target (hover ends `Acts on the NIF edit history; the Textures history is untouched.`; nothing to undo: `Nothing to undo in the NIF edit history`). A button text over 40 characters ends in `…`; the hover keeps it whole (rule 3.4). The AccessKit name is the text for the Textures history and `Undo (NIF edit history)` / `Redo (NIF edit history)` for the NIF one, so it never equals the NIF panel's own `Undo NIF edit` (rule 8.4; controller ruling, fix round 1). Ctrl+Z and the palette route as before. Tests that found the header button by the exact name `Undo` now scope to the header and also take the NIF name (`nif_edit.rs::header_button`).
  - **A portrait photo is a Warnings row (DS26).** `pbr_export::export_asks` lists first `Only the middle {w}×{w} of {name} is used` (`name` is the texture's path, so two same-named photos are two acknowledgements) for each cube job with `cubemap.layout = "photo"` whose source (size from the plan's analysis) is taller than wide, with Acknowledge like every ask. `cube/layout.rs::crop` and `photo_panorama` are unchanged (the size alone tells), so the goldens are untouched.
  - **The Match selector stays put (DO5, DO6).** It starts from the match the user chose for the set, kept in `gui.toml` as `match_slots` keyed by `input_name::match_key(root, set id)` (the root with `/` and lower case, then `fold_id`), else from the set's lowest unused match (`active_matches`; Match 2 for a set whose only match is its derived diffuse; 1 when all eight are used). An unknown or removed set falls back to that. Its frame-to-frame value is egui memory keyed by the project root and the set id, so another folder's set with the same id reads its own value. Only the user moves it: a pick from **All slots** goes to the selected match (`pick_slot` is removed), and choosing another match writes `gui.toml` (through `save_settings`, `write_atomic`). `path_picker::list` asks for its rows' height (`min_scrolled_height`), so a list that grows inside the open menu (All slots ticked over a short slot list) is not clipped to the menu's last height: egui sizes a popup from its last frame, so the new rows sat outside the clip and a click on them closed the menu instead (found while writing the selector test; every picker gets the same fix).
  - **FC8 minor (ledger).** Undo and redo drop `weighting_follow` (`apply_snapshot`), so a weighting re-write queued for the plan of the step undone does not run over it.
  - **"Don't save" then Cancel.** `resolve_open_unsaved` drops the edits and clears the history before `open_folder`, so Cancel brings back no step whose state is gone (pinned by a test).
  - **Tests.** `gui/tests/open_cancel.rs` (`cancel_keeps_the_previous_project`, `a_completed_open_replaces_the_history`, `a_config_error_keeps_undo`, `undo_label_names_the_nif_history`), `review_tab::portrait_face_is_a_row`, `input_name_edit::match_selector_starts_from_the_set_and_stays` (replaces DO5's `picking_a_normal_from_all_slots_sets_match2_and_switches_the_selector` and `a_pick_matches_in_the_slot_the_scan_saw_it_in`); `plan_progress` takes the new hover; two `input_name_edit` tests and `popups.rs` pick Match 1 first; `history::a_plan_rejection_clears_the_history` becomes `a_plan_rejection_keeps_the_history`; `nif_edit`'s panel-fit test finds exactly one `Undo NIF edit` (the panel's) and the header's `Undo (NIF edit history)`. Fix round 1: `open_cancel::cancel_after_dont_save_brings_back_no_dropped_step`, `input_name_edit::the_match_selector_is_per_project`; `portrait_face_is_a_row` uses two same-named photos.
  - **Step 1.** All six live at f045d01a: Cancel reset the history and selection (hover said so), `plan_failed` cleared the history, no zip estimate existed (`write_zip` refused at write time), no portrait row, the selector started at Match 1 and switched on a pick (the RED runs).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FC2 Half size is an Advanced tick, not a Review question (theme 2; supersedes CL24, CL27 M2, CL43 and CL26's dialog; amends CL20: no Hold mode; CL15's names collapse to "Half size"; owner, 2026-10-29; Review's question and the Hold pieces went in Note FM24).**
  - **Engine.** `LowDetailMode` is `Off` or `Warn`: a flagged texture is written at full size either way, and `Warn` adds the `CHK-LOW-DETAIL` warning (test `warn_measures_but_writes_the_full_chain`). `SkipKind::LowDetail` stays in the event schema so older logs parse, but it is no longer emitted. The CLI has no low-detail flag, so CLI output does not change.
  - **Copy settings (CL26).** A saved half size is a tick like any other field (`Half size: true`); there is no dialog (test `copy_settings_ticks_half_size`).
  - **Names (CL15).** "Half size" is the one name. The Advanced row is *Half size* (was *Half size (drop mip 0)*). `CHK-LOW-DETAIL` and "mip 0" are hover text. The clash with the Resize option "Half size (larger side above 512)" goes to Task 9's vocabulary work (controller ruling).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - **Half size has one meaning.** FC2 left the Resize option `Half size (larger side above 512)` beside the Advanced tick `Half size` (which drops the top mip). The option now reads `Halve if larger than 512` (value `half` unchanged), so the words "Half size" mean the Advanced tick only. The check is `Low detail` (was `Fake resolution`, FC2's last stray name; code `CHK-LOW-DETAIL`, key `low_detail` unchanged); "mip 0" is out of the Advanced hover. No test pinned the old Resize wording.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FC8 egui-limit rulings become fixes or marked shortcuts (theme 8).** Supersedes CA14/BS7/BS14's refusal, AG32's Ctrl+S line, CA10's anchor, CP10's two picks and CE16's collapsed default; BN3's deferral is done; CF7, CG2, CE20 and CE21 stand as shortcuts with triggers (owner, 2026-10-29).
  - **One click saves or exports from a text field.** Save, the header Export and Review's Export blur a focused text field on the primary press (`ui::blur_on_press`), so a commit-on-focus-loss field commits on that frame and the release runs the click. A press and release in one frame (a touchpad tap, a slow frame) go through `ui::after_commit`: while a text field had the focus at the frame's start (`actions::focused_at_start`, recorded by `keys`) or has it now, it ends text input and queues the action on `AppModel::after_commit`, which `actions::keys` runs first thing next frame, after the field drew and committed. Never a second click. Save is live while a text field has the focus (that field may hold the only edit) and saves only if the commit left something unsaved. The "leave the text field first" texts are gone (status line, log, `header::field_focused`).
  - **Ctrl+S in a text field** (supersedes AG23's wait and AG32's status line) is consumed, ends the edit and saves next frame through `after_commit` if anything is unsaved; `keys` returns `"save"`. It is the one shortcut besides Ctrl+K that works from a field.
  - **BN3's deferral.** A click, Ctrl/Shift-click, double-click (expand) or a set card's select in the Textures or Sets column goes through `after_commit`, so a press and release in one frame commit the typed name before the selection changes. A right-click still selects at once (its context menu acts on it in the same frame). Every same-frame click while any text field had the focus (a search box too) now lands one frame later.
  - **Toasts clear the bottom panel (CA10).** `bottom::draw` records the open panel's top for this pass (`bottom::open_top`); the toast Area's anchor is lifted by the panel's height, so toasts sit above an open panel and never cover Review's Export; with the panel collapsed they keep the window's bottom-right corner. The Delete-set toast's Undo stays clickable.
  - **Linear once (CP10).** A BC7 weighting pick over an own `perceptual = true` or own non-linear weighting cannot see the inherited value under it. The pick is written as before (`fields::weighting_writes`, so nothing inherited still clears both), and `AppModel::weighting_follow` remembers it; when the next plan settles (`finish_plan`, before the automatic recording), a row that does not show the pick is written again over the now-visible inherited value, under the pick's own history merge key (`bc7-weighting#<pushes>`), so it is one undo step. Each pick has its own key, so two picks stay two steps. The same follow-up also completes a weighting pick over an own Perceptual with an inherited one (re-review N1).
  - **Preview settings open on wide windows (CE16).** `PreviewUi` decides once per preview: a window `preview::WIDE_WINDOW` (1280) or wider starts with the settings open (`settings_open`), so opening Advanced no longer collapses them; below 1280 they start as the strip as before. `ui::centre_floor` now takes the window into account: from 1280 the centre's floor is `PREVIEW_MIN + SETTINGS_WIDTH + 16` (776), so the columns and Advanced give way to it; with Advanced open at exactly 1280 the columns sit at their minimums and the picture is narrower than 480 (the floor cannot be met), which the ruling accepts.
  - **Shortcuts kept, each with a `shortcut:` comment at its code and its trigger:** CF7, a skipped Sets card has no AccessKit node or Tab stop (`sets_column.rs`; upgrade when egui offers nodes for off-screen rows); CG2, no New column in the rename table (`rename_dialog.rs`; upgrade when the dialog is resizable); CE20, an over-wide combo scrolls the preview settings sideways (`preview.rs`; give each combo an exact-width slot when those rows are next reworked); CE21, a window's saved size creeps 3 points per start (`layout.rs::window_rect`; fix when that clamp code is next touched).
  - **Tests:** `input_name_edit::{save_click_commits_the_field_and_saves, export_click_commits_the_field_and_runs, review_export_click_commits_the_field_and_runs, tap_in_the_same_frame_keeps_the_typed_name}` (the first and third in separate frames and in one; they replace the three `*_does_not_save_stale_value` pins), `keyboard::ctrl_s_in_a_field_saves` (replaces `ctrl_s_in_a_text_field_says_so_in_the_status_line`; `no_shortcut_fires_from_a_text_field_except_ctrl_k_and_ctrl_s` now expects `save`), `messages::toasts_sit_above_an_open_bottom_panel`, `bc7_weighting::linear_once`, `ui_smoke::preview_settings_open_at_1280`. The 1280 strip pins moved to 1200 (`at_1200x800_with_advanced_open_…`, `the_collapsed_settings_strip_opens_the_settings`); `the_settings_strip_is_compact` no longer opens the strip at 1280.
  - **Step 1.** Live: every ruling. RED: the Save, header and Review Export clicks saved and ran nothing; Ctrl+S left the project dirty; the tap lost the typed name (3 undo steps, not 4); the toast drew over the open panel; Linear left the inherited `alpha8`; at 1280 the settings started as the strip. CE21's creep was not re-measured.

## Note FK — Every edit control on the texture panel waits for the plan (main team, 2026-11-01)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FL, 2026-11-02)
- **FK1 Cause (owner report 2026-11-01: "all of these buttons, and acknowledge do nothing ... changing alpha 'remove' reverts it back").** Reproduced on a copy of the owner's 10k-texture project (`B:\testdata\textures-10k`, 2920 sets, 9923 `[files]` entries) with the full `SdcApp` frame loop: a click on an alpha answer writes `alpha.policy` to the document at once, then the whole project re-plans for about 18 s. Until that plan lands, the texture panel draws the last plan's job, so the Undecided box, Acknowledge and the Alpha combo (back at `Remove`) look unchanged, and every further click starts a new plan that cancels the one in flight, so the wait starts over. The model, the engine's layering and the write were correct; nothing overwrote the value. This is a mitigation: each edit on such a project still blocks the panel for the length of a whole re-plan.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FL, 2026-11-02)
- **FK2 Fix.** Every edit control on the texture panel (`settings_panel::draw`: the centre panel, the preview's compact copy and the set preview's settings window) waits for the plan. It is off while `AppModel::fix_off()` (CA12) gives a reason: a read-only `sdc.toml` first, else "Wait for the plan to finish" while a plan or the scan-database votes are in flight. That covers the Undecided box, the setting groups' rows, the RNM and Cubemap sections, "Treat as OpenGL (flip green)" / "Treat as DirectX", "Re-encode this file" / "Stop re-encoding", and the Helper role row with its Reset. The reason is the disabled controls' hover text, as on Problems' fix buttons, so nothing moves when a plan starts or lands. `Ctx::edit_off` (was `toml_read_only`) carries it.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FL, 2026-11-02)
- **FK1 Cause (owner report 2026-11-01: "all of these buttons, and acknowledge do nothing ... changing alpha 'remove' reverts it back").** Reproduced on a copy of the owner's 10k-texture project (`B:\testdata\textures-10k`, 2920 sets, 9923 `[files]` entries) with the full `SdcApp` frame loop: a click on an alpha answer writes `alpha.policy` to the document at once, then the whole project re-plans for about 18 s. Until that plan lands, the texture panel draws the last plan's job, so the Undecided box, Acknowledge and the Alpha combo (back at `Remove`) look unchanged, and every further click starts a new plan that cancels the one in flight, so the wait starts over. The model, the engine's layering and the write were correct; nothing overwrote the value. Note FL cuts that wait to the edit's partial plan.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FL, 2026-11-02)
- **FK2 Fix.** Every edit control on the texture panel (`settings_panel::draw`: the centre panel, the preview's compact copy and the set preview's settings window) waits for its partial plan. It is off while `AppModel::fix_off()` (CA12) gives a reason: a read-only `sdc.toml` first, else "Wait for the plan to finish" while the edit's partial plan, the full plan when no partial applies, or the scan-database votes are in flight (Note FL6). That covers the Undecided box, the setting groups' rows, the RNM and Cubemap sections, "Treat as OpenGL (flip green)" / "Treat as DirectX", "Re-encode this file" / "Stop re-encoding", and the Helper role row with its Reset. The reason is the disabled controls' hover text, as on Problems' fix buttons, so nothing moves when a plan starts or lands. `Ctx::edit_off` (was `toml_read_only`) carries it.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - `ui_smoke::the_texture_panel_waits_for_the_plan_its_answer_started`: while an alpha answer's plan is in flight, the alpha buttons (by role) and the Alpha combo are disabled and their hover gives the reason; once the plan lands, the box is gone and the Alpha combo shows `Cutout`, enabled.

## Note FJ — BC4 block search: inner-anchored 6-value mode, least-squares refits, trimmed and local search; coverage guard on BC3 alpha (owner, 2026-11-01)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FJ1 The search and where it lives.** rgbcx anchored both BC4 modes on the block's minimum and maximum, so on blocks holding 0 or 255 the 6-value mode's fixed 0/255 entries were wasted; that cost BC3 cutout alpha about 2 dB against NVTT `-highest`. The vendored `rgbcx::encode_bc4_hq` (`crates/encoders-sys/vendor/bc7enc_rdo/rgbcx.cpp`, patch "rgbcx BC4 search" in `README-VENDOR.md`) gains mode-flag bits `BC4_SDC_SEARCH = 4` and `BC4_SDC_GUARD128 = 8`. With `BC4_SDC_SEARCH`, after the unchanged mode loop the chosen block is the start and is replaced only on a strictly lower error by: a 6-value search anchored on the inner range (values other than 0 and 255), least-squares endpoint refits of the best block of each mode (f64, compiled `/fp:precise`), 6-value searches on trimmed inner ranges, and an exhaustive ±3 around the best pair. The search radius scales the anchor loops and the other-mode re-search; the trimmed ±2 and the local ±3 are fixed. The shim (`shim/sdc_shim.cpp`) sets `BC4_SDC_SEARCH` for BC4 and both BC5 halves (channels searched independently) and adds `BC4_SDC_GUARD128` for BC3 alpha only, at every radius. `BC4_SDC_SEARCH` requires `BC4_USE_ALL_MODES` (documented on the Rust constant, `debug_assert!` in `bc4_encode_block_flags`; the vendored header is not edited, so the tag hash holds). Without the new bits `encode_bc4_hq` is byte-identical to upstream round-to-nearest. The C++ equals the Rust reference `sdc_bench::bc4ref::search` byte for byte (`crates/bench/tests/bc4ref.rs`; on the bench corpus 4,620,288 block-channel-radius checks plus the BC5 entry point, 0 mismatches).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FJ8 `max` near-exhaustive search: not done.** Owner declined after the timing (2026-11-01, "no exhaustive"); numbers in `docs/research/bc4-family.md` ("max: near-exhaustive": about +0.03 dB over the search at radius 10 for about 2.4× its time). `bc45_search_radius = "max"` is the FJ1 search at radius 10; `sdc_bench::bc4ref::search_max` stays a Rust-only measurement. End to end at `max` (median s per texture old → new): BC3 cutouts 0.151 → 0.165 (1.09×), BC4 0.359 → 0.598 (1.67×), BC5 0.776 → 1.348 (1.74×); worst 0.406, 0.746 and 1.497 s.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FJ9 Not done: per-mesh alpha thresholds.** The guard protects the alpha-test value 128 only, the vanilla and Community Shaders default. A mesh with another alpha-test threshold is not protected, because the threshold lives in the NIF (`NiAlphaProperty`), not in the texture the encoder sees.

## Note DM — Per-type encode versions for if-changed (owner, 2026-10-26)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **DM1 The table.** `manifest::encode_version(Subtype)` is an exhaustive `match` keyed by `Subtype` only, so a new sub-type fails to compile until it has an arm. Its value is the engine version of that type's last output change. A record without `encode_version` (written by 1.0.0) counts as `LEGACY_ENCODE_VERSION`, 1.0.0. `if-changed` compares the record's `encode_version` with the current arm instead of the engine version.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **DM4 What the guard hashes, and its blind spots.** `cargo test -p engine --test encode_versions` (feature `test-hooks`) hashes the encoder input, not the encoded bytes: the DDS header, per mip level a u64 LE length plus the texels (Ldr bytes in order; Hdr as u16 LE), the SHA-256 of the resolved profile JSON, and the validation verdict (`passed`). It runs the fixture under both targets and pins 55 outputs: 35 under cs, covering all 29 sub-types (`UNGUARDED` is empty), and 20 under vanilla (keys prefixed `vanilla/`), covering the 16 sub-types vanilla allows. Vanilla refuses the 13 cs-only sub-types (CFG-UNAVAILABLE, `CS_ONLY` in the test), and the test asserts exactly those, plus the two `_rmaos` set members, are refused. A change to any of those must come with the type's arm moving to the new engine version, or the test fails. Blind spots, which a human must bump for: validation-verdict changes that do not show in `passed` on the fixtures; the engine's post-encode steps in `encode.rs` (`bc7_opaque_fix`, the grass corner guard, `pack_uncompressed`); the profile-to-encoder-parameter mapping in `encode.rs` (`bc7_level`, `radius`); paths without a fixture (NVTT outputs, derived jobs); and an output key renamed and changed in the same commit (check_pins pins the new key fresh). Stated risk: the hash assumes the engine's float path is deterministic for one source tree. `.cargo/config` sets no `target-cpu`, and the thread-count check (RAYON_NUM_THREADS 1, 8 and default) passed.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **DM4 What the guard hashes, and its blind spots.** `cargo test -p engine --test encode_versions` (feature `test-hooks`) hashes the encoder input, not the encoded bytes: the DDS header, per mip level a u64 LE length plus the texels (Ldr bytes in order; Hdr as u16 LE), the SHA-256 of the resolved profile JSON, and the validation verdict (`passed`). It runs the fixture under both targets and pins 55 outputs: 35 under cs, covering all 29 sub-types (`UNGUARDED` is empty), and 20 under vanilla (keys prefixed `vanilla/`), covering the 16 sub-types vanilla allows. Vanilla refuses the 13 cs-only sub-types (CFG-UNAVAILABLE, `CS_ONLY` in the test), and the test asserts exactly those, plus the two `_rmaos` set members, are refused. A change to any of those must come with the type's arm moving to the new engine version, or the test fails. Blind spots, which a human must bump for: validation-verdict changes that do not show in `passed` on the fixtures; the engine's post-encode steps in `encode.rs` (`bc7_opaque_fix`, the grass corner guard, `pack_uncompressed`); the profile-to-encoder-parameter mapping in `encode.rs` (`bc7_level`, `radius`); paths without a fixture (derived jobs); and an output key renamed and changed in the same commit (check_pins pins the new key fresh). Stated risk: the hash assumes the engine's float path is deterministic for one source tree. `.cargo/config` sets no `target-cpu`, and the thread-count check (RAYON_NUM_THREADS 1, 8 and default) passed.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **DM8 Semantic versioning (owner, 2026-10-27, https://semver.org/).** From 1.0.0 on, the workspace version follows MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH. MAJOR: a change that breaks existing use (a removed or renamed `sdc.toml`/preset key, CLI flag or exit code, a manifest a previous version cannot read, a removed feature). MINOR: a new backward-compatible feature (new sub-type, setting, command, export). PATCH: a backward-compatible bug fix, including one that changes some type's output (that type's `encode_version` arm moves to the patch version). Pre-releases use `-alpha.N` / `-rc.N`. Cubemap support is the next minor: 1.1.0. (amended by FC7)

## Note NV — M5: NVTT Production backend (plan rulings, 2026-10-13)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-sdc-m5-nvtt-design.md` (owner-approved, §9 at its proposed defaults). Supersedes
Note Y9: `sdc convert --nvtt` works.

- **NV1 Scope.** BC7 and BC6H UF16 only, Production only, one `-nomips` process per engine-built level. NVTT is never a
  default and never suggested.
- **NV2 Locate.** Explicit path (`--nvtt`, GUI NVTT path; missing → not found, no fallback), else `nvcompress.exe` on
  `PATH`, else `C:\Program Files\NVIDIA Corporation\NVIDIA Texture Tools\nvcompress.exe`. Under `cargo test` the target
  folder, which holds the fake `nvcompress`, is on `PATH`, so the real-NVTT tests pass the default path explicitly.
- **NV3 Version.** The probe runs `nvcompress` with no arguments (it exits 1; Spike S7b), parses the banner
  `NVIDIA Texture Tools <x.y.z>` and requires the usage to list every flag NV4 passes. Tested = 3.2.5. Untested →
  `ENC-NVTT-VERSION` (blocking, allowable). Not found or probe failed → `ENC-NVTT-MISSING` (blocking). The batch probes
  lazily (`NvttCell`): a batch with no profile on NVTT never starts nvcompress.
- **NV4 Flags.** `-silent -nomips -production`, then `-bc7` or `-bc6`; for BC7 `-noalpha` when `alpha.policy = remove`
  and `-alpha` for every policy that keeps alpha (coordinator, pending the owner's card: nvcompress weights alpha 0
  unless told the input has it); `-nocuda` always (S7, S7b: byte-identical, 0.13 s less per process). No colour flags;
  the engine writes the header.
- **NV5 Feed.** Each level as a DDS with a **legacy** header (S7b: NVTT reads uncompressed DX10 DDS as garbage): BC7 as
  B8G8R8A8 via `dds_bytes(.., Legacy)`, BC6H as RGBA16F with FourCC 113, written by `nvtt.rs` (the engine's writer has
  no legacy RGBA16F form). One temp folder per file, `%TEMP%\sdc-nvtt-<pid>-<n>`, removed on drop.
- **NV6 Post.** `force_opaque_rotated` and the complex-grass corner guard run on NVTT BC7 output (`bc7_post_fix`); the
  mode-6 byte patch does not.
- **NV7 Slot.** One nvcompress process at a time per process (static mutex). Shortcut: fixed at 1; make it a setting if
  cores idle.
- **NV8 Failure.** `ENC-NVTT-FAILED` (error, Compress): the `file_failed` error starts `ENC-NVTT-FAILED:` and carries the
  exit status and the last 5 output lines. No fallback to the built-in encoder. Cancel kills the child. No timeout
  (shortcut: add one if a hung nvcompress is ever seen). No `ENC-NVTT-PARTIAL`: S7b encodes every BC6H level.
- **NV9 Preview.** Previews of an NVTT texture encode with the format's built-in backend; the export uses NVTT. There is
  no `PreviewResult` flag: the GUI reads `profile.encoder.backend` (its struct literals are in `crates/gui`).
- **NV10 Records.** `EncoderVersions.nvtt` is set on NVTT records only; `if-changed` re-encodes an NVTT output when the
  version found now differs or NVTT is missing. Built-in records ignore it, so pre-M5 records stay current. Engine
  version stays 0.7.0.

Real-NVTT checks (`cargo test --release -p engine --test nvtt_real -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads 1`,
2026-10-13, NVTT 3.2.5):
- (a) `rgb_real/Bricks104`, `alpha.policy = remove`: engine mip 0 RGB PSNR 47.776810 dB = direct `nvtt_production`
  47.776810 dB (Δ 0.000000); decoded alpha 255 on every level.
- (b) `hdr_real/studio_small_08` as a float `_g` (BC6H): validation passed, peak ratio 0.9957.
- (c) Engine run of that BC7 file 18.07 s vs direct per-level `-nocuda` processes 17.66 s (ratio 1.023, limit 1.10).
- (d) `alpha_real/Fingerprints009`, `alpha.policy = blend`: mip 0 alpha PSNR 38.7786 dB on NVTT (`-alpha`), 38.9259 dB on
  bc7e, bench direct `nvtt_production` 38.7883 dB. Not measured: the same file without `-alpha`.

## Note AF — audit 2026-10-13b fixes (2026-10-14)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AF3 The NVTT probe has a timeout, never runs on the UI thread, and refuses two programs.** `probe_path` kills a program that has not exited after `PROBE_TIMEOUT` (5 s; nvcompress answers in about 30 ms) and reports it not found ("no answer within 5 s, not nvcompress"). It never runs `nvtt_export.exe` (NVIDIA's exporter app, a GUI) or the running program itself (same canonical path as `current_exe`, e.g. `sdc-gui.exe` picked as the NVTT path, which relaunched itself at every start). The GUI probes on a worker (start, focus regain, a path change); the result lands later, generation-tagged (a newer probe supersedes), and `busy()` holds while it runs. The CLI (`sdc encoders --nvtt`, the batch's `NvttCell`) gets the same timeout and refusals.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
  - AF3: on a timeout (or a failed wait) the probe kills the whole process tree (`taskkill /T /F`), so a launcher's grandchild does not survive. The program's output goes to files in a temporary folder instead of pipes, so no reader thread can be left waiting on a handle a grandchild holds. A `try_wait` error is reported as such ("waiting for it failed"), not as a timeout. Window focus re-probes only when the file NVTT resolves to (path, size or modified time) changed since the last probe; start-up and a path change always probe. A probe that panics reports `Missing("the probe panicked: …")` under its own generation, so a stale probe's panic cannot end the current probe's wait.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AF10 Fix round 2 (2026-10-14): only a user edit cancels an Export queued behind a scan-finished read (amends AF9/AF4).** The cancel runs in the edit path (`edit_docs`, `batch_edit` through `replan_after_edit`), not in `replan`. A re-plan that is no edit (an NVTT probe whose result changed, a changed source on focus) keeps the queued Export, and that plan carries it to its end. When an edit cancels it, the status line says "Export cancelled: the project changed while scan results were loading — click Export again." The note stays in front of the status line until the next Export click.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AF11 Fix round 3 (owner ruling via coordinator, 2026-10-14): undo and redo count as edits for the queued Export (amends AF10).** `apply_snapshot` re-plans through `replan_changed`, the same cancel-with-note rule as `edit_docs` and `batch_edit`, without the edit's type-follow bookkeeping: undo still resets it, per Z11. Re-plans that are no change to the project (an NVTT probe, a changed source on focus) keep the queued Export.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AF10 Fix round 2 (2026-10-14): only a user edit cancels an Export queued behind a scan-finished read (amends AF9/AF4).** The cancel runs in the edit path (`edit_docs`, `batch_edit` through `replan_after_edit`), not in `replan`. A re-plan that is no edit (a changed source on focus) keeps the queued Export, and that plan carries it to its end. When an edit cancels it, the status line says "Export cancelled: the project changed while scan results were loading — click Export again." The note stays in front of the status line until the next Export click.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AF11 Fix round 3 (owner ruling via coordinator, 2026-10-14): undo and redo count as edits for the queued Export (amends AF10).** `apply_snapshot` re-plans through `replan_changed`, the same cancel-with-note rule as `edit_docs` and `batch_edit`, without the edit's type-follow bookkeeping: undo still resets it, per Z11. Re-plans that are no change to the project (a changed source on focus) keep the queued Export.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AF2 `mergedimage.png` inflation is capped (zip bomb; agrees with AC6).** Loading a `.kra`/`.ora` inflates `mergedimage.png` to at most `archive::MERGED_CAP` = 16384 × 16384 × 4 bytes (a 16384 px RGBA8 image stored raw) + 64 MiB for PNG overhead. An entry whose central directory claims more is refused before anything is inflated; an entry that lies is cut off one byte past the cap and refused. Both are `IN-DECODE` ("mergedimage.png is larger than a 16384 px image can be"). A 16-bit RGBA PNG at the full 16384 px that compresses worse than 2:1 would be refused too; none is known.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
  - AF2: loading a `.kra`/`.ora` first reads the PNG IHDR (`image_size`), so an archive over MAX_DIM is `DIM-TOO-LARGE` before anything is inflated. The inflation cap then comes from that IHDR: `h·(1 + 8w)` (16-bit RGBA stored raw with a filter byte per row) + 1/64 for zlib and chunk framing + 4 MiB for ancillary chunks, never above `MERGED_CAP`. The buffer grows from the central directory's claim, doubling but never past the cap. A 2×2 IHDR with megabytes behind it is refused after at most about 4 MiB, whether the entry's claim is true or a lie. The refusal is `IN-DECODE` ("larger than its size in the PNG header allows").

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
  - AF7: a recipe that writes nothing holds no path in the duplicate check. That covers a failed or cancelled source, `packing.enabled = false`, and sources that differ in size. Its packing switch and source sizes are read once more for this, before planning.

## Note AH — Complex `_m` alpha and Enable parallax (2026-10-14)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
  - **BC7 exactness:** bc7e can encode a constant alpha-0 block as mode 6 with p-bit 1, which decodes alpha 1. So `encoders_sys::bc7_fixup::force_zero_alpha` runs after the encode, on both the bc7e and the NVTT paths. It applies to a `complex_material` with `alpha.parallax` not on, and only to blocks whose source alpha is 0 throughout.
    - It clears the alpha endpoints and, in modes 6 and 7, the p-bits they share with RGB.
    - RGB endpoints that had p-bit 1 move down, unoptimised, like the S5b mode-6 opaque patch: one LSB (1/255) in mode 6, one step of the 6-bit value (about 4/255) in mode 7. Modes 4 and 5 lose nothing.
    - BC3's alpha block stores 0 exactly.
    - Parallax on keeps a source that has alpha byte for byte. So the warned parallax-on-without-alpha state (AH2) may decode 1, which is still <= 4.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
  - **BC7 exactness:** bc7e can encode a constant alpha-0 block as mode 6 with p-bit 1, which decodes alpha 1. So `encoders_sys::bc7_fixup::force_zero_alpha` runs after the encode, after the bc7e encode. It applies to a `complex_material` with `alpha.parallax` not on, and only to blocks whose source alpha is 0 throughout.
    - It clears the alpha endpoints and, in modes 6 and 7, the p-bits they share with RGB.
    - RGB endpoints that had p-bit 1 move down, unoptimised, like the S5b mode-6 opaque patch: one LSB (1/255) in mode 6, one step of the 6-bit value (about 4/255) in mode 7. Modes 4 and 5 lose nothing.
    - BC3's alpha block stores 0 exactly.
    - Parallax on keeps a source that has alpha byte for byte. So the warned parallax-on-without-alpha state (AH2) may decode 1, which is still <= 4.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AH1 Complex `_m` without source alpha writes alpha 0 (owner, 2026-10-14; widens Note M1).**
  - **Before:** M1/P24 set the 0 default only for a packed `cpm` `_m` height. A loose `_m` typed `complex_material` whose source has no alpha channel resolved `alpha.policy = remove`, from the analysis `suggest_alpha` "source has no alpha channel". It was written with a synthesised 255, which CS reads as a plain env mask (smallest-mip alpha >= 251/255, Spike S8).
  - **Now:** `suggest_alpha` gives `data` for `complex_material` with no alpha, with the reason "source has no alpha channel: alpha 0 (complex material on, parallax off)". The pipeline fills alpha with 0 before Preprocess (`ops::complex_alpha_zero`), so every mip is 0. The fill is 0, not 128: mid values run parallax on a flat height and pick up compression noise.
  - **BC7 exactness:** bc7e can encode a constant alpha-0 block as mode 6 with p-bit 1, which decodes alpha 1. So `encoders_sys::bc7_fixup::force_zero_alpha` runs after the encode, after the bc7e encode. It applies to a `complex_material` with `alpha.parallax` not on, and only to blocks whose source alpha is 0 throughout.
    - It clears the alpha endpoints and, in modes 6 and 7, the p-bits they share with RGB.
    - RGB endpoints that had p-bit 1 move down, unoptimised, like the S5b mode-6 opaque patch: one LSB (1/255) in mode 6, one step of the 6-bit value (about 4/255) in mode 7. Modes 4 and 5 lose nothing.
    - BC3's alpha block stores 0 exactly.
    - Parallax on keeps a source that has alpha byte for byte. So the warned parallax-on-without-alpha state (AH2) may decode 1, which is still <= 4.
  - **`exact_opaque` on `complex_material`:** kept `true`, because it still matters for an `_m` with alpha whose flat regions are 255. It is irrelevant for a 0 fill, so `ALPHA-EXACT-OFF-M` is not raised for an alpha-0 `_m`, even with `exact_opaque` off.
  - **Warnings:** the `SUG-CONTRADICTION` "reads as a plain envmask" fires only when a solid 255 is actually written (parallax on). `CHAN-CONSTANT-ALPHA-DATA` is not raised for a 0 fill. `VAL-M-GRAYSCALE` (grey, non-black RGB) still applies.
  - **Output identity:** the engine version stays 0.7.0 (owner rule). The `profile_sha256` of every `complex_material` changes once, because of AH2's new field, so `--on-existing if-changed` re-encodes them.

## Note BC — Audit REPORT 2026-10-16 fixes, engine and CLI (owner, 2026-10-16)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BC9 Hung nvcompress (#18).** The per-level wait has a ceiling of 2 minutes plus 1 s per 4 000 texels (`nvtt::level_timeout`): Spike S7 measured BC7 Production at 0.036–0.07 Mpix/s on ~13 cores (the slow end is 1 s per ~36 000 texels), so the limit is about 9x the slow end, for smaller CPUs or a busy pool (~70 min at 4096², ~4.7 h at 8192²). It catches a hung process, not a slow one. Past it the process is killed and the level fails, so the file is `ENC-NVTT-FAILED` (its `file_failed` names it) and the NVTT slot is freed for the next file. Tested with the fake's `sleep` mode and the test-only `SDC_NVTT_LEVEL_TIMEOUT_MS` (read only with `test-hooks`).

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BC12 NVTT temp folders (audit EP-7).** `nvtt::TempDir::new` creates its `sdc-nvtt-<pid>-<n>` folder with `create_dir` and moves to the next `n` when it exists, so a folder a crashed run left under a recycled pid is never reused; the level's output file is removed before nvcompress starts, so a stale one cannot pass for this run's. Tested in its own test binary.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BC13 Review minors (2026-10-16).** `bake::decode_peak` returns 0 for a header over `MAX_DIM` (a forged size could overflow the product; the load refuses it anyway). `nvtt::TempDir::new` creates a missing system temp folder before `create_dir`. Open, not fixed here: non-finite values in `[defaults]`, sub-type tables and rule overrides (BC6); the complex-grass top half is not coverage-validated (BC3, accepted).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BC13 Review minors (2026-10-16).** `bake::decode_peak` returns 0 for a header over `MAX_DIM` (a forged size could overflow the product; the load refuses it anyway). Open, not fixed here: non-finite values in `[defaults]`, sub-type tables and rule overrides (BC6); the complex-grass top half is not coverage-validated (BC3, accepted).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BC5 Glob typos (#7).** `policy::compile_glob` (the matcher `glob_match_input` caches) checks every glob where it enters: `parse_set`'s `file:<glob>` (usage error), `check_preset`'s own rule globs (preset error, so `sdc presets validate` and the GUI preset editor see it), and `check_up_front` for `file:` overrides built without `parse_set` (usage) and every rule glob of the preset chain, the alias presets and the configs (config error). The lazy cache stays.

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **BC7 Preset export (#16).** `sdc presets export --out` writes with `write_atomic`, so a failed or killed write leaves the old file whole. No new test (the report proposed none; the export tests cover the normal write).

## Note FM — Tessalith 2.0 (main team, 2026-11-02)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FM15 The format alone picks the encoder; the rgbcx level is fixed; the mode-6 byte patch is gone (plan task 5, 2026-11-02).** `EncoderSpec` has no `backend` or `rgbcx_level`. `Backend`, `policy::backend_fits`, `default_backend`, `backend_follows_format`, the sources that tracked a pinned backend, and the `CFG-INVALID` checks for an encoder that cannot write the format and for a level above 18 are deleted. `encode_band` already chose by `p.format`: rgbcx for BC1, BC3, BC4 and BC5, bc7e for BC7, ISPC for BC6H. `bc1_encode_block` and `bc3_encode_block` take no level and pass `RGBCX_MAX_LEVEL` (18, the value every built-in preset set). No encoded byte changes: `identity.json` is untouched, while `profile_hashes.json` and `encode_inputs.json` change because the encoder spec lost two fields.
  - **Removed names.** `migrate::REMOVED` gains `encoder.backend` (any value, after FM14's `nvtt` row, so that value still names NVTT), `encoder.rgbcx_level` and `allow` = `IN-FLOAT-LDR`. `Code::parse_allow` reads the same table, so a config, a preset, `--set` and `--allow` all give the removal message. `IN-FLOAT-LDR` leaves the catalogue (114 codes); it was never emitted.
  - **Patches are required.** `build.rs` stops the build when `bc7e_ispc.h` lacks `m_exact_opaque_alpha` (patch 0001) or `m_endpoint_search` (patch 0002). `encode.rs` asserts `BC7E_EXACT_OPAQUE_NATIVE` at compile time. `encode_band` loses its `native` parameter, `bc7_opaque_fix` its `mode6_patch` arm, and `bc7_fixup::force_opaque_mode6` goes with its tests. `check_profile` no longer refuses `encoder.effort = "max"`. The rotated mode 4/5 fix stays. The two constants stay public (always true) and the C++ shim keeps its `#if`s; both are left for a later clean-up.
  - **Vendor header.** `vendor/bc7enc_rdo/rgbcx_table4_small.h` is deleted. `rgbcx.cpp` names it only inside the `RGBCX_USE_SMALLER_TABLES` branch, which `rgbcx.h` defaults to 0 and nothing sets, so it is never compiled; the vendored source is left as it is. `SHA256SUMS` and `README-VENDOR.md` follow.
  - **GUI.** The Advanced drawer loses the rgbcx level row, the pin summary loses its encoder word (it read "bc7e", "rgbcx" or "ispc (BC6H)"), and the preset creator no longer moves a backend with the format. ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FM9 Engine 2.0.0 has one encode version; a 1.x record is a stale baseline (plan task 3, review focus 1, 2026-11-02).** `ENGINE_VERSION` is 2.0.0 (the workspace version and its entries in `Cargo.lock`) and `manifest::encode_version` returns `"2.0.0"` for every sub-type from one `|` arm list, still exhaustive (a new `Subtype` fails to compile). `LEGACY_ENCODE_VERSION` is gone. `RELEASED_ENGINE_VERSION` stays `"1.1.0"` until the release task, so the guard in `encode_versions.rs` accepts every encode-input change meanwhile; `identity.json` (FM1) carries the byte proof. A manifest record without `encode_version` (schema 1 or 2, written by 1.0.0 to 1.2.0) is a known, stale baseline: `encode_current` is false for it, `IfChanged` re-encodes and replaces the output (its `output_stamp` still marks the file as ours, so no `OUT-UNKNOWN-EXISTING`), and the upgrade notice reads `First export with 2.0.0: N files` (`recode_causes`). `ManifestEntry::from_value` stays as the overwrite guard's reader for such records (it needs `schema`, `output` and `output_stamp`; a record without `schema` or `output` is skipped as before); `MANIFEST_SCHEMA` stays 2, a record of a newer schema stays raw. `migrate_manifest_record`, `MANIFEST_STEPS` and `Manifest::load`'s call to the migrate hook are gone. `golden.rs` `two_d_outputs_unchanged` (it held 2D arms at 1.0.0) is deleted, and so are the BC4 arm list in `events_manifest.rs` and the engine-side tests of the 1.0.0 record rule; `api.rs` `a_1x_manifest_record_is_known_and_stale` (schemas 1 and 2) and `also_export.rs` `a_1x_twin_without_encode_version_is_re_encoded_...` replace them. Output bytes did not change: `identity.json` is byte-identical, and `encode_inputs.json` and `profile_hashes.json` were not rewritten.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FM17 BC1 and the colour half of BC3 encode with rgbcx Dual; the decoder setting is gone (plan task 7, controller ruling P11, 2026-11-02).** rgbcx mode `cBC1Dual = 4` scores NVIDIA and AMD decoder error together (patch "rgbcx BC1 dual", cherry-picked from `research/bc1-dual` 9363fa72). It is the only BC1 target: BC1 outputs, the colour block of BC3, and the BC1 copies an Also export writes (a file copy of the main output) all use it. Measurements: [the Research page](../docs/wiki/Research.md).
  - **API.** `encoders_sys::init()` is infallible and idempotent (`Once`) and selects Dual; `InitError` is gone. `Bc1Model` is `{ Nvidia = 1, Amd = 2 }`, decode models only. `Session::new()` and `BatchRequest` take no model; `decode_level` and `decode_level_rgba` take none. `engine::manifest::BC1_ENCODE_ID` (`"dual"`) is written to `ManifestRecord.bc1_model` for every record and `is_up_to_date` compares it for BC1 and BC3 outputs, so a 1.x record (a decoder model's name) is stale once. `versions().rgbcx_patch` is `bc4-round-to-nearest+bc4-search+bc1-dual`; BC1, BC3, BC4 and BC5 compare it, so their records go stale once too (BC4 and BC5 bytes do not change).
  - **Decoder rule.** Validation, previews, readouts and PSNR decode BC1 with `Bc1Model::Nvidia` only, one decode so each readout has one value. Dual's NVIDIA PSNR is the lower of its two vendor values on the research corpus (39.51 against 39.74 dB). A BC1 DDS read as an input (`load.rs`, the `dds_patch` transparency scan) keeps the exact model-less `bcdec_rs` decode (controller ruling T7-a): a re-encode must not bake one vendor's decoder error into the pixels, so `ANALYSIS_VERSION` stays 1.
  - **Removed.** `--bc1-decoder` (clap refuses it, exit 2), `Bc1Choice`, the GUI's BC1 decoder combo and its "restart Tessalith to apply" line, and `Session::bc1_model()`. `REMOVED` gains `bc1_model`, so a config, preset or `--set` that names it is an error that says so (`bc1_model_in_a_config_is_named`). A 1.x `gui.toml` with `bc1_decoder` still loads (serde ignores the key; `a_1x_gui_toml_with_bc1_decoder_still_loads`). Files touched under the NIF team's shared GUI paths: `crates/gui/src/model/{mod,overlay,preview}.rs` and `worker.rs` (the `Session::new()` call and the decode parameter); `ui/mod.rs` is untouched.
  - **Pin.** `crates/encoders-sys/tests/bc1_dual.rs` pins the summed NVIDIA + AMD squared error of the 64x64 research image at 4 998 895.
  - Supersedes: F (the BC1 decoder model bullet), CN32/CN33/CN64 (in part: the per-process model sentences).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FM20 Opt-in BC1 1-bit alpha (plan task 9, controller rulings P8 and P10, 2026-11-02).** `encoder.bc1_alpha` (bool, default off, not serialised when off) stores BC1 with 1-bit alpha. No preset, rule, analysis or mesh sets it; the user does (GUI: **1-bit alpha** in the Alpha group of the texture panel and of the preset editor, shown only for BC1). `Profile::bc1_one_bit_alpha()` is format BC1 with the flag on.
  - **Removed name.** It replaces `encoder.bc1_transparent_black`, which `migrate::REMOVED` names with the hint "delete the line; use encoder.bc1_alpha for 1-bit alpha" (any value, so a 1.x file with `= false` must drop the line). `sdc-base.toml` loses it.
  - **Cutoff.** Every level is cut at `alpha_cut(&p.alpha)` (FM19; 128 at the default 127/`gt`) after the mips are built: alpha at or above the cutoff becomes 255, a texel below it becomes `[0, 0, 0, 0]`. The cut is in `quantize::to_encoder_input`, so encode, validation and the encode-input hash see the same image, and validation compares transparent texels as the black they decode to.
  - **Encoder.** `rgbcx::encode_bc1_alpha` (`bc1_encode_block_alpha(px, mask)`), mask bit i = texel i alpha < 128. Mask 0 gives exactly `encode_bc1`'s bytes; mask 0xFFFF gives endpoints 0, 0 with every selector 3; any other mask gives a 3-colour block fitted to the opaque texels only (principal axis, up to 8 least-squares refits, then a greedy ±1 endpoint search), opaque texels on selectors 0..2 scored with the Dual NVIDIA + AMD error (FM17), masked texels on selector 3. The `rgbcx_patch` tag gains `+bc1-alpha`.
  - **Mips (P8).** With the flag on, `cutout`, `cutout_blend` and `blend` all build coverage-preserving cutout mips (`coverage_enabled()`), then cut. `mips.coverage = "off"` turns that off for `blend` too. `VAL-COVERAGE` applies. The GUI treats `blend` with the flag as alpha-tested (`is_cutout`), so its threshold and coverage rows show.
  - **Policy.** `stores_alpha(p)` counts BC1 as storing alpha only with the flag on and policy `cutout`, `blend` or `cutout_blend`. Otherwise `FMT-BC1-ALPHA` blocks; `data` always blocks. With the flag on, `ALPHA-BC1-ONE-BIT` (warning; 115 codes) fires when more than 1 % (`BC1_SOFT_ALPHA_SHARE`) of the source texels have alpha 1..=254; only varying alpha is counted.
  - **Hashes (P10).** Dropping the always-serialised `bc1_transparent_black` changes every profile hash and encode input (`profile_hashes.json`, `encode_inputs.json` rewritten); every encode-version arm is already 2.0.0, so nothing re-encodes beyond 2.0's own pass. `identity.json` keeps every key and gains `cov/textures/c/cutout_bc1` (cutout, box mips, flag on).
  - Supersedes: F (in part: BC1 opaque-only), K's `bc1_transparent_black` bullet (archived), T2 (in part: "`bc1_transparent_black` stays false").

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FM38 Legacy uncompressed DDS: 24-bit RGB is read and written, A8L8 and L16 are read (plan task 27B; owner 2026-11-03).**
  - **Why.** A survey of the owner's 93,234 loose DDS files (2026-11-03) found 476 24-bit R8G8B8 files, 166 of them winning in game across 19 mods (UBE 2.0, COtR, Heartbreaker, Starsight Eyes and others); the vanilla tint masks are 24-bit too, so the game loads the format. It also found 59 winning L16 files (CS terrain heightmaps) and 1 winning A8L8 (a CS shader LUT), and no 565, 1555, 4444, X8B8G8R8 or A8 file. The owner's rule is "output yes if mods can use it", so 24-bit gets write support; A8L8 and L16 stay read only, and the layouts the survey did not find stay unsupported (the existing `Unsupported("uncompressed layout …")` error).
  - **Reader.** `DdsPixelFormat` gains `Rgb8` (24 bits, masks R `0xff0000`, G `0xff00`, B `0xff`: bytes B, G, R, rows unpadded), `La8` (masks R `0xff`, A `0xff00`, at a bit count of 16 or of 8, which some writers put on it; two bytes a texel either way) and `R16` (16 bits, mask `0xffff`). The decode is `[r, g, b, 255]`, `[l, l, l, a]` and `[h, h, h, 255]` from the high byte of an L16 (the decoder has no 16-bit grey path, so it is LDR only). A8L8 means L to RGB and A to alpha in Tessalith's decode; how the game's loader maps it was not verified, so the decode follows the file's meaning. As a DDS input (`load.rs`) they are 8-bit sources: RGB8 has 3 channels, L16 has 1, and A8L8 has 4 channels with alpha, not 2, because `analyse` takes `channels == 2` for a two-channel (Z-less) normal source and an A8L8 file is grey plus alpha (`legacy_uncompressed_layouts_load_with_the_source_info_of_what_they_hold`). `dxgi()` is 0 for all three: they have no DXGI format the reader maps back, and A8L8 must not read as R8G8 (49), which `policy` takes for a two-channel normal. An R, G, B ordered 24-bit file stays unsupported (`malformed_headers_are_rejected_without_panicking` now uses it where it used the B, G, R order).
  - **Writer.** `Format::Rgb8` (id `rgb8`, not block, no alpha, not HDR, 3 bytes a texel) is `pixel_format` `Rgb8`, packed B, G, R by `pack_uncompressed`. It has a legacy header only: `DDPF_RGB`, 24 bits, those masks, no alpha mask, no DX10 block, the pitch one unpadded row (`3 * w`), mips allowed. `resolve_header` returns legacy for it whatever `header` says, and `header_bytes` refuses a DX10 header for it (`DdsError::NoDx10Form`). `as_format` reads a written 24-bit file back as `(Rgb8, false)`, so `validate` judges it like any other format (the PSNR channels are R, G, B).
  - **Policy.** `encoding_warnings` (the one source of `allowed_encodings` and the GUI format dropdown) treats `rgb8` as `rgba8` for every rule, with one more: the `_SRGB` tag is `CFG-INVALID` ("RGB8 has no _SRGB form"), because a legacy header cannot carry it. So RGB8 is offered exactly where `(rgba8, UNORM)` is, cubemaps included (controller ruling T27B-c: the owner's load order has a winning 24-bit file under `textures\cubemaps`; the cubemap list is BC1, BC3, BC7, RGBA8 and RGB8, which amends that list in Note DS). A 24-bit cube writes with the legacy header and the cube caps (`an_rgb8_cube_round_trips_with_a_legacy_header`). `check_profile` adds `FMT-RGB8-ALPHA` (Blocking, `Code::ALL` has 94; "RGB8 stores no alpha, but alpha.policy is <id>; …") for any alpha policy but `remove`, as `FMT-BC1-ALPHA` does for BC1, and `CFG-INVALID` for an explicit `header = "dx10"`. `--allow FMT-RGB8-ALPHA` writes the colour and drops the alpha. `stores_alpha` does not count `rgb8`, so a varying alpha under an inferred `remove` still blocks with `LOSS-ALPHA-DATA`.
  - **GUI.** The dropdown label is `RGB8 · linear (24-bit, uncompressed)` (the label convention is `<name> · <what>`, and a test pins it; controller ruling T27B-d); the tag row is locked with "RGB8 has no _SRGB form". The Problems sentence for `FMT-RGB8-ALPHA` is in `code_text.rs`. A 24-bit DDS previews from the CPU decode, as every uncompressed layout does.
  - **render3d (NIF team crate, `textures.rs` only; controller ruling).** A 24-bit body texture failed to load in the 3D view, so the three layouts upload: L16 as `R16_UNORM` (56) as stored, 24-bit RGB and A8L8 expanded per level and face by `expand_legacy` (pure; B, G, R becomes R, G, B, 255; L, A becomes L, L, L, A, the engine's decode) and uploaded as `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` (28). `load_dds_as` still refuses a format that maps to 0. The line is in `nifparseref\plan\HANDOFF.md`.
  - **Encode versions.** Every arm stays "2.0.0" (no existing output changes). `identity.json` gains two keys and no existing key changes: `cov/textures/c/opaque_rgb8.dds` (a 64 x 64 opaque diffuse) and `cov/textures/c/wide_rgb8.dds` (32 x 16, a 2:1 mip chain). `encode_inputs.json` and `profile_hashes.json` are untouched. Schemas: the new code and the `rgb8` format id.
  - **Tests.** `dds_read::{a_24_bit_bgr_file_reads_with_its_rows_unpadded, an_a8l8_file_reads_as_grey_with_alpha, an_a8l8_file_with_an_8_bit_count_reads_the_same, an_l16_file_reads_as_grey, other_legacy_layouts_stay_unsupported, rgb8_output_round_trips_through_the_reader}`, `dds_read::{an_rgb8_cube_round_trips_with_a_legacy_header, an_rgb8_dx10_header_is_refused}`, `load::legacy_uncompressed_layouts_load_with_the_source_info_of_what_they_hold`, `out_size` (RGB8 is 3 bytes a texel; the estimate reads `pixel_format`), render3d `legacy_layouts_expand_to_rgba8_as_the_engine_decodes_them`, `policy_checks::{rgb8_is_refused_when_alpha_is_kept, rgb8_is_refused_with_an_srgb_tag}`, `allowed_encodings::rgb8_is_allowed_where_rgba8_unorm_is`, `fields::the_dropdown_offers_rgb8_where_rgba8_unorm_is_offered`, the `encode` pack unit test, the `config` classification test and the `encode_versions` coverage fixtures. The first run failed to compile (no `Rgb8`, `La8`, `R16` or `FmtRgb8Alpha`). Main-team files only except render3d's `textures.rs`.

  - **Left alone.** `cs-compact` and `vanilla-compact` do not halve a Gray map (their `resize = "half"` rows list the existing grayscale types); that is a preset edit for the release task.

## Note J — result verbs print to stdout (controller ruling, 2026-10-09, Task 42 review)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
`validate` infers the alpha policy from the file: mip-0 alpha ≥ 254 everywhere → `remove`; otherwise the sub-type's alpha-keeping default, or `data`. `--alpha-policy` overrides it. `alpha.exact_opaque` is a promise of sdc's own encoder, so `validate` sets it off whatever the sub-type's preset says (a third-party BC7 whose alpha decodes to 254 passes with the `ALPHA-254` info); `--exact-opaque` asks for the 255 guarantee (Codex-CLI 3). Conversion-time validation is unchanged. For sub-type inference, mip-0 alpha with a minimum of 251 or more is not varying (Note L).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
The verbs whose report *is* the result print their human report on **stdout**: `inspect`, `validate`, `presets` and `encoders`.

## Note L — near-opaque source alpha (owner, 2026-10-09)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **Rule:** source alpha whose minimum is **≥ 251** (no texel below 251) is treated as opaque. It resolves like constant-255 alpha, so `remove` applies and no LOSS-ALPHA-DATA is raised. An info code `ALPHA-NEAR-OPAQUE` reports the minimum.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **Rule:** source alpha whose minimum is **≥ 251** (no texel below 251) is treated as opaque. It resolves like constant-255 alpha, so `remove` applies and no LOSS-ALPHA-DATA is raised. No code reports it (Note FM21).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **Below the cutoff:** one texel below 251 keeps the normal varying-alpha rules.

## Note AC — Authoring-format inputs: PSD/PSB, Krita, OpenRaster, WebP (plan rulings, 2026-10-14)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AC2 What reads.** RGB and Grayscale; 8, 16 and 32 bits per channel (32-bit is float and linear light, S1); raw or RLE composite data. RGB channel 3 (Grayscale channel 1) is alpha: the document's transparency, or its first alpha channel when it has a Background layer, as the Photoshop DDS plugins read it. Later channels are ignored. An ICC profile (resource 1039) is `IN-ICC-IGNORED` (S8).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AC7 WebP** through the `image` crate's `webp` feature (`image-webp` 0.2, MIT or Apache-2.0), like the other `image` inputs (lossy and lossless; an ICC profile is `IN-ICC-IGNORED`).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
Owner ask (2026-10-14): support `.psd`, and look at other common authoring formats sdc does not read yet (Krita, OpenRaster and WebP followed, AC6–AC7; GIMP `.xcf` and Paint.NET `.pdn` store no flattened image, so sdc would have to composite their layers: not planned).

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AC6 Krita `.kra` and OpenRaster `.ora`** (owner, 2026-10-14: "yes add .kra and .ora and webp"): both are ZIP archives holding the flattened document as `mergedimage.png`; sdc reads that PNG through the PNG decoder and never the layers. Chosen by content (a ZIP signature). Own minimal ZIP reader (`engine::archive`: stored or deflated via `flate2`, already a workspace dependency); ZIP64, encrypted entries and other methods are refused, and an archive without `mergedimage.png` is `IN-DECODE` ("re-save it in Krita"). The size is read from the PNG's IHDR (only its first bytes are inflated), so `DIM-TOO-LARGE` comes before decoding (in `image_size`; the load inflates the whole entry, capped by AF2). Not yet run on a real Krita file.

## Note AL — ORM packing input (2026-10-15)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AL2 Loose maps win, with an info note.** A loose map for a slot the `_orm` also holds wins that slot (glossiness counts as a roughness source, roughness as a glossiness source). Each such slot raises `PACK-ORM-OVERRIDDEN` (info) on the packed job, naming both files; it is not a conflict. An `_orm` that every slot of the recipe takes from loose maps is `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` ("a loose map is used for each channel it could supply"). A second `_orm` in one set is unused, as for any role (Note M13).

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AL4 RNM bent AO reads an `_orm` (fix round 1).** The bent layer's AO source (Note W9) is a loose `_ao` (R), else an `_orm` (R), else an `_rmaos`/`_rfaos` file (B). A set with an `_orm` and no `_ao` no longer raises `RNM-AO-MISSING`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AL5 `_rfaos` notes the dropped ORM metallic (fix round 1).** A cs set with an `_orm` and a fuzz source (or `packing.recipe = rfaos`) builds `_rfaos`, which has no metallic channel. The `_orm` is still used for R and B, so it is not `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT`; the packed job gets a `PACK-ORM-OVERRIDDEN` info note naming the unused B instead. That code now covers any `_orm` channel left unused.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AL3 Representation.** `ChannelSource::Source` gains an optional `channel` (0..=2), serialised only when set, so a job without an `_orm` keeps its JSON and manifest bytes; provenance reads `iron_orm.png:G`. The packed source's role is `orm`. `RawMap::ALL` stays the eight single-channel roles that have a `packing.fill` (`--fill orm=…` is refused); `RawMap::INPUTS` adds `orm` where a file name is mapped to a role. The engine version stays 0.7.0; outputs of sets without an `_orm` are unchanged.
