## Note AZ — cpm target removed (owner, 2026-10-15)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **AZ1 Hard error.** `Target` is `cs` or `vanilla`; `TargetArg` lost `Cpm`. Serde and clap reject `cpm` in `--target` (convert, validate, patch-header), `sdc.toml`, the user config and a preset's `target`. `--preset cpm-quality` and `base = "cpm-quality"` are unknown presets. No alias was added. The CLI exits 2 for each (tested). `sdc-project.toml` has no target field, so it has nothing to reject. A manifest is read leniently (its `target` is not deserialised as `Target`): an old cpm record no longer matches and re-encodes once under `if-changed`. A user preset in `%APPDATA%\sdc\presets\` with `target = "cpm"` is skipped with `CFG-PRESET-SKIPPED` (Note T6, unchanged; the run goes on), and the warning's reason names the unknown variant `cpm`; selecting that preset is then an unknown-preset error (review M3, tested).

## Note FI — GUI review D fixes (main team, 2026-10-31)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FI3.** A DDS input is re-encoded from a button (texture panel **Re-encode this file**, bulk panel **Re-encode *n* DDS files**), which writes the S6 `reencode` selector to the file's `[files]` entry as one undo step (`AppModel::set_reencode`; off clears the field, false is never stored; refused while `sdc.toml` is read-only); the hand-edit hint goes (extends FC10's hand-remedy rule; S6's default off stands). A re-encoded DDS (it has a job, or failed at plan) shows **Stop re-encoding** at the top of its panel. The button is off for a DDS another input already writes, since re-encoding it would make both blocking duplicates (`IN-DUPLICATE`, `api.rs` duplicate check): `reencode_blocker` compares the item's `job_key` (the case-folded output path) with every other item that has a job, which leaves out GEN-REPLACED and IN-DUPLICATE inputs (no job) and counts an image twin or another re-encoded DDS. The bulk row counts only skipped DDS files without a blocker and names the left-out ones on hover. Into the folder it comes from, the button stays on (its hover says it cannot be written there) and S6 fails the file at plan: a red Failed row, never a silent skip. The panel says a skipped DDS is not written to the export, and its header issues read as one line in words with the raw `check_header` fields on hover (naming **Patch header…** when it is offered). Re-encoding the diffuse of an all-DDS PBR set (FI2) gives the set `pbr`, its Diffuse slot and its PGPatcher JSON entry; re-encoding only another member leaves `pbr` off, since `SetView.pbr` follows the diffuse's job. The FC1 recording the re-encode's settled plan makes (the new job's sub-type) joins the re-encode's step by its history merge key, as FC8's weighting follow does, and gets no Problems row of its own: one Undo takes back the selector and the types it caused, one Redo puts both back. FC1 is unchanged for every other trigger. No bytes change for files that are not opted in.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FI11.** (D13, D14, D15, A11, A15, A17; rule 4.5; supersedes AN3's and AN4's menu labels; the keys and behaviour stand) The slot menu reads `Pin this slot (always use this file)` / `Unpin this slot` and `Keep the original texture (PGPatcher won't replace it)` / `Let PGPatcher replace the texture`, FB13's words; the terms ride on hover (`Pinned slots refuse other files (sdc.toml pin)`, `PGPatcher lock: lock_<slot> in the JSON`, drawn by `bulk_menu` from the entry's action). The Decide… menu leads with a weak `Alpha means:`. FC1's row hover lists `{file name}: {sub-type as the picker shows it}` (no folder, no raw id) and ends with `High confidence: the file name and the pixels agree on the type.` when a sub-type was set. An empty slot's `From this set (nothing)` is disabled with `This set has no file for this slot`; `already from this set` stays for a slot that has a file. Accept all suggestions' disabled hover says `Only type suggestions are accepted here; an alpha meaning needs your answer` while an undecided texture still asks its alpha meaning; Accept all is not widened (U3 stays High types only). The Match menu (`Match {n} · {name}`, the key `match{n}` on hover) and Slot commands (`Slot {n} ({name})`) take the set editor's empty-card name for the slot (`model::sets::slot_name(pbr, n)`: `SlotKind::short_label`, the card's name without its `(slot n)`; slot 5, which has no card, is `Cubemap`). A vanilla set's Slot commands take the Meshes tab's slot names (`edit_slot_label`, read only: slot 6 is `Env mask`; the table is nif_edit.rs's, keep in step). Only TruePBR sets have a Match menu, so it uses the PBR names. The High-confidence line is added only to a recording that wrote a sub-type (an alpha-only row has no type to agree on). Guards `the_slot_menu_says_pin_and_keep_in_words`, `the_decide_menu_starts_with_alpha_means`, `the_automatic_row_explains_high_confidence`, `an_empty_slot_says_the_set_has_no_file`, `accept_all_explains_an_alpha_proposal`, `the_match_menu_uses_the_sets_slot_names`, and `no_stray_words` with `(sdc)` and `(PGPatcher lock)`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **FI14.** (A16, A20, A21, A22; rule 1.7, 4.5) The JSON editor is `PGPatcher JSON` everywhere: its tab and window title (was `TruePBR JSON`), the set editor's `PGPatcher JSON editor…` button and the cs-only note; the More menu item and the palette entry were already so named. Its add button reads `+ Add a delete section` (PGPatcher's "delete" sections keep their name; the verb is the action). The Files tab says `Not exported yet.` while `export.rows` is empty and shows no counts of nothing (was five zeros and `No files yet.`); with rows the counts line is unchanged. The status line reads `1 texture, 1 set` (singular), through `AppModel::counts_line`. The Undecided row joins its parts with `. ` (was `; `): `Undecided: what its alpha means. This alpha channel carries data…`. The Acknowledge buttons take their text from `code_text::ack_label`: `LossAlphaData` reads `Drop the alpha anyway` (`… for this texture` in the panels), every other code keeps `Acknowledge`; the hover is the code's sentence. Checked in the engine: `LOSS-ALPHA-DATA` is raised only for a varying alpha that the profile discards (policy `remove` not set by the user, or a format that stores no alpha, `policy.rs` alpha-loss branch), and an acknowledgement only downgrades the code to a warning (`policy.rs` allow loop) without changing the profile, so the export writes the texture without its alpha. `Acknowledge all` is unchanged. Pinned in `vocabulary.rs` (`the_json_editor_has_one_name`, `the_files_tab_before_export_says_not_exported_yet`, `one_set_is_singular`, `the_alpha_acknowledge_names_its_action`).

## Note DZ — Analysis disk cache (owner, 2026-10-28)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **DZ4 What the pin covers (review fixes).** `tests/analysis_version.rs` hashes, with the old hand-built images: PNGs generated in the test (8-bit RGBA, 16-bit RGB, gray) through `load_image_meta`; DDS files written with the engine's own writer (`dds_bytes`, from deterministic block bytes so no encoder, whose output can differ by CPU, is involved) for 2D BC1, BC5 and BC7 and one single-cube BC1, loaded through the same entry (`load_dds` including the 6:1 cube strip); two packs through `pack::assemble` (gray members, and a `channel: Some(..)` extraction from an RGBA member with an inverted channel plus a non-gray 16-bit member read by luma); and `schemars::schema_for!(Analysis)`, so a field added with a serde default still moves the pin. Not covered: PSD and Krita/ORA decoding (the engine has no PSD or ORA writer, and no dev-dependency makes one), packs with members of other sizes (`assemble` refuses them), and EXR/HDR/TGA sources. Bump `ANALYSIS_VERSION` by hand when those change. Bump it too when decoding, `analyse_with`, thumbnails or pack assembly change their results. It stays 1: nothing has shipped.

## Note FA — Trace fixes 2026-10-28 (owner, 2026-10-28)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **FA14 Engine modules `coverage`, `dilate`, `migrate`, `pipeline`, `sysmem` and `zip` are `pub(crate)` (quality audit B, NIF handoff item 7).** No front-end, test or example names them, so the public API shrinks to what the front-ends use, and none can call `pipeline::process_file` and skip the `Engine::run` checks. With `pipeline` crate-private, `dead_code` found `FileOutcome::record` (the manifest record `process_file` hands back, read by nothing; `Engine::run` reads `output` and `report` only), so the field is deleted; the record is still appended to the manifest first. No output byte changes.

## Note GF — Review wiring for NIF edits (NIF team, 2026-10-30)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **GF8 A cancel during the Also zip and report phase writes no NIF edits (fix round 1, review finding 1).** `also_zipped` dropped the re-armed cancel token unchecked, so `after_export` still made the queued NIF export due and the glue wrote it, an answered Overwrite included. It now checks the token and abandons the queued export when it was cancelled, as `export_ended` does for a cancel during the conversion. Test: `model::also::tests::a_cancel_while_zipping_makes_no_nif_export_due` (RED before). app.rs writes NIF edits only on `take_nif_export_due`, so the flag being false is what keeps the file on disk unchanged; the test is at model level because the zip phase cannot be timed from an app harness.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **GF9 A cancelled run drops its Review answers (fix round 1, review finding 2; GD29).** Review's Export takes Review away without `close_review`, so a cancel left the Overwrite answer in the NIF model, and the next Review could export over the foreign file with no new answer (the hash check only catches changed bytes). New `AppModel::abandon_nif_export`: when a cancel abandons a queued NIF export (`export_ended` during the conversion, `also_zipped` during the zip phase, GF8), it also sets `nif_clear_answers_request`, so app.rs calls `clear_nif_answers`. Tests: `nif_edit::a_cancelled_export_drops_the_overwrite_answer` (app level: answer Overwrite, Export, cancel, reopen Review; the row asks again, and an Export without a new answer leaves the foreign file as it was; RED before) and the clear request in GF8's test.

## Note GI — XPMF profiles and landscape projected maps (NIF team, 2026-11-01)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-02)
- **GI27 Also export with Vanilla ticked puts a PBR Landscape set's Projected noise, Detail normal and a `pbr = false` XPMF profile in the Vanilla folder (final review I1b, owner, 2026-11-01).** The owner ruled that the Vanilla folder keeps what the PBR folder gets, as it keeps the `_p` (O6). Engine: `DeriveSet::kept_maps` (the set's `_noise` and `_detail` jobs, found by the PBR phase's slot rule) joins the `_p` in `plan_derived`'s keep list when `DeriveRequest::keeps_landscape` (Vanilla mode, PBR Landscape, no DDS member, an albedo or normal) holds, and is re-pathed by `vanilla_rel` / `vanilla_game_path` (TA12); the CM folder never has them. The Vanilla plan types them as the PBR phase did (the `api.rs` pick for such a set is PBR Landscape until the derive step replaces it, so a medium `_noise` and a detail layer resolve), and `finish_also` counts the GUI's extra files in the Vanilla folder as it does the PBR folder's, so the Vanilla zip holds the copy. GUI: with the tick on, `xpmf_json::prepare` adds `PendingXpmf::vanilla` for each PBR Landscape set that is not left out of Also, that the Vanilla derive keeps (`vanilla_keeps`: no DDS member, a diffuse or a normal) and whose profile is written: the same file name under the Vanilla folder, built by `profile_in(.., vanilla = true)`: `pbr` false whatever the block says (so `specularMult` stays), default textures through `vanilla_rel` (the Vanilla diffuse, normal, noise and detail), typed values unchanged, the same schema check. `finish` writes it with the main file's guard (`may_replace` with the recorded `xpmf_sha256` of that path, `write_atomic`, a foreign file kept and noted, a texture of this project not on disk in the Vanilla folder stops the file, orphans reported and never deleted; a main profile that is refused keeps the copy's record out of the orphans, as it keeps its own). `XPMF-Profiles.md` says so. The earlier note that the Vanilla folder gets neither is gone.

## Note AQ — 2D preview gamma (2026-10-15)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AQ1 Cause.** The expanded 2D preview drew an sRGB colour map's Source pane darker and more contrasty than its Encoded pane. `SourcePreview.levels` was `prep.chain` (`engine/src/session.rs`, `preview_source` and `preview`): the chain in the engine's working domain. For sRGB colour that is linear light (`ops::preprocess_with` step 5 decodes sRGB before the mips); for normals, MSN and flow it is −1..1 vectors. The encoded side is the stored bytes (`quantize::to_encoder_input` re-encodes; the textures are UNORM and the CPU decode gives stored 0..1). The shader and every GUI reader (AG55 readout, heatmap, Split, lens, tiling) treat both sides as stored values, so the source was shown as linear values read as sRGB: grey 128 drew as about 55. The shader, the texture formats, the sRGB / UNORM toggle and the tone map were right.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note FM, 2026-11-03)
- **AQ3 Readers fixed with it.** Source pane, heatmap against the source, pixel readout source line (its "stored values, sRGB-encoded" label is now true for both sides), the normal readout's source vector (AG56 unpacks 0..1, which the source now is), Split, lens and tiling (same slots). Pin and compare use decoded images only and were right. The UNORM toggle still re-encodes the raw stored values with `linear_to_srgb` on both panes alike (AG55).

## Note BE — Audit REPORT 2026-10-16: scan fixes (modscan)

ARCHIVED (superseded by Note GJ6, 2026-11-02)
- **BE1 Scan after PGPatcher (#5).** A reused copy that is a derive-source candidate but has no stored CRC goes back to the parallel pass, which hashes loose and BSA entries alike. `crc_of` is a map lookup; the serial `fs::read` fallback, `new_crcs` and `Store::set_derived`'s `crcs` parameter are gone. A BSA source now verifies (`match`) on a warm store instead of staying `unverified`. A candidate whose read fails has no CRC, stays unverified and is re-read next scan (accepted).

The empty table stays in `SCHEMA` with a comment: no schema bump, no forced rescan; drop it at the next bump. Stores scanned earlier keep stale rows nobody reads. Tests count `tex_winner`; the `scan_dump` fixture was regenerated without the `tex_copy` section.

## Note BW — A tainted scan unloads plugin rows (NIF team, 2026-10-17)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ5, 2026-11-02)
* **BW1 The tainted early return calls `store.unload_plugins()`.** After `set_textures(&HashMap::new())` the tainted branch of `scan::run` now clears `rec_winner`/`rec_inert` and `plugin.lo_index`, as the Folder path does. Before, the old winning records stayed while `tex_winner`/`nif_winner` were empty, so Plugin file references > Missing listed every path a winning record names (trace-debug wave 2 finding 7; display only). The cancelled and failed paths were checked: they leave via `discard_pending`/`Err` before any winner change and already have a test. Test: `a_tainted_scan_leaves_missing_and_unused_empty`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ5, 2026-11-02)

The `Cancelled` arm in `scan::run` now calls `unload_plugins()`; parsed records and the cache are kept, so the next clean scan restores the lists. Test: `a_scan_cancelled_at_the_plugin_pass_leaves_missing_and_unused_empty`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ5, 2026-11-02)

The cancelled and failed paths were checked: they leave via `discard_pending`/`Err` before any winner change and already have a test. Test: `a_tainted_scan_leaves_plugin_rows_empty`.

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ5, 2026-11-02)

## Note GH — GUI review D fixes and quick NIF export (NIF team, 2026-10-31)

ARCHIVED (wording before the edit; superseded in part by Note GJ5, 2026-11-02)
- **GH7 Slot numbers count from 1 and carry the NIF index on hover (N7, A14, A15; owner O12 (a), 2026-10-31).** Every visible and AccessKit slot number in the Meshes view is 1-based, as in the set editor, Slot commands and the JSON editor: the shape cards (`slot 1 (diffuse):`), the "Users of" list, the card's Details, the shader form (`Diffuse path (slot 1)`, `Browse for Diffuse (slot 1)`, `Fix slot 1: …`), the pending list (`Texture slot 1`) and the RMAOS warning (`nif::edit::validate::EMPTY_RMAOS` now reads `Slot 6 (RMAOS) is empty: …`; GD6's anchor `Target::Slot { slot: 5 }` is the 0-based index and is unchanged). Hovering a slot's name gives `NIF texture index {i}`. Names: the form shows the set editor's card name where a True PBR set has one that differs (`edit_slot_shown`: slot 3 `Emissive / Skin`, slot 7 `Coat normal / Fuzz`, with the form's own name added to the hover); every other slot keeps `edit_slot_label`'s NIF-specific name (`Rim / back-light`, `Env cube`, `Inner layer / tint`, …) because the set editor names only the True PBR slots, and the shape cards keep `slot_name`'s lower-case names. The plugin-reference lines are not covered: the set editor's "Used by plugins" list (`ui/plugin_refs.rs`, a main-team file outside N7) prints `TxstUse.slot`, the 0-based NIF `BSShaderTextureSet` index (`modscan/src/plugin_store.rs`), so a diffuse reads `slot 0` there (pinned in `tests/set_plugin_refs.rs`) while the set editor numbers from 1. Handed to the main team: show `slot + 1` there. The apply error in `nif::edit::structural` ("slot 3 is past the set's 2 slots") is 1-based too, and a pending texture row ("Texture slot 2") gives `NIF texture index 1` on hover.
